**Blockers**
- [high] [I] The capstone this section gates checks things S13 never teaches. CP-N1-C's P0 critical failures include «Sin mecanismo de corrección», and its final interface is `familiarity.review(case) -> ReviewPacket` with `risk_decisions`, `correction_mechanism` and `human_review_required=True`. A grep of S13 for `corrección`, `correction`, `ReviewPacket`, `familiarity.review` and `geofenc` finds none of them. The capstone You Do also asks for «geofencing» and «análisis de falsos positivos», which the section You Do does not. If CP-N1-C is graded against its BRIEF/RUBRIC, Lucía fails a non-compensable P0 she was never told about. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-C/BRIEF.md` («Fallos críticos (P0)»), `FINAL_INTERFACE.md`, `YOUDO.md` vs `youDo` of S13 — evidence: «- Sin mecanismo de corrección» — fix: either `src/lib/course/sections/s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: add a T4 step plus a You Do stub for a correction path (a reviewer overrides a pair; the override is logged with reason) and a `review(case)` function returning the packet; or `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-C/{BRIEF.md,FINAL_INTERFACE.md,YOUDO.md,RUBRIC.json}`: rewrite them to the S13 contract (ER doc+block, rel 0.5/0.3/0.2, 0.40/0.80 matrix, CF-1). (The section and its gate must define the same product.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam bank is a template with no S13 content. All 24 items read «Sobre «<concept-slug>», ¿cuál es la práctica correcta…?», and the key is always index 1, «Aplicar normalize-blocking-er con evidencia verificable, límites explícitos y datos sintéticos o autorizados». Options are served unshuffled (`route.ts`: `options: JSON.parse(q.options)`). The concept slugs are English kebab-case with no gloss. — where: `S13-evidence-dashboard.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «La clave de normalize-blocking-er es aplicarlo con trazabilidad y sin sobreclaim de evidencia.» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'evidence-dashboard'`): write real items from the section's worked cases, e.g. `decide_ops_status(0.4,'low')`, `er_score` for doc-only match, precision for tp=5/fp=1, `{x,y}=={a,b}` for B→A, uncertainty for |0.9−0.4|. Shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (The Level-1 closing exam cannot discriminate between someone who did the section and someone who did not.)

The docstring states the expected values: «Pair de demo esperado tras correcciones: er=1.0, rel=1.0». Only the `DECISION_MATRIX` asserts and `pseudonymize` can show progress. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`main`, `er_score`, `relationship_signal_score`, `blocking_key`) — evidence: «def er_score(a: dict, b: dict) -> float: … return 1.0» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: add an `ER_MATRIX` with asserts. It needs a doc-only pair (→0.5), a three-token name «Luis Huamán Soto» where paterno ≠ materno, a `D-1`/`d1` doc pair, a different-doc pair (→0.0), and a `REL_MATRIX` pair with phone only (→0.5). (As written, a learner who fixes nothing sees the "expected" output. That is a tautological check.)

A pair at 0.75 is `needs_review` by `decide_ops_status` but is not in `clerical_queue`, so nobody reviews it. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[1].code` (`int(score >= 0.7)`), `weDo.steps[4]`, `theory[7].paragraphs[6..8]` — evidence: «Scores en banda intermedia (p. ej. [0.4, 0.7]) **nunca** auto-mergean … Aceptar solo si score ≥ 0.8» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: make the clerical queue `review_low <= score < accept_min` (0.40/0.80) everywhere and change the iDo prediction threshold to 0.8. - [medium] [O] The blocking explanation contradicts the ER rule. The figure caption says «Solo se comparan pares dentro del mismo bloque. The reconciliation (multi-key blocking by document) appears only in prose. No demo, We Do or You Do implements a second blocking key, so she cannot see how a cross-block pair ever reaches `er_score`. — where: `theory[2].figure.caption`, `theory[2].paragraphs[1–2]` — evidence: «0.5 si solo el documento coincide (bloques distintos — sospecha de migración o error de región)» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: add `candidate_pairs(records)` to iDo.steps[0] or weDo T1-A-E3, using the union of the name|region block and the doc block, and show that the doc-only pair arrives through the second key. - [medium] [O] The You Do asks for far more than the starter scaffolds, and part of the starter claims to be finished when it is not. The starter (`familiarity_dashboard.py`) contains none of: published fixtures, a `pr_metrics`, a txs function, a case-card/explain function, a tests file, or a module named `demo_n1_dashboard`, so the required command fails with «No module named demo_n1_dashboard». Its docstring says «privacy_sheet y level1_regression_notes ya están listos», but `level1_regression_notes()` returns descriptions with no pass/fail or evidence column, so it cannot meet rubric[4]. — where: `youDo.requirements[3,5]`, `youDo.rubric[0,4]`, `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «4) Corrige pseudonymize; privacy_sheet y level1_regression_notes ya están listos» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: ship the You Do as a small package (`demo_n1_dashboard.py` or `__main__`, `fixtures.json`, `tests/test_rules.py`) and give `LEVEL1_REGRESSION_MATRIX` `status`/`evidence` fields to fill in. A grep of S01–S12 finds `matplotlib` only as an install example in S01, and no plotting, HTML or map rendering anywhere. S13's «scaffold» is a list of printed dicts. Yet the portfolio note asks for «captura del dashboard con 3 casos» and the requirements ask for «Dashboard scaffold 3 casos + mapa/tooltips». She will not know whether a terminal screenshot counts, or whether she is expected to learn a plotting library on her own. — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[0]`, `youDo.objectives[5]`, `youDo.portfolioNote` — evidence: «tres fichas + puntos de mapa con coords sintéticas (Lima/Arequipa) y tooltips de geoseñal» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: state explicitly that the N1 "dashboard" is a text/Markdown case sheet (e.g. `render_case_sheet(cases) -> str` written to `dashboard.md`) and that the "map" is a lat/lon table. Alternatively, add a 20-line stdlib HTML export. (The section title promises a dashboard; the deliverable is undefined.)

The exam slugs are English. — where: `title`, `youDo.title`, `theory[1].paragraphs[4]` — evidence: «el paquete CF-1 —privacidad, demostración y runbook— entregado» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: translate the title («Tablero de evidencia de familiaridad», as `catalog.ts` already does), spell out CF-1 at first use, and drop «wire». (Lucía reads English slowly; this section closes the level she paid for.)
- [medium] [O] Precision and recall are new here (grep S01–S12: zero hits) and get two formulas and one sentence each. Neither the theory, the We Do nor the self-check explains why both are needed or what a «FN» looks like in the case data. The self-check item [7] only tests the formulas. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «precision = TP/(TP+FP) y recall = TP/(TP+FN)» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: add a 2×2 table built from the 6-pair worked case, with one plain-Spanish sentence per cell. (This is a data-analyst interview staple; she should leave able to explain it, not only compute it.)

No solution prints it. — where: `weDo.steps[0..23].starterCode.code` — evidence: «print('ok', True)» — fix: `s13-evidence-dashboard.ts`: remove the line. (A false success signal.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — the 9-row `DECISION_MATRIX`, `pseudonymize`, `norm_doc`, `blocking_key`, `er_score` and the canonical rel score are direct reuses of the We Do and are doable. The dashboard/map, published fixtures with precision/recall, txs signals, tests, the `demo_n1_dashboard` module, pass/fail regression across S01–S13 and the CP-N1-C «mecanismo de corrección» are either untaught or unscaffolded, and four DEFECT fixes produce no visible change.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes, mostly [O] — the 9 items cover score separation, FP ≠ fraud, grey zone, CF-1 contents, regression, the exact 0.4 boundary, blocking purpose, precision/recall arithmetic and the parts[1] rule. Uncertainty bands, txs/common counterparties and pseudonymization are untested. Most distractors are absurd («Publicar PII real en el mapa»), so it checks ethics recall more than skill.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase0_walked`; `reliable_automation_development` [S13+S24]; `integrated_automation_engineering_practice` [S13,S20,S21,S24]; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations` [S01–S13]): no [O]. The bank is a template whose answer is always option 2. `reliable_automation_development` claims «selector_resilience» (Playwright/Selenium selectors), but S13 states «la automatización de navegador … llegan después», and neither the content nor the exam touches selectors. `integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations` lists `reliable_automation_development` as a prerequisite, which needs S24, so the «Foundations» capstone credential cannot be earned at the end of Foundations. Its `skill_nodes` include `pandas_numpy`, `classical_ml` and `python_visualization`, which Level 1 does not teach. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: drop S13 from the RPA badges' `required_sections`, and remove the S24-dependent prerequisite and the untaught skill nodes from the capstone credential.
- Value: mixed, leaning shortchanged — the conceptual core is excellent and rare in beginner courses: two separate scores, abstention, FP ≠ fraud, exact thresholds, pseudonymization. But Level 1 closes on a templated exam, an off-topic playground, a You Do whose demo cannot tell a fixed solution from a broken one, and a capstone BRIEF that grades something else. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: as briefed, a single script that prints dicts, with the asserts embedded in `main()`, no tests folder, no fixture file, no visual and an English title. A recruiter would see a "Dashboard" repo with no dashboard. With a `tests/` folder, `fixtures.json`, a Markdown case-sheet output and a README that includes the ER≠REL rationale, it could be one of her best portfolio pieces.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none in lesson code (stdlib only: `re`, `math.isfinite`, `random`; no pandas/numpy reprs; `float("nan")` prints `nan` in both). `python -m demo_n1_dashboard --synthetic` and `python -m venv` (iDo.steps[7], theory[9]) are shell commands that cannot run in the browser and are shown as strings only. The S13 playground uses `time.sleep` and `random`, which work but are off-topic (see Issues).

### Level 2 (S14–S26)

#### S14 — NumPy y cómputo vectorizado

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica seguridad: hashing y cifrado» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `mismatch`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 11 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S14.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S14-security.json` (learningOutcomes, theory[0–9], iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.steps[0–23], youDo, selfCheck.questions[0–10], resources) and all 24 items of `S14-security.exam.json`. Also read the S14 browser playground (`src/components/course/SectionView.tsx` `demos['security']`), `src/components/course/CodePlayground.tsx` `REQUIRED_PACKAGES`, `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (option order), and the badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` whose `required_sections` include S14. Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S13 `.json` for `import numpy`, `np.`, `ndarray`, `float64`, `allclose`, `assert`, `-O`, `perf_counter`, `CP-N2-A`, `IEEE`, `SLA`, `O(n`, `inf`, `nan`, `pip install`, `numpy`. NumPy is only named in S01 (as a third-party package) and S06/S12 («no NumPy de S14»). `CP-N2-A`, `allclose`, `IEEE` and `SLA` have zero hits.

**Blockers**
- None found. Every snippet is NumPy plus stdlib and runs offline with no paid service.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam can be passed without knowing NumPy. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`. The key is also the longest option in 24/24 items. `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` returns `options: JSON.parse(q.options)` without shuffling them, so the answer is always the second option. Most distractors are absurd for the topic. Two concepts were never taught: [20] asks about `np.vectorize` (0 hits in the S14 JSON) and [19] asks about warming up («calentar») before timing. — where: `S14-security.exam.json[0..23]`; `[5].options`, `[15].options`, `[20]` — evidence: «Leer CSV de SUNAT», «Encriptar PII», «Crear venv», «np.vectorize sobre una función Python pura:» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'security'`, line ~4159): write distractors that are real misconceptions taken from the section's own `edgeCases` (`len(ids)/len(ids)`, `x == np.nan`, `and` between arrays, `axis` inverted, `float32` nbytes). Spread `correctIndex` across 0–3. Replace `np.vectorize` and «calentar» with taught items. Also shuffle options per attempt in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (The 70% pass mark and the 85% badge floors currently measure test-taking, not NumPy.)
- [high] [O] The in-browser playground for this section is about something else, and what it teaches is unsafe. `demos['security']` is titled «Practica seguridad: hashing y cifrado». It stores passwords with a single fast SHA-256 hash and compares them with `==`. Its comment says SHA-256 is «irreversible - para passwords». The section is NumPy, and this is the only runnable editor on the page. — where: `src/components/course/SectionView.tsx` `demos['security']` — evidence: «# 1. Hash SHA-256 (irreversible - para passwords)» — fix: `src/components/course/SectionView.tsx`: replace it with a NumPy playground (mask + `mean(axis=0)` + `np.unique` uniqueness + `isfinite`) that has a deterministic `expectedOutput`. (The key is stale from an older section map. Even in a security section, plain SHA-256 for passwords is the anti-pattern OWASP warns against.)
- [medium] [O] The lesson contradicts itself on `assert`. theory[2] forbids `assert` for validating incoming arrays. The first I Do then validates with `assert`, and its `why` calls that «el mismo hábito fail-closed» as We Do E3, which actually uses `raise ValueError`. theory[2].callout and theory[5].paragraphs[2] also say «asertarlos» / «aserta la compatibilidad». — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[0].code`, `iDo.steps[0].why`, `iDo.steps[3].code`, `theory[2].callout.content` — evidence: «**No uses `assert` para esto**: Python borra los asserts cuando se ejecuta con `-O`» vs «El `assert` es el mismo hábito fail-closed que practicarás en We Do T1-A E3 (`validate`)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s14-security.ts`: in `make_quality_arrays` and `s14_ido_4`, use `if …: raise ValueError(...)`. Change the callout to «documenta dtype y shape y valida con `ValueError`». (She copies what the demo does, not what the paragraph says.)
- [medium] [O] The callout names the wrong exception. Two places say a mismatched boolean mask raises `IndexError`; the callout says `ValueError`. NumPy raises `IndexError` («boolean index did not match»). A learner who writes `except ValueError` from the callout will see the exception escape. — where: `theory[3].callout.content` vs `theory[3].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[1].preamble` — evidence: «La máscara debe tener la misma shape que el eje indexado; de lo contrario ValueError.» — fix: `s14-security.ts`: change the callout to `IndexError`. (The two statements contradict each other on adjacent lines.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do benchmark breaks the section's own "honest benchmark" rule. `bench_weighted_mean` is tested on a 3×2 matrix, and the only timing assert is `ratio > 0.0`, which any two timings pass. Theory says not to measure tiny N. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`_run_tests`, `main`), `theory[8].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «No midas N=10» vs «X = np.array([[0.9, 0.1], [0.4, 0.6], [0.85, 0.15]], dtype=np.float64)» … «assert bench["ratio_loop_over_vec"] > 0.0» — fix: `s14-security.ts`: keep the 3×2 fixture for the `allclose` test, and add a `main()` run with `rng.random((20_000, 5))` that prints `N`, dtype and `ratio_gt_1`, as iDo.steps[6] does. (The portfolio claim «benchmark honesto» needs a benchmark that means something.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do rubric cannot be scored. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O]. Each of the five stubs mirrors a demo or We Do she has done (`mean(axis=0)` + ndim check ← weDo.steps[2]/[6]; `np.unique` ← weDo.steps[7]; `isfinite` + range mask ← theory[7]/iDo.steps[5]; `[:, None] - [None, :]` ← iDo.steps[3]; loop vs `@` with `perf_counter` ← iDo.steps[6]). `_run_tests()` gives her a clear "tests OK" signal.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. The 11 items cover dtype, masks, axis, views, `nanmean`, broadcasting rules, `allclose`, honest benchmark, nbytes, `writeable`, and uniqueness, with plausible distractors. Only `isfinite`/inf, `keepdims` and fancy indexing are untested, and no item asks her to read code.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase1_walked`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; no competency badge requires S14): no [O]. The answer is always option 2, and absurd distractors leave nothing to decide. The capstone credential's `newbie_friendly_description` says the Phase 1 capstones cover «security, packaging, SQL, RAG, deep learning, and automation», which matches the stale slugs. It never mentions NumPy, pandas or data quality, even though S14–S16 teach exactly those (its `skill_nodes` do list `pandas_numpy`).
- Value: fulfilled (with caveats). This is the strongest of S14–S16 for a data-analyst track. The teaching is clear and honest: mask, axis-as-business-meaning, view vs copy, NaN ≠ 0, and equivalence before speed, with deterministic fixtures and good E1→E3 scaffolding. She loses value to the off-topic playground and a meaningless exam. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: medium. It is a single `quality_board_numpy.py` with inline asserts and a README checklist (shapes, rtol/atol, memory budget, machine-dependent ratio), but no `tests/` file, no data file and no README template. Benchmarking a 3×2 fixture would look naive to a reviewer.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): [I] Low. Every printed output goes through `.tolist()`, `float()`, `int()` or `round()`, so numpy 1.26 vs 2.2 repr differences (`np.float64(...)`) do not show in any graded output. The You Do `print("bench", bench_weighted_mean(X, w))` would show `np.float64(...)` locally under numpy 2 if she stores NumPy scalars, but it has no pinned output. The real browser problem is that the page's playground is the off-topic hashing demo.

- None found. Everything runs locally with pandas plus stdlib. The only optional dependency, `openpyxl`, is free, and the section offers a CSV + JSON-schema fallback.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam can be passed without knowing pandas. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and the key is the longest option in 23/24. Options are not shuffled (`route.ts` returns `options: JSON.parse(q.options)`). The distractors are absurd: «Un socket HTTP», «Forzar GPU», «El theme de Streamlit», «Firmar el parquet». Several stems use terms the section never taught: «sentinels», «upcast», «downcast», «RangeIndex», «StringDtype», «cuarentena vs fail-fast». — where: `S15-stdlib-deep.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «Un DataFrame de pandas se describe mejor como: … Un socket HTTP» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'stdlib-deep'`, line ~4451): build distractors from the section's real misconceptions (`iloc` vs `loc`, `decimal=','` omitted, `index=True` → `Unnamed: 0`, `errors='coerce'` without counting, hashing `str(df)`). Spread keys across 0–3 and shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (Pandas ingestion is the core job skill for her target role, and this exam cannot tell her whether she has it.)
- [high] [O] The in-browser playground teaches `functools.lru_cache`, `itertools` and `partial`, with no pandas at all. Its expected output must not depend on the `str`/`object` dtype display (see browser risks). (The slug «stdlib-deep» is stale; the demo follows the slug, not the content.)
- [high] [O] The You Do is graded against what the section taught. theory[3] and weDo.steps[3] teach her to declare the provider marker `SIN_DATO` in `na_values`. The You Do fixture uses `SIN_DATO`, and `_run_tests` requires `report["score"] >= 1` and `report["monto"] >= 1`. If she follows the lesson and declares it, `SIN_DATO` becomes a parser null before `to_numeric`. The coercion delta is then 0 (verified under pandas 3.0.5: `delta 0`) and the tests fail. requirements[2] quietly asks her not to declare it, which contradicts theory[7]'s rule to «declara `na_values` solo para marcadores del proveedor». — where: `youDo.requirements[2]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`_run_tests`) vs `theory[3].paragraphs[2]`, `weDo.steps[3]`, `theory[7].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «CSV con `SIN_DATO` en monto → declara `na_values=['SIN_DATO']`» vs «El fixture usa `SIN_DATO` (no es nulo por defecto del parser) para que score y monto reporten al menos 1 fallo de conversión» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s15-stdlib-deep.ts`: in the fixture, keep `SIN_DATO` as a declared provider null and add a truly unparseable token (e.g. `0,9x` or `abc`) for the coercion. Have the report give both counts: `{"score": {"provider_null": 1, "coerced": 1}}`. (As written, honest practice fails the test, and passing it requires mixing up the two kinds of missing value the section says to keep apart.)
- [medium] [O] The section is pinned to pandas 3, but it drills a warning that pandas 3 no longer has. Under pandas 3.0.5, `pd.errors.SettingWithCopyWarning` does not exist; chained assignment emits `ChainedAssignmentError` and never writes to the parent (verified). Yet learningOutcomes[3], selfCheck[1], selfCheck[4] and theory[5].callout all name «SettingWithCopyWarning». In selfCheck[4], the "wrong" option[1] («El subset siempre es una copia, así que la asignación se pierde») is the closest description of what pandas 3 actually does, while the key says the fix «Evita SettingWithCopyWarning». — where: `selfCheck.questions[4]`, `selfCheck.questions[1]`, `learningOutcomes[3]`, `theory[5].callout.content` — evidence: «Evita SettingWithCopyWarning y deja la asignación en el DataFrame original» — fix: `s15-stdlib-deep.ts`: say «en pandas 2.x avisaba `SettingWithCopyWarning`; en pandas 3 la cadena escribe en una copia temporal y pandas emite `ChainedAssignmentError`». Make selfCheck[4]'s key «Escribe en el original en un solo paso; la cadena escribiría en una copia temporal». (A learner who searches the warning name in her pandas 3 install finds nothing.)
- [medium] [O] One We Do defect is invisible. In weDo.steps[10], the starter mutates the `loc` slice without `.copy()`, but under pandas 3 it prints `[True, True]`, exactly the expected output, with no warning (verified). Lucía gets "success" from the buggy starter, so walkthrough question 4 fails. — where: `weDo.steps[10].starterCode` — evidence: «sub = df.loc[df["score"] > 0.5]\nsub["ok"] = True\nprint(sub.get("ok", pd.Series([])).tolist())» — fix: `s15-stdlib-deep.ts`: make the observable defect a parent mutation. For example, use the chained `df[df["score"] > 0.5]["ok"] = True` and print `df.columns.tolist()` (no `ok`), or make the target «el padre no debe tener `ok`» and print both. (An exercise whose bug produces the right answer teaches nothing.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do asks for things the section says are not needed here, and contradicts itself on the hash. objectives[4] requires justifying a partition/layout in the manifest, but theory[10] says columnar engines are not needed or installed, requirements[9] makes partitioning optional, and `_run_tests` does not check it. The hash is «sha1 del CSV» in objectives[3] and in the starter docstring, but «SHA-256 preferible» in requirements[4], and theory/I Do only teach SHA-256. — where: `youDo.objectives[3..4]`, `youDo.requirements[4]`, `youDo.requirements[9]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`export_with_manifest` docstring) — evidence: «Justificar en el manifest la decisión de layout: qué columnas pide la consulta habitual y por qué esa partición ayuda o no a saltarse bloques» / «manifest con rows/columns/sha1/source» — fix: `s15-stdlib-deep.ts`: make objectives[4] conditional, matching requirements[9] («si particionas…»). Say SHA-256 everywhere, accepting `sha1` only as a legacy key in the test. (Lucía cannot tell what is required, and her 40% You Do weight depends on it.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do rubric cannot be scored: six weighted criteria, no level descriptors. «Privacidad / sin PII real / sin secretos / score ≠ culpa» is worth 20% and is satisfied by the given fixtures. — where: `youDo.rubric[0..5]` — evidence: «criterion: Privacidad / sin PII real / sin secretos / score ≠ culpa / weight: 20%» — fix: `s15-stdlib-deep.ts`: add 0–3 descriptors tied to `_run_tests` asserts and README contents, and turn privacy into a pass/fail gate. (Self-assessment against an 80% floor needs levels.)
- [low] [O] The coercion example contradicts its own text. theory[7] says default tokens like `N/A` can give a delta of 0 and that she should practise with «basura no reconocida (`x`, `SIN_DATO`)». The case described is «`monto` con `xx`», but the code uses `"N/A"` and reports 1 (correct only because the DataFrame is built in memory, not read from CSV, which is not said). — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[1..2]` vs `theory[7].code` — evidence: «Caso sintético: `monto` con `xx` (texto inválido) → `coercion_report={'monto': 1}`» vs «"monto": ["10.5", "N/A"]» — fix: `s15-stdlib-deep.ts`: use `"xx"` in the code, as the paragraph says. - You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. `read_csv(StringIO)`, `astype('string')`, `to_numeric(errors='coerce')` with the isna delta, a `KeyError` for a missing column, `to_csv(index=False)` and SHA-256 are all drilled (iDo.steps[5], [7]; weDo.steps[15–16], [18], [23]). But applying the taught `na_values=['SIN_DATO']` makes the tests fail, and the mandatory partition objective has no stub and no test.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. The 12 items cover loc/iloc, chained assignment, `coerce`, the manifest, `parse_dates`, a missing column, `index=False`, `category`, the Index, and the columnar layout. They skip the Peru-relevant parser contract (`sep=';'`, `decimal=','`, `na_values`), Series alignment and the isna delta. Two items are keyed to a pandas-2-only warning.

Two competency badges list S15 under the stale slug «stdlib-deep». They claim packaging with `pyproject.toml`, CI/CD and FastAPI, with critical competencies `type_safety_production_hardening` and `reproducibility_determinism`. A pandas-ingestion exam with key 1 on every item evidences none of those. The closest overlap, the schema contract and the manifest hash for reproducibility, is untestable when the answer is always B.
- Value: mixed. The core is valuable and right for her goal: declared dtypes, Peruvian-style `;`/`,` CSVs, a coercion report, `index=False`, and manifest hashing. The tone (synthetic data, «score ≠ culpa») is responsible. She is shortchanged by the version gap (pandas-3 content that never tells her to install pandas 3), the SIN_DATO trap in the graded project, an exam that measures nothing, and a functools playground. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good if done as briefed. `ingest_cp_n2a.py` has `_run_tests()`, JSON coercion report and manifest, and a README that explains the coercions. It would read as a credible junior ingestion step; it needs a `requirements.txt` pinning pandas and a sample output.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2, pandas 2.2.0): [I] High for printed outputs. Under pandas 2.2 these print `object` instead of `str`: theory[2], theory[6], theory[8], theory[9], iDo.steps[6], iDo.steps[7], weDo.steps[20] (expected `{'cliente_id': 'str', …}`), and the weDo.steps[12]/[17] starter text. These print `datetime64[ns]` instead of `[us]`: theory[3], theory[6], iDo.steps[1], iDo.steps[5]. `memory_bytes` (254/315) differs in theory[9] and iDo.steps[7]. weDo.steps[10] emits a `SettingWithCopyWarning` in the browser but not locally. `openpyxl` is not in `REQUIRED_PACKAGES`, so theory[8], iDo.steps[6] and weDo.steps[19] would raise `ModuleNotFoundError` if pasted into the playground. The content blocks are `local-python`, so this bites only in the browser editor, which here is the off-topic functools demo.

To check the money-parsing contract, I ran `norm_money` from theory[6] under `.venv-content` and `Intl.NumberFormat('es-PE')` in Node.

**Blockers**
- None found. Everything is pandas plus stdlib, local, with synthetic data.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam can be passed without the section. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and the key is the longest option in 24/24. Options are not shuffled (`route.ts`: `options: JSON.parse(q.options)`). Distractors include leftovers from the old wxPython section map. Stems use untaught jargon: «fan-out», «expect/assert», «monto_igv coherente con base imponible». — where: `S16-wxpython-gui.exam.json[0..23]`, e.g. `[10].options`, `[18].options` — evidence: «Que el GUI crasheó», «El logo debe ser azul», «El color del NaN» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'wxpython-gui'`, line ~4743): rewrite with near-miss distractors from the section's own defects (`notna` vs `isna`, `duplicated()` default vs `keep=False`, `fillna` before `was_null`, `3,00` → 300, `pass = n_q > 0`). Spread keys and shuffle in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (As built, the exam asks nothing about data quality.)
- [high] [O] The in-browser playground simulates wxPython button callbacks («sin wxPython en Pyodide»), with nothing about nulls, duplicates or gates. — where: `src/components/course/SectionView.tsx` `demos['wxpython-gui']` — evidence: «title: 'Practica eventos y callbacks (simulado)'» — fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with a small `run_quality_gate` playground (null policy → `duplicated(keep=False)` → domain mask → `json.dumps(metrics, sort_keys=True)`) that has a deterministic `expectedOutput`. (Stale slug; the only runnable editor teaches an unrelated topic.)
- [high] [O] The «locale PEN» money contract misreads Peru's own format. Rule (2) treats «coma y punto» as dot = thousands, comma = decimal. Peru's actual convention (CLDR `es-PE`; `Intl.NumberFormat('es-PE',{currency:'PEN'})` → «S/ 1,250.50») is the reverse. Running `norm_money` from theory[6] on «S/ 1,250.50» returns `1.2505` and on «S/ 1,250» returns `1.25` (verified), a 1000× error on the receipts Lucía sees every day in Lima. The section calls this the documented PEN locale and teaches it as the guard against KPI distortion. — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[6].code` (`norm_money`), `iDo.steps[4].preamble`, `weDo.steps[13]`, `selfCheck.questions[5]`, `learningOutcomes[4]` — evidence: «si hay **coma y punto**, el punto es miles y la coma es decimal (`1.250,5` → `1250.5`)» / «el locale PEN está documentado (coma sola = decimal latino)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: call it what it is («contrato del proveedor X: formato europeo `1.250,5`»). Add the real `es-PE` case (`S/ 1,250.50` → 1250.5), and make `norm_money` take the convention as an explicit parameter (`decimal=','|'.'`) that the memo must declare. Rule (3), «solo coma ⇒ decimal», is ambiguous for `1,250`: route it to quarantine instead of guessing. (The section's own principle is «no adivines», but this rule guesses, and it guesses wrong for Peru.)
- [medium] [O] One We Do defect is invisible. In weDo.steps[16], the starter checks only the upper IQR fence, but with this fixture it already prints `[100.0]`, the expected output (verified). The feedback is wrong for the starter: «Si la lista está vacía, solo miraste un lado del fence». — where: `weDo.steps[16].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[16].feedback` — evidence: «el fixture solo ejercita upper, pero la máscara bilateral es el hábito del gate» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: add a low outlier to the fixture (e.g. `[-80.0, 1.0, 2.0, 3.0, 100.0]`) so the one-sided starter prints `[100.0]` and the solution prints `[-80.0, 100.0]`. (Walkthrough question 4: she cannot see that she fixed anything.)
- [medium] [O] A hint states the wrong default. pandas' `keep` defaults to `'first'`; `True` is not a valid value. — where: `weDo.steps[6].hints[1]` — evidence: «keep por defecto (True) omite la primera aparición.» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: «keep por defecto (`'first'`) no marca la primera aparición». (She will try `keep=True` and get a `ValueError`.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do context and requirements promise a broad suite (null policy, dups/conflicts, normalisation with a raw column, IQR, schema drift, cross-field, audit), but the fixture exercises only three things. It has no raw strings to normalise, no date pair for a cross-field check, and no missing column for drift. The `schema` argument is only a null-policy dict with no dtypes or domain bounds. The rubric, like the others, has weights and no levels, with 20% for privacy, which the given fixture already satisfies. — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.starterCode` (`__main__` fixture), `youDo.rubric[0..5]` — evidence: «schema = {"cliente_id": "required", "monto": "required", "region": "optional"}» vs «- normalización con columna raw (valor original) lateral … - contratos de schema y cross-field» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: extend the fixture with `monto_raw` strings, `inicio/fin` dates and a second batch missing `monto`. Pass a schema of the form `{col: {"policy":…, "dtype":…, "bounds":…}}`, assert the `reason` codes, and add rubric descriptors. (Lucía cannot tell how much of the context list is graded.)
- [low] [O] The regions are called Peruvian but include Madrid. requirements[0] says «regiones PE», the context and fixtures say «Lima, Madrid y Cusco», and theory[6] maps «MAD» → «Madrid». — where: `youDo.requirements[0]`, `youDo.context`, `theory[6].code` — evidence: «Las regiones son Lima, Madrid y Cusco; los montos son PEN ficticios.» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: use a Peruvian region (Arequipa, Madre de Dios) or drop «PE». (A Lima reader will wonder if it is a typo for Madre de Dios.)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] `groupby` is used before it is taught. theory[4] uses `groupby(...).transform('nunique')` and iDo.steps[2] uses `groupby(...).filter(lambda g: ...)`, and weDo.steps[7] asks her to write it («Agrupa por `cliente_id` y mide nunique»). In S01–S15, `groupby` appears only in S15 theory[2], which says it «llega[n] cuando trabajes uniones y groupby». S17 is titled «Joins, reshape, groupby y cierre analítico». Nowhere does S16 explain what grouping is, what `transform` or `filter` return, or why the result is indexed by `cliente_id`. — where: `theory[4].code`, `theory[4].paragraphs[1]`, `iDo.steps[2].code`, `weDo.steps[7].instruction`, `weDo.steps[7].hints[0]` — evidence: «para conflictos, `groupby(clave)[attr].transform('nunique')>1` **por cada atributo que deba coincidir**» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: add a 3-line «groupby en una frase» block before T2-A (split by key → compute per group → `nunique` gives one number per key), with a printed intermediate `df.groupby('cliente_id')['region'].nunique()`. Alternatively detect conflicts with a S15-level tool (`drop_duplicates(['cliente_id','region'])` then `duplicated('cliente_id', keep=False)`). (She can copy the hint, but she cannot reason about it or adapt it in the You Do.)
- [low] [O] Some tools appear with no explanation. `pd.to_datetime(x, format="%d/%m/%Y")` format codes and `.dt.strftime` show up without a word on what `%d`/`%m`/`%Y` mean (grep S01–S15 for `strptime`/`strftime`/`%Y-%m-%d`: no hits). «IQR» and «z-score» are used in theory[7] and glossed only later in `youDo.context`. — where: `theory[6].code` (`parse_fecha`), `theory[7].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «IQR y z-score solo **identifican candidatos**» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: gloss inline at first use («IQR, rango intercuartílico: Q3 − Q1»; «`%d/%m/%Y` = día/mes/año de 4 cifras»). (Low: her university statistics likely covers IQR, but not the strftime codes.)
- [low] [O] The density of English terms is high for a reader who reads English slowly. A single paragraph can stack «quality gates», «raw», «outliers», «audit trail», «fail-closed», «schema drift», «cross-field», «runbook», «stakeholder». Most are glossed once, then used bare. — where: `jobRelevance`, `theory[8].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «según la política documentada en el runbook del job» — fix: `s16-wxpython-gui.ts`: add a short glossary block like S17's «Diccionario rápido de la sección», and prefer Spanish after the first gloss («cuarentena», «registro de auditoría»). (This is friction, not a blocker.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. The three graded checks (null required → quarantine, C001 region conflict, `monto < 0`) and `metrics`/`audit` each mirror a We Do (weDo.steps[0–2], [7], [15], [21–23]), so the asserts are reachable. But conflict detection needs `groupby`, which was never taught. Assembling quarantine rows with `reason` codes plus a `clean` frame from several masks is a larger integration step than any We Do, and it has no stub or helper.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. The 8 items cover required nulls, exact vs conflict, raw column, IQR risk, publishing on fail, `3,00`, fail-closed, and conflict action. Nothing covers the imputation cap or `was_null` order, cross-field rules, 1:1 cardinality or `duplicated(keep=False)`. selfCheck[5] tests the mislabelled «contrato PEN».
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase1_walked`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; no competency badge requires S16): no [O]. The key is always option 2. The capstone credential claims «security, packaging, SQL, RAG, deep learning, and automation» for Phase 1, which reflects the stale slugs, not data quality. Its `critical_competencies` (`reproducibility_determinism`, `communication_audience_tuned`, `business_framing_judgment`) are not exercised by any exam item.
- Value: mixed. The judgement taught is exactly what a junior data analyst needs and rarely gets: NaN ≠ 0, classify before dropping, quarantine with a reason, IQR proposes and the domain decides, publish metrics when failing. The opening story (`monto.fillna(0)` dragging the average ticket) is excellent. She is shortchanged by an untaught `groupby`, a money parser that is wrong for her own country's receipts, a wxPython playground and a meaningless exam. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak as briefed. It is one function plus a `__main__` with four asserts, with no test file, no memo template, no sample JSON output and a 4-row fixture. With an extended fixture, `tests/` and the memo, it would be a strong portfolio piece («quality gate con cuarentena y audit»).
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2, pandas 2.2.0): [I] Low for S16's own snippets. Outputs go through `to_dict(orient="list")`, `tolist()`, `float()`, `int()` or `json.dumps`, and none print dtype names, `[us]` resolution or numpy scalar reprs. `print("null_rate", rate)` in theory[3] prints a numpy float, which shows `0.4` under both numpy 1.26 and 2.x via `str`. The browser problem is the off-topic wxPython playground.

#### S17 — Joins, reshape, groupby y cierre analítico

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica semver y dependencias» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 5 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S17.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S17-packaging.json` (jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–10], iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23], youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck.questions[0–4], resources), and all 24 items of `S17-packaging.exam.json` (cross-checked against `prisma/seed.ts` §S17). Also read: `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (draw and shuffle logic), badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` whose `required_sections` include S17, `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` (CP-N2-A entry, `GATE_MAP`), and every file in `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-A/` (BRIEF, YOUDO, WEDO, IDO, RUN, FINAL_INTERFACE, RUBRIC.json, gate.json, execution.json, evidence_manifest.json, system_or_data_card.md, demo.py, data/generate.py, tests/test_demo.py, STARTER/). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S16 `.json` for `pd.DataFrame`, `import pandas`, `.merge(`, `groupby`, `transform(`, `pivot_table`, `melt(`, `rolling(`, `to_period`, `to_datetime`, `pd.Timestamp`, `.dt.`, `is_unique`, `indicator=True`, `MergeError`, `pd.errors`, `set_index`, `sort_index`, `reset_index`, `as_index`, `drop_duplicates`, `MultiIndex`, `nunique`, `pd.isna`, `leakage`, `resample`, `merge_asof`, `agg({`, `train`, `wheel`, `outlier`, `missingness`, `profiling`, `hipótesis`, `asociación`, `seed`, `CP-N2`, `capstone`.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] S17 is the gate for capstone CP-N2-A, but nothing in S17 mentions the capstone, and its deliverable is a different project. S17 never names it: `grep -c "CP-N2" S17-packaging.json` returns 0, and so do `capstone`, `outlier`, `profiling`, `missingness` and `seed`. Meanwhile the capstone requires «profiling, missingness, reconciliación, distribuciones, comparaciones por segmento, outliers con justificación, supuestos, limitaciones y memo ejecutivo que distinga observación/asociación/hipótesis/recomendación/limitación», plus the interface `eda.profile(dataset) -> EdaReport`. The S17 You Do instead asks for `portfolio_summary(clientes, tx, cutoff) -> dict` covering joins and leakage. Outliers, distributions and the observation/association/hypothesis memo are taught in S18, which mentions outliers 20 times and hypotheses 14 times, so after the gate. Lucía reaches the gate without having been taught half of what it grades. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-A/BRIEF.md` («Problema», «Interfaz de integración final»), compared with `youDo.context` and `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «**Gate:** S17» (BRIEF) vs. «Criterios de aceptación del dict (mínimo): `rows_merge` (int), `n_huerfanos_left_only` (int), …» (youDo.context) — fix: `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` + `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-A/BRIEF.md`: either move the CP-N2-A gate to S18, or rewrite the CP-N2-A brief so that it is the S17 portfolio (joins + reconciliation + leakage + limits memo) and leave profiling, outliers and the 5-category memo to CP-N2-B. Also add a «Capstone CP-N2-A» callout to `youDo.context` in `src/lib/course/sections/s17-packaging.ts` (a gate the learner is never told about cannot be prepared for).
- [high] [O] The CP-N2-A tests that are supposed to verify the capstone are placeholders, so a learner cannot tell whether she passed. The materials are also inconsistent with each other. `tests/test_demo.py` test_0…test_4 are each `assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check`, yet `YOUDO.md` names «Los tests normal/boundary/failure pasan» as a success criterion and `execution.json` records `"tests_pass": true`. The brief's data spec says «Campos: date, region, product, units, revenue, cost». `data/generate.py` emits three records with `name`/`email` and no such fields. `demo.py` builds `id/segment/amount/score` rows. `RUN.md` says «Solo stdlib», although S15–S17 teach pandas. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-A/tests/test_demo.py`, `data/generate.py`, `demo.py`, `RUN.md` — evidence: «assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check» — fix: `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-A/tests/test_demo.py`: replace the placeholders with real checks (missingness per column, a same-seed hash comparison, a regex for causal verbs in the memo, five memo categories). Make `data/generate.py` emit the fields that BRIEF declares, and allow pandas in `RUN.md` (tautological tests turn an unverified claim into a green check, and the learner gets no feedback on her capstone).

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam can be passed without knowing anything. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` shuffles question order (`// Shuffle question order (anti-cheating)`) but never option order, so «always pick B» scores 100%. Many distractors are absurd and are residue from the stale `packaging`/wxPython slugs: «Exportar a wheel», «Crear wheels», «Convertir a wx widgets», «Color favorito», «Sockets». — where: `S17-packaging.exam.json` items 0–23 — evidence: item 16 options «Borrar fechas || Suavizar o agregar en ventanas móviles… || Validar 1:1 joins || Crear wheels» (key 1) — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` §S17 (`'packaging'`): spread the keys across 0–3 and write plausible distractors drawn from the We Do bugs (inner vs. left, mean vs. sum, `max` cohort, `>` cutoff). Also shuffle options server-side in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (a constant key position makes the 70% pass mark meaningless).
- [medium] [O] Several exam variants test material S17 never teaches. Item 17 asks about «resample» (zero occurrences in S01–S17 content). Item 23 asks about «as-of / merges temporales ordenados» (`merge_asof`, never taught; S17's «as-of» is a boolean filter). Item 12 asks about `groupby(...).agg({...})` dict syntax, while S17 teaches only named aggregation. Item 14 expects «groupby + transform (p. ej. x / x.sum())», a lambda pattern that is never shown. — where: `S17-packaging.exam.json` items 12, 14, 17, 23 — evidence: «resample requiere típicamente: … Índice datetime-like y frecuencia explícita» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` §S17: replace these with variants on taught content: `transform('mean')`, named agg, `rolling(2)` NaN, filtering with `fecha <= cutoff` (assessment must align with what was taught, Biggs).
- [medium] [O] The You Do field `reconciled` cannot be computed honestly from the function's inputs. The docstring says «reconciled = True si el residual de partes vs. total de referencia cabe en eps», but `portfolio_summary` receives only `clientes`, `tx` and `cutoff`: there is no reference total. The natural implementation compares the sum of regional parts with the sum of the same merged rows, which is always True, a tautology. The rubric still gives reconciliation 15%. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (docstring), `youDo.rubric[3]` — evidence: «(o documenta residual en el memo si usas tabla puente externa)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s17-packaging.ts`: define it concretely, for example `reconciled = abs(m['monto'].sum() - tx['monto'].sum()) < 1e-9` (merged total vs. source total). That check fails on fan-out and on dropped orphan transactions, so it ties T4 back to T1 as theory[9] promises.
- [medium] [O] The You Do fixture cannot exercise the anti-join, and no expected output is given. Both clients in the starter have transactions, so `n_huerfanos_left_only` is always 0. The only guidance is «Esperado conceptual … n_huerfanos depende de matches». Lucía cannot tell whether her 0 is right or a bug. (Computed by hand from the fixture: rows_merge 3, n_huerfanos 0, total_monto 65.0, total_pre_cutoff 15.0, leakage_delta 50.0.) — where: `youDo.starterCode` (the `if __name__` block) — evidence: «# rows_merge=3, n_huerfanos depende de matches, total_pre usa ene, leakage_delta > 0» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s17-packaging.ts`: add a client with no transactions (C003) and a transaction with no master record, and print the exact expected dict (walkthrough Q4, she needs to see progress).
- [medium] [O] A theory paragraph and the code beside it disagree on the window size. The paragraph says «media móvil de montos diarios con window=2» and «tx en ene–mar 2024». The code uses `rolling(3)` on 1–4 January, labelled `roll3`. — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[2]` vs. `theory[8].code.code` — evidence: «media móvil de montos diarios con window=2» / `df["roll3"] = df["monto"].rolling(3).mean()` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s17-packaging.ts`: make both say window=2 (the I Do and We Do use 2), or update the text to 3.
- [low] [O] The You Do starter passes `cutoff` as a string (`"2024-01-31"`), although We Do T4-B-E1 warns «cuidado con comparar strings de fecha en vez de timestamps». — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`cutoff: str`) vs. `weDo.steps[21].preamble` — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s17-packaging.ts`: type it as `pd.Timestamp`, or add `cutoff = pd.Timestamp(cutoff)` as the first line (keeps the contract consistent with the warning).
- [low] [O] The comment in theory[10]'s code says «# mal: usar max global incluyendo febrero», but the line computes a `sum`. In weDo.steps[12], the success example `{'Madrid': 3.0, 'Lima': 3.0}` has a different key order from the graded output `{'Lima': 3.0, 'Madrid': 3.0}`. — where: `theory[10].code.code`; `weDo.steps[12].preamble` — fix: `s17-packaging.ts`: change the comment to «suma global», and copy the solution output into «Éxito».

- [medium] [O] The badge mapped to S17 does not match what she studied, and the slug misleads in the same way. The callout «Qué empaquetas aquí» exists only to explain away the «packaging» slug, and the resource link is `pillb.github.io/pyarcana/` with the section labelled «Joins · groupby · cierre» under id `packaging`. Lucía sees «Production Python Delivery» on the badge (see the verdicts) and cannot connect it to joins. — where: `theory[0].callout`, `id` — evidence: «No publicas un paquete en PyPI: empaquetas un dataset limpio…» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s17-packaging.ts` + routing: rename the id/slug to `joins-reshape-groupby` (with an alias), then drop the apologetic callout.

The rubric criterion «Script reproducible (`if __name__`) + memo de límites/no-claims» cannot be scored consistently without one. — where: `youDo.requirements[5]`, `youDo.rubric[6]` — fix: `s17-packaging.ts`: give a 5-heading memo skeleton (Pregunta, Números con n, Reconciliación, Leakage delta, No-claims) and state the file name `MEMO.md`.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — every operation in it (left merge + `indicator`, `<= cutoff`, sums, delta) is drilled in weDo.steps[0–23], and steps[23] is an explicit rehearsal. But `reconciled` is underspecified, the fixture hides the anti-join, and there is no expected output. Separately, the CP-N2-A gate she meets here asks for EDA content S17 never teaches.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — 5 items cover validate, anti-join, schema, cohort and leakage. Nothing covers agg vs. transform, sum vs. mean, or reconciliation/denominators (theory[7], theory[9]), and the distractors are throwaways («Usas CSV en vez de Excel», «Haces melt»).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`production_python_delivery_foundations`, plus `integrated_production_python_practice`, which needs S17-YOUDO only): no [O] — the badge's `newbie_friendly_description` claims «package Python code … `pyproject.toml` … CI/CD … FastAPI … tests», with critical competencies `type_safety_production_hardening` and `reproducibility_determinism`. The S17 exam covers joins/reshape/groupby/leakage and has none of that. It is also passable by always choosing option B. Fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: remove S17 from `production_python_delivery_foundations.required_sections` (the S10 packaging/CLI section is the evidence that fits) and add S17 to a data-prep badge (`independent_data_preparation` currently lists S06/S07/S08/S18).
- Value: mixed — the theory and drills are genuinely job-grade for a junior analyst in Lima (fan-out, anti-join, reconciliation, as-of leakage are exactly what interviewers probe). But the 9-hour section ends in an ungraded-looking dict, and the capstone gate grades other content with placeholder tests. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak — one script with the fixture embedded in code. No README, no data file, no tests are requested, and the memo has no defined location.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none identified with confidence. Outputs are `to_dict`/`tolist`/`float` casts with no pandas-3-only display or numpy-2 reprs. iDo/weDo steps are tagged `environment: local-python`.

**Issues**

The correct option is index 0 («Dependencias/seed/filtros versionados y script reejecutable»), but `correctIndex` is 1 («Un color corporativo exacto»). Lucía answers correctly, is marked wrong, and then reads an explanation that contradicts the marked key. — where: `S18-data-engineering.exam.json` item 22 / `prisma/seed.ts` §`'data-engineering'` (~line 5594) — evidence: «correctIndex: 1» under «'Un color corporativo exacto'» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: set `correctIndex: 0` (a reproducibility item that rewards «color corporativo» damages trust in the section's central claim).
- [high] [O] Apart from item 22, the key is index 1 on 23 of 24 items, and options are not shuffled (`src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` shuffles question order only). «Always B» scores 23/24. The distractors are mostly absurd («La versión de Node.js», «La contraseña de la base», «Falta de parquet», «Solo existe en PDF»). — where: `S18-data-engineering.exam.json` items 0–23 — evidence: `Counter({1: 24})` over `correctIndex` — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` §S18: spread the keys and use the We Do bugs as distractors (p10/p90 as quartiles, MAD anchored on the mean, a margin without √n, `pob − share`, a one-sided Tukey fence). - [medium] [O] The traceability template shown as the model contradicts itself: the question names Madrid and the metric computes Lima. The surrounding paragraph says Lima. — where: `theory[10].code.code` / `.output`; the same pattern appears in `iDo.steps[6].code.code` («pregunta: ¿Hay diferencia de ticket mediano Madrid vs. Bogota?» with «hipotesis: mediana_Lima > mediana_Bogota» and `resultado {'Lima': 28.0, …}`) — evidence: «pregunta: ¿El ticket mediano en Madrid supera 25 PEN?» / «metrica: median(monto | region==Lima)» — fix: `s18-data-engineering.ts`: align the region in the question, metric and result (the block teaches «cada conclusión referencia un cálculo», and here the calculation answers a different question).

In fact the starter generates a fresh 100-ticket dataset with no link to the S17 clientes/transacciones portfolio. — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.starterCode` header — fix: `s18-data-engineering.ts`: either load the S17 output (`portfolio` CSV) or say plainly «nuevo extracto sintético de tickets» (promising reuse that does not happen confuses her about what to carry forward).

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] Region names were swapped throughout the section (it looks like a find/replace artifact), so instructions, titles and code disagree. Lucía cannot tell which region an exercise asks about:
  - `weDo.steps[6].title` «Proporción de Madrid en la muestra», while the goal says «calcular e imprimir `share_Lima`». If she counts Madrid she gets 0.25 and fails.
  - `weDo.steps[16].title` «Tasa de flags solo en Madrid», while the goal says «media de flags donde region == "Lima"», and hint[1] says «si el contrato pide solo Madrid».
  - `weDo.steps[8].preamble` «(9 Madrid / 1 Berlin vs. 50-50)», while the code is `{"Lima": 9, "Madrid": 1}`.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes (mostly) [O] — the six checkpoints map one-to-one to weDo T1–T4, and the starter leaves only `se`/`ic95_z`, p90 and the association step to complete. The experimental/observational objective has no data to act on, and the starter's limitation text is wrong.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O] — the 10 items cover median vs. mean, correlation, data note, sample bias, CI meaning, Tukey, Cohen's d, self-selection and the p-value. The distractors are plausible (e.g. «La media recortada al 10 %», «Con un intervalo más estrecho, esa misma comparación pasaría a ser causal»). This is the strongest self-check of the three sections.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`independent_data_preparation`, which requires S06/S07/S08/S18; progress badges are walk-through only): partly [O] — the badge claims «clean it up … missing values, wrong types, and duplicates … basic descriptive statistics … validation checks». The S18 exam covers descriptive statistics, bias and honesty (the stats half). Cleaning and validation live in S15/S16, which this badge does not require. The exam is passable by always choosing B, and item 22 grades the correct answer as wrong. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: add S15/S16 to `independent_data_preparation.required_sections` so the cleaning claim has evidence.
- Value: fulfilled (content) / mixed (polish) — theory[6] and theory[8] (self-selection vs. randomisation, p-values, peeking measured at 0.045 vs. 0.191) are better than most paid bootcamps, and exactly what separates a junior analyst in interviews. The region mix-ups and the wrong exam key are what she will remember as sloppiness. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: moderate — a script with a JSON data note, seed and hash. It needs the README and an edge-case test that the rubric implies but the requirements omit.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): low [I]. The seeded outputs that are graded by exact match (`weDo.steps[11]` bootstrap `boot_ic95 (10.89, 31.17)`, `weDo.steps[14]` `r_residual 0.075`) assume the numpy `Generator` stream is identical in numpy 1.26.4 and 2.2.6. That holds in practice but is not guaranteed by NumPy's stream-compatibility policy. Everything else casts to `float`/`int` before printing, so there are no `np.float64(...)` reprs.
