- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_sql_query_development`, which requires S19+S37 with exam; also `integrated_data_analyst_practice` / `integrated_data_science_practice`, which require S19-YOUDO only): no [O] — the SQL badge's `newbie_friendly_description` claims «SELECT, JOIN, GROUP BY … window functions … CTEs», with critical competency `sql_competency`. The S19 exam has zero SQL items, and the section has no SQL exercise. The visualisation exam itself is passable by always choosing B, and item 4 has the wrong key. Fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: replace S19 in `applied_sql_query_development.required_sections` with the section that actually practises SQL (S12 teaches `sqlite3`/JOIN). Map S19 to the visualisation skill node (`python_visualization`) instead.
- Value: mixed — the honest-axis, parity and alt-text discipline is rare and valuable (WCAG 1.4.1, baseline-0 gate, sample_n/universe_n). But for 9 paid hours she gets little actual plotting practice, an SQL detour she cannot use, and examples that break the rules they teach. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good if completed — 4 versioned PNGs, a JSON spec, a parity CSV, alt texts and a README with a permitted vs. rejected claim is a portfolio piece a recruiter can see without running code. She should fix the starter's false alt example first.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): [I] `iDo.steps[2]` and `youDo.starterCode` (`build_bar_median`) pass a **list** to `ax.bar(..., hatch=hatches)`. I believe per-bar hatch lists are a recent Matplotlib feature (≥3.7 or later) and not supported in Pyodide's matplotlib 3.5.2, where the starter's smoke test would error or print a different `hatches` line. Verify in Pyodide, or set `bar.set_hatch(h)` per patch, which works in all versions. Everything else (`Agg`, `bar_label` ≥3.4, `savefig` to `BytesIO`) should run in 3.5.2.

#### S20 — Automatización robusta de Excel (platform id `rag`)

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica retrieval y similitud (simulado)» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 8 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S20.json`.

Read: all learner-facing fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S20-rag.json` (jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] incl. code/output/callouts, iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, feedback, retrospective, starter, solution, output), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck[0–7], resources) and all 24 items of `S20-rag.exam.json`; `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (option order); badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` whose `required_sections` include S20; `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` (CP-N2-B lists S20). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S19 `.json` for `openpyxl` / `pip install openpyxl` (S15 only, as an install note), `groupby` / `pd.DataFrame` (S15–S19), `hashlib` / `sha1` (S08+), `Counter` (S08, S18), `BadZipFile` (none), `PermissionError` (S09), `tempfile` / `shutil` (S08, S10), `fail-closed` (S07+), `allowlist` (S03, S11, S12), `idempot` (S05, S08, S09), `manifest` (S07+), `headless` (none), `dashboard` / `PNG` (S19 is the visualization section, so the S19 cross-references hold), `Madrid` (S04, S15–S19).

**Blockers**
- None found. All the code is stdlib, openpyxl or pandas, and openpyxl's install was mentioned in S15.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do's central gate checks nothing. In the starter, `portada` is read back from `Salida!B1`, the cell `materialize_salida` just filled with `det_sum`, and `n_xlsx` is read back from the `n_rows` it just wrote. `reconcile(det_sum, portada, …)` therefore compares a number with itself. It catches only "forgot to write the cell", never a wrong sum. The same goes for idempotency: `"idempotent": dig_a == dig_b` digests the same `rows` list reversed, and `dig_rows` sorts first, so the result is True by construction. The process is never run twice. Theory promises the opposite: «aquí se comprueba corriendo el proceso dos veces y comparando». The `backup` copies the fresh `out` straight after copying the master, so it is not a backup of a previous result. Lucía will see `reconcile_ok True` and `idempotent True` whether her code is right or not. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (steps 3–5), `theory[0].paragraphs[3]` — evidence: «portada = sal["B1"].value if sal["B1"].value is not None else -1.0» / «dig_b = dig_rows(list(reversed(rows)))  # mismo multiconjunto, otro orden» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s20-rag.ts`: reconcile against an independent source, either a `pd.read_csv` of the synthetic input or a `portada` cell the template already holds, and include one deliberately wrong fixture that must print `reconcile_ok False`. Prove idempotency by running `run_factory(input)` twice and comparing `dig_rows` of the two `Salida` sheets read back from disk. Back up the *previous* `results.xlsx` before overwriting it. (AGENTS.md treats tautological checks as worse than none, and this one sits in the deliverable.)
- [high] [O] The exam does not test what the section teaches, and the key is always option 2. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and `route.ts` serves the options unshuffled (`options: JSON.parse(q.options)`). About a third of the bank covers material the section explicitly sets aside or never mentions: named ranges, Excel Tables, `defined_names`, embedded chart series refs, protected sheets with `unprotect`, data-validation dropdowns, shared strings, atomic temp+replace. Theory[2] says the opposite: «Si el negocio habla de “tablas” o “named ranges”, en este tramo usamos el equivalente práctico: headers fijos». The concept slugs are English (`sheets-cells-tables-named`, `dates-locales-merged-protect`). — where: `S20-rag.exam.json[0,1,7,8,11,16]` — evidence: «En openpyxl, un named range bien usado sirve para:» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` block `'rag'`: rewrite those items from the taught cases (`data_only` on a fresh formula, the merge anchor in B1:D1, `structural_ok` superset, `reconcile(22.0, 22.005)`, mean vs sum in the pivot, `BadZipFile` vs `PermissionError`, the order-invariant digest), and shuffle the options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (Answering B to every item scores 100%.)
- [medium] [O] Six worked examples promise one result in the text and print another in the code. The prose was rewritten Lima→Madrid but the code was not. The "Éxito" line is the success contract she checks her output against. — where: `weDo.steps[13].preamble` / `.instruction`, `weDo.steps[16].preamble`, `weDo.steps[17].preamble`, `iDo.steps[0].preamble`, `iDo.steps[2].preamble`, `iDo.steps[5].preamble` — evidence: «**Éxito:** `{'Bogota': 7.0, 'Madrid': 15.0}`», but the solution output is «{'Bogota': 7.0, 'Lima': 15.0}». Also «Barcelona e Ica no están en la allowlist Madrid/Bogota/Berlin», but the code has `allowed = {"Lima", "Bogota", "Madrid"}` — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: align every preamble, instruction, feedback and retrospective with the code (Lima/Bogota/Madrid). Also fix «Observa las dos líneas de salida: nombre de archivo, master intacto y bold» (three items). (With an exact-match check, her correct `Lima` output looks wrong next to an «Éxito» that says Madrid.)
- [medium] [O] The demo and the We Do teach the weak evidence that theory explicitly rejects. Theory[4]'s code hashes the master before and after, with the comment that existence is not proof. iDo.steps[2] then prints `master_intact` as `master.exists()`, and weDo.steps[8] asks her to «probar que el master sigue existiendo». — where: `theory[4].code` vs `iDo.steps[2].code` / `.why`, `weDo.steps[8].preamble` — evidence: «"El archivo sigue ahi" no es lo que promete copy->save: promete que la plantilla no cambio» vs «El bool `master.exists()` es evidencia mínima de plantilla intocable para el manifest» — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: use the sha256 before/after comparison in iDo.steps[2] and weDo.steps[8] as well. (She will copy the demo, not the comment.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do leaves nothing on disk. Everything runs inside `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()`, so `results.xlsx`, the `.bak` and `manifest.json` are deleted when the script ends. Yet the checklist asks her to deliver «results.xlsx + manifest.json + master intacto», and S21 expects to read this workbook for its parity check. — where: `youDo.starterCode`, `youDo.requirements[5]`, `youDo.portfolioNote` — evidence: «with tempfile.TemporaryDirectory() as tmp:» — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: write to `./out/` (with `templates/` and `out/` in the repo layout) and keep the tempdir only for the in-browser demos. (The deliverable and the S21 input otherwise do not exist.)
- [medium] [O] Objectives and rubric require a batch the starter never scaffolds. Objective[4] and rubric[1] (20%) cover «batch con manejo de corrupt/lock», and rubric[3] (15%) covers «Pruebas o casos de borde documentados (corrupt, lock, re-run)». The starter processes exactly one in-memory workbook and has no `classify`, no quarantine and no test file. — where: `youDo.objectives[4]`, `youDo.rubric[1,3]`, `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «Batch con manejo de corrupt/lock (BadZipFile / PermissionError o cuarentena)» — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: add `inputs/` with 2 good files, 1 truncated `.xlsx` and a simulated lock, plus a `tests/test_factory.py` stub; or drop batch from the You Do rubric. (35% of the grade has no scaffold.)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The setting contradicts itself and her context. The client is «El VP de operaciones en Madrid», the currency is PEN («tolerancia documentada (0.01 PEN)»), and the labels must be «español profesional (es-PE)». Yet the starter's `Salida` labels are English snake_case (`total_monto`, `n_filas`). — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.requirements[4]`, `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «El VP de operaciones en Madrid entrega plantillas sintéticas» — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: make the VP Lima-based (or use EUR for Madrid), and show one es-PE label example («Total (S/)», «N.º de filas»). (Lucía cannot tell which locale requirement[4] grades.)
- [low] [O] English jargon is used without a gloss: «headless» (theory[3], iDo.steps[1].why, selfCheck[0].explanation; zero hits in S01–S19), «factory», «batch», «gate», «fail-closed». The last four were taught earlier but are dense here. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «en producción headless» — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: gloss «headless (sin pantalla ni Excel abierto)» at first use.
- [low] [O] `data_only=True` is discussed in theory, the self-check and three exam items, but she never sees it run. — where: `theory[3]`, `iDo.steps[1].retrospective` — evidence: «¿qué imprimiría `data_only` sobre una fórmula recién escrita sin cache?» — fix: `s20-rag.ts`: add a 4-line demo: save a workbook containing `=A1+A2`, reload it with `data_only=True`, and print `None`. (The question is posed but never answered on the page.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. The three `COMPLETAR` holes take one line each and are taught. The batch, the tests, a real reconciliation, a real re-run and on-disk artifacts are required but not scaffolded. As delivered, the success prints appear without her having to get anything right.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. The 8 items cover data_only/formulas, the merge anchor, manifest contents, idempotency, fail-closed reconciliation, copy→save, `BadZipFile` and `structural_ok` superset. They align with T1–T4, though distractors such as «La contraseña del VP» make some items trivial.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_rag_llm_service_development` [S20,S21]; `integrated_automation_engineering_practice` [S13,S20,S21,S24]; `integrated_ml_engineering_practice` [S09,S10,S20,S21,S23,S29,S43]; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; `progress_phase1_walked`; `progress_journey_completed`): no [O]. The RAG badge's `newbie_friendly_description` says «You can build a RAG application … and expose it as a FastAPI web service». S20 teaches openpyxl Excel automation, and no exam item touches retrieval, LLMs or FastAPI. The automation badge's critical competency `selector_resilience` is not in S20 either. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S20 from `applied_rag_llm_service_development` and `integrated_ml_engineering_practice`, and map S20 to a "reporting/Excel automation" competency that matches the stale `rag` id's real content.
- Value: mixed. The habits are exactly what a junior analyst in Lima is paid for: never overwrite the master, materialize numbers, reconcile with a tolerance, fail closed, quarantine bad files. The demos are short and clear. But the deliverable's checks are tautological and the exam is a template with a fixed key. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak as briefed. A single script writes into a temp dir that disappears, and there are no tests, no input fixtures and no README skeleton. With `templates/`, `inputs/` (including a corrupt file), `out/manifest.json` committed as a sample, and `tests/` that run the factory twice and diff the outputs, it would be a strong, recognisable portfolio piece.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): low [I]. openpyxl and pandas are, I believe, in the Pyodide distribution, and `tempfile`/`shutil`/`hashlib`/`zipfile` work on the in-memory FS. `pivot.to_dict()` output is the same in pandas 2.2 and 3.0, and no output depends on pandas-3 `str` dtype display or numpy-2 reprs. All iDo steps are tagged `environment: local-python`, so the learner is told to run them locally anyway. The You Do's `.xlsx` files never persist in the browser (and do not persist locally either; see Issues).

#### S21 — Reporting Factory: Jinja, DOCX, PDF y provenance (platform id `fastapi`; gates CP-N2-B)

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica validacion con pydantic (simulado)» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 8 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S21.json`.

Read: all learner-facing fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S21-fastapi.json` (learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] incl. code/output/callouts, iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23], youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, retrospective, rubric), selfCheck[0–7], resources) and all 24 items of `S21-fastapi.exam.json`; `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`; badges in `badge_catalog.json` listing S21; `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` (CP-N2-B entry, `GATE_MAP.S21`); every file in `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-B/` (BRIEF, FINAL_INTERFACE, IDO/WEDO/YOUDO, RUN, ACCESSIBILITY, PRIVACY, RESPONSIBLE_USE, SECURITY, RUBRIC.json, gate.json, execution.json, evidence_manifest.json, system_or_data_card.md, demo.py, data/generate.py, tests/test_demo.py, STARTER/). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S20 `.json` for `Jinja`/`jinja2` (none, so it is new here and taught here), `docx` / `PDF` (S19 only defers them to S21), `reportlab`/`pypdf`/`fitz`/`PIL` (none), `OCR` (none before S21; S24 «OCR y Document AI» confirms the forward reference), `a11y` (S19), `provenance` (S08, S12, S13, S15), `PNG`/`dashboard` (S19), `CP-N2-B` (S18, S19), `teclado` (none in S19–S21).

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] The capstone this section gates grades a different product, and its own package names S20/S21 by their stale ids. The CP-N2-B BRIEF lists the prerequisites as «S20 (RAG (esto es, Generación Aumentada por Recuperación…)), S21 (FastAPI)». Its interface is `reports.render(spec) -> ReportBundle` with `pdf_bytes, traceability, freshness, errors, a11y_checks`. Its P0s and tests include «eje engañoso detectado por check» and «codificación no es solo color». YOUDO.md asks for «filtro por región … check de contraste … exportación CSV», and ACCESSIBILITY.md requires «Operación por teclado». S21 teaches and asks for `build_docx / build_pdf / extract_and_render / manifest` and none of the above: no freshness, no empty-state, no axis check, no CSV, no keyboard (grep `teclado` in S19–S21: none). RUN.md says «Solo stdlib», but S21 needs `jinja2 python-docx reportlab pypdf pymupdf pillow`. If CP-N2-B is graded against BRIEF/RUBRIC, Lucía fails P0s she was never shown, and she will look for a RAG/FastAPI lesson that does not exist. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-B/BRIEF.md` («Prerrequisitos», «Criterios de aceptación»), `FINAL_INTERFACE.md`, `YOUDO.md`, `RUN.md` vs `youDo` of S21 — evidence: «S20 (RAG (esto es, Generación Aumentada por Recuperación: …)), S21 (FastAPI)» — fix: `course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py` + `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts`: regenerate the CP-N2-B BRIEF, interface, YOUDO, RUN and RUBRIC from the live S18–S21 content (EDA → S19 PNG → S20 workbook → S21 DOCX/PDF + provenance, `pending_review`). Take prerequisite titles from section titles, not ids. Either move the axis and colour checks into the S19 part of the brief explicitly, or drop the keyboard/CSV/contrast requirements. (The gate and the section must describe the same deliverable.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The capstone's tests and reference demo are tautological, yet recorded as passing. `tests/test_demo.py` has 5 of 6 tests reading `assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check`. `demo.py` hard-codes every `a11y_checklist()` value to `True` and emits `"status": "pass"`, and `execution.json` records «"tests_pass": true». `data/generate.py` emits `name/email/amount/status` records, not the BRIEF's «month, region, kpi, value, source_ref», and `demo.py` never reads them. — where: `CP-N2-B/tests/test_demo.py`, `demo.py`, `execution.json`, `data/generate.py` — evidence: «assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py`: emit one real assert per `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` entry, generate KPI fixtures matching «Datos», and set `tests_pass` only from a real run. (A green check over placeholders is worse than no test.)
- [high] [O] The exam key is always option 2, and the options are served unshuffled (`route.ts`: `options: JSON.parse(q.options)`). All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`. The topics do match S21 (Jinja context, missing-safe formatting, DOCX/PDF, digital vs OCR, exec summary/method/findings, captions/limits, a11y, provenance/approval). But most distractors are absurd («Compilar el template a C», «Traducir todo a latín», «La playlist del analista»), so an answer can be picked by elimination. — where: `S21-fastapi.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «"correctIndex": 1» (24/24) — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` block `'fastapi'`: build items on the section's real traps (`Template` without autoescape on `<b>28</b>`, `None` → `—` vs `0.00`, `all([])`, `any` vs `all` in `ready`, `fitz.open(pdf)[0]` lifetime, `pending_review` vs `approved`, 2 vs 1 decimals), with plausible distractors. Shuffle the options in `route.ts`. (The gate exam for a capstone should discriminate.)
- [medium] [O] One We Do exercise has its bug hidden by the fixture. The starter `round(v, 0)` on `[28.04, 28.0]` already prints the expected `[28.0, 28.0]`, so the exact-output check passes before she changes anything. The feedback admits it. — where: `weDo.steps[18].starterCode` / `.feedback` — evidence: «Si imprimes `[28.0, 28.0]` con `round(v, 0)` “de casualidad” en este fixture, el bug sigue ahí» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s21-fastapi.ts`: use `vals = [28.04, 27.96, 28.26]`, so that `round(...,0)` → `[28.0, 28.0, 28.0]` and `round(...,1)` → `[28.0, 28.0, 28.3]`. (Feedback must change when she fixes the bug.)
- [medium] [O] The decimal contract contradicts itself across theory, We Do and You Do. Theory[3] and weDo.steps[4] use 2 decimals for detail tables. Theory[8] says explicitly that the rule is not "same decimals everywhere". Theory[7], weDo.steps[18] and youDo.objectives[3] say 1 decimal across the whole package. Theory[8]'s own code then «proves» consistency by rounding `[28.04, 28.0, 28]` numerically and printing `consistente True`, even though the paragraph above says `28.0` vs `28` printed are «dos números». — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[8].code`, `weDo.steps[18].preamble`, `youDo.objectives[3]` — evidence: «unidades PEN a 1 decimal en todo el paquete» vs «no dice que todo el paquete use la misma cantidad de decimales, porque las tablas de detalle usan 2» — fix: `s21-fastapi.ts`: state one rule (detail 2 / summary 1, the same in every channel), rewrite theory[7] and weDo.steps[18] to match, and have theory[8]'s check compare the *formatted strings* (`fmt_pen(v)`), not rounded floats. (She cannot satisfy both versions in the You Do.)
- [medium] [O] An I Do demo uses the pattern its own theory block calls flaky. Theory[5] explains why `fitz.open(pdf)[0]` can free the document and make `get_pixmap` fail intermittently, and uses a `with` block. iDo.steps[3] then does exactly `fitz.open(pdf)[0].get_pixmap().save(png)`. — where: `iDo.steps[3].code` vs `theory[5].code` comment — evidence: «Con fitz.open(pdf)[0] nadie guarda el documento … get_pixmap falla a veces y funciona otras» — fix: `s21-fastapi.ts`: use `with fitz.open(pdf) as doc: doc[0].get_pixmap().save(png)` in iDo.steps[3]. (She copies demos, and this one fails intermittently.)
- [medium] [I] The You Do's parity and visual checklist cannot be computed from her real S19/S20 outputs, so she will hard-code them. Objectives and portfolioNote require parity across «dashboard + xlsx + informe» and a `visual_checklist {dashboard,xlsx,doc}`. But the S20 You Do writes `total_monto`/`n_filas` (15.0, n=2) inside a temp dir that is deleted, never `median_Lima 28.0, n=40`. The starter has no input path for the S19 PNG or the S20 workbook. `data_sha1_8` in every exercise hashes the constant `b"synthetic"`, not the data or the artifacts, although requirement[5] asks for «lista/hashes de artefactos». — where: `youDo.objectives[2,4]`, `youDo.requirements[3,5]`, `youDo.starterCode`, `weDo.steps[21–22]` — evidence: «visual_checklist {dashboard,xlsx,doc} y approval.status == "pending_review"» — fix: `s21-fastapi.ts` (and `s20-rag.ts`): make S20 materialize `median_Lima`/`n_Lima` into `out/results.xlsx`, add `load_xlsx_metrics(path)` to the S21 starter, compute sha256 of each artifact's bytes in `manifest()`, and derive `visual_checklist` from real checks (PNG size > 0, xlsx reopened, DOCX headings). (Otherwise «paridad» is three hard-coded dicts, as in iDo.steps[5].)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The section's URL/id says «fastapi», and the capstone package and badges call it FastAPI. The content never mentions FastAPI. Lucía, who reads the course map and her badge cards, will think she skipped something. — where: section id `fastapi`; `CP-N2-B/BRIEF.md`; badge `production_python_delivery_foundations` — evidence: «build a FastAPI service (a modern Python web framework)» — fix: rename the learner-visible slug where possible, or at least the BRIEF/badge text (see Verdicts). (Stale ids leak into learner-facing text.)
- [low] [O] Jargon is used before or without a gloss: «factory» / «Reporting Factory», «em-dash» (a Spanish reader knows it as «raya»), «smoke», «outline» (glossed), «bundle» (glossed), «pending_review». — where: `iDo.steps[1].description`, `iDo.intro` — evidence: «Render condicional seguro de tabla con missing como em-dash» — fix: `s21-fastapi.ts`: «raya (—)» at first use; «fábrica de reportes» in prose.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. `build_docx`, `build_pdf`, `extract_and_render` and a `pending_review` manifest are each rehearsed in weDo.steps[6–11, 21–23] and are doable. Real parity with the S19 dashboard and S20 workbook, artifact hashes, and the rubric's tests for «missing, needs_ocr, parity fail» have no scaffold. The capstone CP-N2-B asks for a different product altogether.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. The 8 items cover context/template separation, needs_ocr, parity, closure contents, `decision=None`, missing as «—», Heading styles and the `all([])` trap, all of which are taught in T1–T4. Autoescape and provenance hashing are untested.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_rag_llm_service_development` [S20,S21]; `production_python_delivery_foundations` [S15,S17,S21]; `integrated_ml_engineering_practice`; `integrated_automation_engineering_practice`; `integrated_production_python_practice`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; `progress_phase1_walked`): no [O]. The RAG badge claims «build a RAG application … expose it as a FastAPI web service with tests». The delivery badge claims FastAPI. `integrated_production_python_practice` lists S21 for packaging and deployment. Neither S21 nor its exam touches retrieval, LLMs, FastAPI, Docker or CI. The exam does test document/report generation, which no badge names. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S21 from the RAG/FastAPI/production badges and attach it to a reporting/communication competency (e.g. `communication_audience_tuned`, `python_visualization`) whose claim matches «trazable DOCX/PDF report with provenance».
- Value: mixed. The section teaches rare, valuable habits for a data analyst: missing ≠ 0, one context feeding every channel, reopening artifacts as evidence, finding ≠ decision, a minimal a11y gate, no self-approval. Most of the We Do produces real files. But the capstone it gates, the badges it unlocks and its exam point elsewhere or cannot discriminate. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: promising. Real DOCX/PDF/PNG files and a JSON manifest are exactly what a recruiter can open. It needs a README with a rendered PNG, a `requirements.txt`, sample inputs from S20, and a `tests/` folder covering the three edge cases the rubric names.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): high for T2 [I]. `python-docx`, `reportlab` and `pypdf` are not in the Pyodide 0.26.2 distribution, and `micropip` needs network, which the runtime lacks. PyMuPDF (`fitz`) is, as far as I know, not shipped in 0.26.2. So theory[4–5], iDo.steps[2–3], weDo.steps[6–11] and the You Do cannot run in the browser. `jinja2` and Pillow are available, so T1, T3 and T4 run. All steps are tagged `local-python` and theory[0] gives the `pip install`, so the page does warn her, but a phone-only session would stall at T2. The snippets write `informe.docx`/`.pdf`/`.png` to the working directory.

#### S22 — Email, identidad y aprobación humana: borrador .eml (platform id `rapidfuzz-entity`; opens CP-N2-C)

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica fuzzy matching (simulado)» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 5 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S22.json`.

Read: all learner-facing fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S22-rapidfuzz-entity.json` (learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] incl. code/output/callouts/figures, iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23], youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, retrospective, rubric), selfCheck[0–4], resources) and all 24 items of `S22-rapidfuzz-entity.exam.json`; `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`; badges in `badge_catalog.json` listing S22; `catalog.ts` CP-N2-C (gate S26, sections S22–S26). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S21 `.json` for `MIME`/`email.mime` (none, so new and taught here), `html.escape`/`XSS` (S21 only, autoescape), `urlparse` (none), `OAuth`/`least privilege` (none), `scope` (S01, S03, S05, S14, but not in the OAuth sense), `fromkeys`/`OrderedDict`/`isdisjoint` (none), `timezone` (S08), `idempot` (S05, S08, S09), `hashlib` (S08+), `lambda` (S05+), `class` (S11), `rapidfuzz` (none). Also checked S23 («Browser RPA con Playwright») and S24 («OCR y Document AI») to confirm the forward references in theory[3] and theory[9]: they are correct.

**Blockers**
- None found. Everything is stdlib and runs offline, and no step sends real mail.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam key is always option 2, and the options are served unshuffled (`route.ts`: `options: JSON.parse(q.options)`). All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`. The bank's topics do match the section well (MIME, sanitization, scopes, drafts/expiry, recipient verification, BCC, state machine, idempotency), which is better than S20/S21. But the distractors are mostly absurd («Usar la contraseña personal del CEO», «Convertirse en SMS», «Page Objects»), so it measures common sense, not the skill. — where: `S22-rapidfuzz-entity.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «"correctIndex": 1» (24/24) — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` block `'rapidfuzz-entity'`: rebuild the items on the section's own traps (`'example.pe' in url` vs `example.pe.evil.test`, `Name=` vs `Content-Disposition`, `approve` from `draft` → `invalid`, `now > expires_at` inverted, `[:16]` with `|` separator, `list(set(xs))` order) with plausible distractors. Shuffle the options in `route.ts`. (Answering B everywhere scores 100%.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do's acceptance prints give almost no feedback. As shipped, the starter already prints `verified True`, `key_len 16` and `draft_id d001`, so 3 of 5 acceptance lines pass before she writes anything. The two MIME «Completa» holes (HTML part, meta attachment) have no printed check at all, so a learner who skips them still sees full acceptance. The idempotency branch is dead code: `store = {}` is created just before `if key not in store`, so the `else` (retry → same `draft_id`) never runs, though rubric[1] grades «idempotency key» and theory[9] makes retry the point. `expires_at` is stored but never checked. — where: `youDo.starterCode`, `youDo.requirements[6]`, `youDo.rubric[1]` — evidence: «store = {}\ndraft_id = None\nif key not in store:» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s22-rapidfuzz-entity.ts`: add acceptance lines `parts N` (count of `Content-Type:` = 5 with the attachment), `has_html True`, `retry_same_id True` (call `create_draft_once` twice) and `usable True`, and save `out/draft_d001.eml`. (Acceptance that passes on the untouched starter is tautological.)
- [medium] [O] One self-check item has two defensible answers. Option[1] «Reutilizar el draft solo si el contenido no cambió» describes the taught contract exactly, because the key includes `body_ver`, and theory[9] says «Solo un cambio de `body_ver` … genera una key distinta y un draft nuevo». The key accepts only option[2]. — where: `selfCheck.questions[3]` — evidence: «Reutilizar el draft solo si el contenido no cambió» — fix: `s22-rapidfuzz-entity.ts`: change the distractor to something clearly wrong, e.g. «Reutilizar el draft aunque cambie el destinatario». (Lucía, having understood the key, gets marked wrong.)
- [low] [O] A theory demo does not show what its text describes. Theory[7] says the external partner arrives «en CC» and «Tras higiene, el externo pasa a BCC y el conteo de visibles baja». In the code the partner is already `"role": "bcc"` in the input, so the BCC-forcing branch never fires and nothing visibly changes. — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[2]` vs `theory[7].code` — evidence: «{"email": "externo@other.test", "role": "bcc"},» — fix: `s22-rapidfuzz-entity.ts`: set the input role to `"cc"` and print the visible list before and after. (Feedback should show the policy acting.)
- [low] [I] The starter for weDo.steps[15] can pass by chance. `list(set(['a@x','b@x','a@x']))` has hash-randomised order across runs, so roughly half the time it prints the expected `['a@x', 'b@x']` with the bug still present. — where: `weDo.steps[15].starterCode` — evidence: «print(list(set(xs)))» — fix: `s22-rapidfuzz-entity.ts`: use a fixture where set order visibly differs from first-appearance order (e.g. 5 addresses), or print `== ['a@x','b@x']` alongside.

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] OAuth is used as if known. «OAuth», «service account con delegación de dominio e impersonación explícita», «scopes» and «least privilege» appear at first use with no plain-Spanish definition (grep S01–S21: no OAuth, no least privilege). The lab then uses invented scopes (`mail.draft`), and the exam asks about real ones («Gmail/Outlook vía OAuth», «refresh token»). — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «usa **OAuth** (o, en Workspace, una **service account** con delegación de dominio e impersonación explícita)» — fix: `s22-rapidfuzz-entity.ts`: add two sentences («OAuth: el usuario autoriza a tu app a hacer *solo ciertas cosas* en su cuenta, sin darle su contraseña; cada cosa permitida es un *scope*…») and gloss «mínimo privilegio». (The exam tests the vocabulary the theory assumes.)
- [low] [O] A gloss that explains nothing: «en jerga de operaciones, “filtra” significa filtrar o exponer la lista», which is repeated in the callout. — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[7].callout.content` — evidence: «“filtra” significa filtrar o exponer la lista de quién trabaja el caso» — fix: `s22-rapidfuzz-entity.ts`: «CC muestra a cada destinatario las direcciones de todos los demás».
- [low] [O] The section id says «rapidfuzz-entity», but the only matching is a hand-rolled prefix score (weDo.steps[14]), and theory[6] warns against reading it as identity. If the id leaks into a URL or catalog, she may expect an entity-resolution/fuzzy-matching lesson. — where: section id — evidence: «calcular prefijo común, redondear a 2 decimales y anexar siempre `match_no_es_fraude`» — fix: rename the learner-visible slug or title mapping. (Stale id.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O]. Every hole (HTML part, attachment with `Content-Disposition`, `apply(..., "submit", "analyst", audit)`) is rehearsed in weDo.steps[0–2, 20], and the end-to-end pipeline is short. But the acceptance prints cannot tell a finished You Do from an unfinished one (see Issues).
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. It has only 5 items (other sections have 8), and two of them (Q1, Q4) test the same «match ≠ fraude» idea. MIME structure, HTML escaping/host allowlist, BCC/minimum disclosure and the state machine (four of the eight outcomes) are untested. Q3 has a second correct option.
- Exam tests the badge claim (no competency badge requires S22; only `progress_phase1_walked`, `progress_journey_completed` and `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent` via CP-N2-C): partly [O]. The exam content matches the section. The capstone credential's description says the Phase-1 capstones «pull together security, packaging, SQL, RAG, deep learning, and automation», and S22 contributes HITL/email safety, which no badge names. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: add the human-approval/least-privilege competency S22 actually builds (e.g. under `security_mindset`) to the CP-N2-C capstone credential's text, and drop «RAG» from it unless a section teaches RAG.
- Value: fulfilled, with caveats. This is the most coherent of S20–S22. The ethic is clear and it rings true (draft-only, human approval, match ≠ fraud, BCC for externals). The stdlib-only code runs anywhere, and the We Do starters each carry one named, visible bug. The exam's fixed key and the weak You Do acceptance lines are the caveats. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good potential. A `.eml` she can open in Thunderbird/Outlook, an audit JSON and a state-machine table make a strong "responsible automation" story for an analyst. As briefed, though, nothing is written to disk and there are no tests. Adding `out/*.eml`, `audit.jsonl` and `tests/` for invalid transitions, retry and host bypass would make it portfolio-ready.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none [I]. Only stdlib is used (`email.mime`, `html`, `re`, `urllib.parse`, `hashlib`, `datetime`, `collections`). theory[5]'s `bytes 184` depends only on the `email` package output for a fixed message, which is the same in CPython 3.12 and Pyodide's 3.12. Outputs that use `datetime.now()` print only booleans. No network or SMTP is used.

#### S23 — Browser RPA con Playwright

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica procesamiento de imagenes (simulado)» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 9 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S23.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S23-computer-vision.json` (jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] incl. code/output/callouts/figure, iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23] (preamble, instruction, hints, tests, feedback, starter, solution, retrospective), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck[0–8], resources) and all 24 items of `S23-computer-vision.exam.json`. Also: the "Pruébalo tú mismo" playground for this section (`src/components/course/SectionView.tsx` l.2114 `demos['computer-vision']`, selected by `demos[sectionId]` l.4143), `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (option serving), badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` whose `required_sections` include S23, and `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/{BRIEF.md,gate.json,IDO.md,WEDO.md}`. Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S22 `.json` for `DOM`, `CSS`, `XPath`, `ARIA`, `HTML`, `selector`, `cookie`, `localStorage`, `CAPTCHA`, `ToS`, `403`, `429`, `handoff`, `Playwright`, `Page Object`, `for-else`/`for/else`/`else del for`, `lambda`, `key=lambda`, `next(`, `hashlib`, `sha256`, `PermissionError`, `CI `, `flaky`, `on-call`, `runbook`, `backoff`, `idempot`, `fixture`, `checkpoint`, `trace`, `user-agent`, `Chromium`, `MFA`, `CSRF`, `OTP`, `pip install`, `str | None`.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] The section's own "Pruébalo tú mismo" playground teaches something else entirely: image processing. `demos['computer-vision']` is titled «Practica procesamiento de imagenes (simulado)» and runs a 5×5 grayscale matrix with `threshold` and «Contar objetos». The section is browser RPA with Playwright. For Lucía this is the one block that says «Este editor corre Python de verdad en tu browser». It shows up under a Playwright lesson with no link to anything she just read, so she cannot tell whether she missed a part of the section. — where: `SectionView.tsx` `demos['computer-vision']` (rendered for section id `computer-vision`) — evidence: «title: 'Practica procesamiento de imagenes (simulado)'» / «# Simulacion de conceptos de vision por computadora» — fix: `src/components/course/SectionView.tsx`: replace the demo with a dict-DOM locator + `should_retry` + `handle_blockers` playground built from `theory[2]`, `theory[7]` and `theory[9]` (all three are stdlib and already pass) (the demo is keyed by a stale slug, and the content behind that slug changed).

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The section contradicts itself on whether a `last_ok_step` checkpoint makes a run idempotent, and the self-check marks the contradicted version correct. theory[7] says plainly that a checkpoint «no alcanza para llamarlo idempotente». It also warns that skipping earlier steps «supone que ninguno dejó estado que el siguiente necesite». A login session is exactly that kind of state. Yet weDo[17] says the checkpoint «hace la corrida idempotente a nivel de paso». youDo.objectives[4] asks for «reanudación idempotente a nivel de paso». selfCheck[7] keys «form (el siguiente step), evitando el doble envío del login» and marks «Volver a login: la sesión pudo expirar entre corridas» wrong, even though theory[7] supports that answer. "Double-sending a login" is also not a thing. — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[2]` vs `weDo.steps[17].retrospective`, `youDo.objectives[4]`, `selfCheck.questions[7]` — evidence: «no alcanza para llamarlo idempotente» vs «hace la corrida idempotente a nivel de paso» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s23-computer-vision.ts`: drop "idempotente" from weDo[17] and youDo.objectives[4]. Rewrite selfCheck[7] so the stem says the session is still valid (storage_state reused), and change the key text to «form; reanudar no vuelve a enviar lo ya confirmado» (as written, the assessment rewards the misconception the theory warns about).
- [high] [O] The iDo demo for ToS returns the wrong action, and its own "why" says so. iDo[7]'s code is `if sig.get("captcha") or sig.get("tos"): return "human_handoff"`, so ToS gives `human_handoff`. The step's `why` and theory[9] both say ToS → `abort`, and weDo[22] grades exactly that. Lucía is told the demo output «coincide exactamente» with the model she will copy. — where: `iDo.steps[7].code` vs `iDo.steps[7].why` — evidence: «ToS prohibido → abort (gana sobre handoff)» — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: check `tos` first and return `"abort"`, add a `print(handoff({"tos": True, "captcha": True}))` line, and update the output (the worked example models the anti-pattern that weDo[22] then penalises).
- [high] [O] The rule on success traces contradicts itself, so a requirement cannot be met with the scaffold. theory[6].callout says CP-N2-C «exige trace de éxito y de falla». youDo.requirements[2] asks for «Trace de al menos un éxito y una falla forzada». But theory[6] says «en éxito, sample rate bajo o desactivado», weDo[14] teaches «Solo adjunta trace cuando ok es False», and the You Do starter's `evidence()` adds `trace` only when `not ok`. Following the section exactly fails the requirement. — where: `theory[6].callout.content`, `youDo.requirements[2]` vs `weDo.steps[14].hints[2]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`evidence`) — evidence: «Solo adjunta trace cuando ok es False (política de no llenar disco)» — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: say once that the You Do needs one success trace as acceptance evidence and that production samples success traces. Add an `always_trace` flag to `evidence()` (two lessons give opposite rules for the same artefact).
- [high] [I] The exam can be passed by position, and options are served in authored order. 23 of 24 keys are option index 1; the exception is item [8] (index 0). `route.ts` returns `options: JSON.parse(q.options)` with no shuffle. Most distractors are absurd («Phishing al equipo de seguridad», «Entrenar YOLO», «Desactivar el antivirus del host»), so "pick B" scores about 100%. — where: `S23-computer-vision.exam.json[0..23].correctIndex` — evidence: «Detenerse y escalar a handoff humano / canal permitido» (key [21], index 1) next to «Fuerza bruta de imágenes» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (`'computer-vision'` block, l.6787): spread keys across 0–3 and replace joke distractors with misconceptions the section names (sleep-fixed, retry-captcha, nth-child, export-ignored). Also shuffle options server-side in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (the exam currently measures test-taking, not RPA judgement).
- [medium] [O] Exam items use terms and ideas the section never teaches. Seen in the exam but not in S01–S23: «Desactivar CSRF», «Hardcodear OTP de producción», «login es multi-factor», «Firmar DKIM», «Compilar TypeScript a WASM», «Entrenar YOLO», «Correlation ids», «to_have_count», «Recovery tras un modal inesperado». Item [15]'s key requires «con backoff», but theory[7] calls backoff «opcional». Item [11]'s key «bypass controlado» clashes with the section's «sin bypass» stance. — where: exam items [8], [11], [13], [14], [15], [16] — evidence: «Usar entorno de prueba con bypass controlado o handoff documentado» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: rewrite these items against taught content (stale-handle recovery, `should_retry` set, `storage_state`) (assessment must align with the activities).
- [medium] [O] The section repeatedly says its work feeds CP-N2-C, but CP-N2-C contains none of it. S23 says «el web adapter de CP-N2-C», «el sandbox de CP-N2-C usa credenciales demo (`demo` / `sandbox`)» and «El adaptador web de CP-N2-C exige trace». The CP-N2-C BRIEF is an Excel → validation → email-draft flow. It lists S23 as «computer vision» and has no portal, locator, trace or download step. theory[1] promises «un sitio de práctica local con HTML y CSV sintéticos», but the course provides no such site, so the Playwright path has nothing to run against («no llames esto sin un servidor local de práctica»). — where: `theory[1].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[4].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[6].callout`, vs `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/BRIEF.md` («Prerrequisitos») — evidence: «S23 (computer vision), S24 (RPA advanced)» — fix: either ship a tiny static practice portal (HTML + CSV, served with `python -m http.server`) under `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/data/portal/` and add a web-adapter acceptance line to `BRIEF.md`, or remove the CP-N2-C claims from `s23-computer-vision.ts` (the learner is promised a capstone link that does not exist).

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] The core vocabulary of the section is never taught. «DOM», «CSS», «XPath», «`#app > div:nth-child(3)`», «div», «árbol de accesibilidad», «ARIA», «cookies y localStorage» and «403» have no hits in S01–S22. HTML appears only in S21–S22 as autoescape for templates. Lucía has never seen a web page's structure. theory[0] gestures at it («el tercer div dentro del segundo formulario»), but nobody tells her what a selector is before «Prefiere get_by_role… sobre CSS/XPath frágiles». — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[0]`, `iDo.steps[0].why`, `theory[4].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «no `#app > div:nth-child(3)`» — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: open theory[2] with a 6-line HTML snippet (a `<button>` and a `<label>` + `<input>`), and map each part to role / name / testid / CSS selector in a small table. Gloss cookies / localStorage at first use (selector strategy cannot be understood without knowing what is being selected).
- [medium] [O] weDo[4] is built on `for … else`, which no earlier section teaches. No hit for `for-else`, `for/else` or `else del for` in S01–S22. The hint «El bloque else del for corre solo si no hubo break» is the first time she meets it, inside an "independent" exercise. — where: `weDo.steps[4].hints[1]` — evidence: «for-else en Python: else se ejecuta cuando el loop termina sin break.» — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: add `for…else` to theory[3] (the `wait_until` demo is a natural place), or rewrite E2 with a `found` flag (an exercise should not depend on an unseen construct).
- [medium] [O] The Page Object / auth model changes shape four times. theory[5] uses `submit(user, password)` with `ctx["auth"] = "authenticated"`. iDo[3] uses `go(ctx)` with `ctx["auth"] = True`. weDo[9] uses `submit(ctx, password)` with a boolean. weDo[11] uses `'anonymous'`/`'authenticated'` strings again. The You Do uses `submit(ctx, user, password)` and «mutar ctx['auth'] a True/False». Lucía has to guess which contract the You Do grader expects. — where: `theory[5].code`, `iDo.steps[3].code`, `weDo.steps[9].solutionCode`, `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «mutar ctx['auth'] a True/False» vs «self.ctx["auth"] = "authenticated"» — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: pick one signature and one value type (the state strings, which weDo[11] needs) and use them everywhere (consistency is the point of a Page Object).
- [low] [O] Text points to a sketch that is not where it says. theory[0].callout says «El sketch de arriba es la forma API», but the Playwright sketch is in theory[1], below it and marked optional. theory[2] also admits it contradicts itself: «aunque el párrafo anterior los nombre en el otro orden». — where: `theory[0].callout.content`, `theory[2].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «El sketch de arriba es la forma API» — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: change to «el bloque de referencia de abajo», and reorder theory[2].paragraphs[0] to role → label → testid → text → CSS so the apology is not needed.
- [low] [O] The CAPTCHA recovery figure sits in the auto-wait block. theory[3] (Auto-waiting) carries figure `S23-rpa-recovery`, captioned «Un CAPTCHA no es un fallo transitorio», three blocks before CAPTCHA is introduced. — where: `theory[3].figure` — fix: `s23-computer-vision.ts`: move the figure to theory[7] or theory[9].

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — The dict version is easy. The starter already implements everything except `LoginPage.submit` («el resto ya modela el contrato»), so the code part is about one line. The rest is a runbook. The "trace of a success" requirement conflicts with the taught `evidence()`. The Playwright option has no practice site to run against.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — Its 9 items cover all 8 subtopics. But selfCheck[7] keys an answer the theory argues against, and several distractors are joke options («Un video de YouTube genérico de Playwright»).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`responsible_machine_learning_evaluation` [S10+S23], `integrated_data_science_practice`, `integrated_ml_engineering_practice`, plus progress/capstone badges `progress_phase1_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`): no [O] — Three competency badges list S23 as ML or deep-learning evidence. Examples: «train a machine-learning model (… PyTorch or TensorFlow) and evaluate it honestly», critical competency `leakage_prevention`. S23 now teaches browser RPA, so a badge built on the stale `computer-vision` slug is earned with Playwright knowledge. Meanwhile the automation badges that claim `selector_resilience` (`reliable_automation_development`, `integrated_automation_engineering_practice`) do not require S23, the only section that teaches selectors. Fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: move S23 from the ML badges to the automation badges.
- Value: mixed — The judgement content is good and current: role-first locators, condition waits instead of sleeps, verifying the downloaded file, retrying only transient errors, API/export first, handing CAPTCHA and ToS cases to a human. But a paying learner finishes a section called «Browser RPA con Playwright» without ever running Playwright or seeing a web page's structure. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is a mostly pre-written dict script plus a runbook, with no README, tests, data or demo required, and a reviewer would see no browser automation in it.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): `theory[1].code` (`playwright_sketch.py`) does `from playwright.sync_api import sync_playwright` at import time, so it raises ModuleNotFoundError if run in the browser. Its printed output («sketch_ready True») is also tautological. All other snippets are stdlib-only (`hashlib`, dicts; `str | None` in the You Do is fine on Pyodide's Python 3.12), so I infer no other risk.

We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 5 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 0 console/page/network errors; no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Also: the section playground (`src/components/course/SectionView.tsx` l.2188 `demos['rpa-advanced']`), `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`, badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` whose `required_sections` include S24, and `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/{BRIEF.md,gate.json}`. **Blockers**
- None found. Every exercise and the You Do are stdlib-only and can be solved from S24 plus earlier sections (`re`, `datetime.strptime`, `key=lambda` taught in S06/S07/S08).

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The section teaches the DPI rule and then grades the opposite. theory[2] states that upscaling recovers nothing, and that the lab «no acepta un upscale como sustituto». The figure caption repeats it. Then the code, the demo and three exercises do exactly that upscale: `max(dpi, 200)` turns 96 into 200 and reports success. weDo[0] feedback praises «`max(96, 200)` eleva al piso de OCR del lab». iDo[0].retrospective asks her to explain «por qué 72 DPI se convierte en 200 antes de llamar al OCR». Lucía cannot satisfy both statements. The graded behaviour is the anti-pattern the theory names. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[0]` and `theory[2].figure.caption` vs `theory[2].code`, `iDo.steps[0]`, `weDo.steps[0]`, `weDo.steps[2]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`preprocess`: «dpi >= 200») — evidence: «Reescalar después de 96 a 200 no recupera nada» vs «elevar el DPI efectivo al piso del lab con `max(dpi, 200)`» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s24-rpa-advanced.ts`: change the contract to a gate, `dpi_ok = dpi >= 200`, and if not → `human_rescan`/`needs_rescan`. Keep `source_dpi` and never rewrite `dpi`. Update weDo[0]/[2] tests to print `False`/`rescan`, and update the You Do comment (the activity and assessment must not reward the misconception the section exists to prevent).
- [high] [O] Money is handled as `float`, against the course's own earlier rule. S02 says «los montos usan `Decimal` y no `float`… para sumar montos en soles», and S08 lists `Decimal` in the CP-N1-B stack. S24 defines `total` as `float` in the schema. `norm_total` returns `float(s)`. Cross-field validation relies on `abs(sum(lines) - total) > 0.01`, a float tolerance that exists only because of float error. Exam item [13] then asks for «tipo numérico + moneda». — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[0]` («`total` float»), `theory[6].code` (`norm_total`), `theory[7].code`, `weDo.steps[14]`, `youDo.requirements[3]` («"150,00" → 150.0») — evidence: «`ruc` str de 11 dígitos, `total` float» — fix: `s24-rpa-advanced.ts`: return `Decimal(s)` from `norm_total` and compare `sum(lines) != total` exactly (or quantize to `Decimal("0.01")`). Expected outputs become `Decimal('150.00')` (S24 would otherwise silently undo a habit S02 built on purpose).
- [high] [I] The exam can be passed by position: all 24 keys are index 1. `route.ts` serves `options: JSON.parse(q.options)` unshuffled, and most distractors are absurd («Convertir tablas en audio», «Cambiar a BCC», «El usuario apaga el laptop»). — where: `S24-rpa-advanced.exam.json[0..23].correctIndex` — evidence: «Mejorar legibilidad de glifos para el motor OCR» (key [0], index 1) beside «Cifrar el PDF» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (`'rpa-advanced'` block, l.7078): spread keys over 0–3, and write distractors from the section's named misconceptions (average confidences, `replace(",", "")`, `%m/%d/%Y`, label bbox, upscale). Also shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (positional keys make the 70% pass mark meaningless).
- [high] [O] The exam redefines a metric the section taught and contradicts a locale rule. The section defines `coverage_auto = auto / (auto + review)`, «qué fracción de documentos pasa sin HITL». Exam item [20] asks «Cobertura de plantillas/layouts mide» and keys «Qué fracción de formatos reales/sintéticos objetivo el sistema maneja bien», which is a different quantity. Item [13] asks to normalize «S/ 1,234.50» (EN thousands format) «con reglas de locale». theory[6] said that format «no es fixture del lab: no lo "adivines"; recházalo». Item [17] (persisting a reviewer's correction with actor) and item [4] (denoise erasing i-dots) were never taught. — where: exam items [4], [13], [17], [20] vs `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[6].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «Un formato EN con miles (`"1,150.00"`) no es fixture del lab… recházalo» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: rewrite [20] around `coverage_auto` versus `acc_ruc`, rewrite [13] with «150,00»/«1.150,00» or key "reject", and replace [4]/[17] with taught concepts such as `ruc_conf_missing` or value bbox (exam and section must agree).
- [medium] [O] The privacy guidance contradicts itself on deleting raw images. theory[9].paragraphs[0] says a bbox is only meaningful with a reviewable rendition, and that deleting the raw image leaves the reviewer unable to audit. The callout of the same block says «Borra imágenes crudas cuando el expediente solo necesita campos + bbox». — where: `theory[9].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[9].callout.content` — evidence: «Si borras la imagen cruda sin dejar una copia de revisión controlada, el revisor no puede auditar el resaltado» — fix: `s24-rpa-advanced.ts`: callout → «Borra la imagen cruda solo cuando el caso cierre la revisión; mientras tanto conserva una copia controlada con caducidad» (one block should give one rule).
- [medium] [O] The S23 → S24 bridge does not hold. S24 says its input is «Una descarga verificada del adaptador web de S23», and the You Do «cierra el arco S23→S24». But S23 downloads a report export («CSV sintético», `b"synthetic-report-xlsx"`), which has no pixels to OCR. S24 processes scanned invoices. CP-N2-C, which both sections say they build, is an Excel → email-draft flow with no OCR or intake step (`BRIEF.md` lists S24 as «RPA advanced»). — where: `theory[1].paragraphs[2]`, `youDo.context` vs S23 `youDo.starterCode` and `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/BRIEF.md` — evidence: «el artefacto descargado (meta de imagen + tokens OCR simulados) entra al **document intake** de CP-N2-C» — fix: `s24-rpa-advanced.ts`: either make the S23 artefact a scanned supplier PDF, or drop the "same artefact" claim. Add an intake stage to `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/BRIEF.md`, or remove the CP-N2-C references (the narrative promises continuity the materials do not provide).
- [medium] [O] The section playground teaches the opposite retry policy of S23 and has nothing to do with OCR. `demos['rpa-advanced']` is «Practica orquestacion y retries». Its `retry` decorator catches every `Exception`, sleeps and retries, and its fake download points at «https://api.com/report». S23 taught retrying only transient errors, never blanket retries. — where: `SectionView.tsx` `demos['rpa-advanced']` — evidence: «except Exception as e: … Reintentando en {delay}s...» — fix: `src/components/course/SectionView.tsx`: replace it with a stdlib intake playground (theory[6] `norm_*` + theory[7] `validate`) (this demo is keyed by the stale slug).

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The You Do asks for things its scaffold cannot produce, without saying she must invent them. The context says «Procesa al menos 3 "documentos"», but the starter has tokens for one document. `validate` needs `lines` for `total_mismatch`, but the tokens carry no line items. Golden row 1 is `{"ruc_pred": None, … "total_pred": None}` with «completa pred vs. true», but no instruction explains where predictions come from, i.e. by running her own pipeline on synthetic docs she writes herself. Lucía may treat the golden as data to fill by hand, which makes the metrics tautological. — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.starterCode` (`tokens`, `golden`) — evidence: «# Mini golden (al menos 2 docs) — completa pred vs. true» — fix: `s24-rpa-advanced.ts`: ship 3 synthetic docs (tokens + line items + `true` values) and have `golden` built by running the pipeline: `pred = extract(doc)` (otherwise the "evaluation" can be hand-typed to any number).
- [medium] [O] Several terms are used before or without explanation. «bounding boxes» and «confidence» in the tagline stay English throughout; bbox appears earlier only in S19, and the theory's «la caja» is the only gloss. Other unglossed terms: «zip-bomb», «DoS», «spoofable», «firmas mágicas», «mediana o morfología», «intake» used as a noun, «Document AI» (in the title but never defined), «worker», «batch nocturno». — where: `tagline`, `theory[9].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[3].paragraphs[1]`, `title` — evidence: «Tope de tamaño (p. ej. 5_000_000 bytes) mitiga zip-bomb y DoS al worker OCR. El mime del caller es **spoofable**» — fix: `s24-rpa-advanced.ts`: gloss each at first use in Spanish, e.g. «spoofable (el remitente puede declarar un tipo falso)», «zip-bomb (archivo pequeño que al abrirse ocupa gigabytes)», and give one line saying what Document AI is (a reader who is slow in English loses the security reasoning).

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — Every function she needs (`preprocess`, `parse_kv`, `norm_*`, `validate`, `field_acc`, `gate_file`) appears almost verbatim in the theory or weDo. But she must invent two more documents, line items and a prediction step that the brief never describes. The DPI "preprocess" she is told to complete enshrines the upscale the theory forbids.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — It has only 5 items, covering abstention, cross-field, per-field accuracy, mime gate and orientation. Normalization (coma decimal, day-first dates, RUC with a letter) and value-bbox evidence, the section's most concrete skills, are not checked.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`reliable_automation_development` [S13+S24], `integrated_automation_engineering_practice` [S13+S20+S21+S24], plus `progress_phase1_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`): no [O] — Both automation badges list `selector_resilience` as a critical competency. The automation badge description says «build a … script (using Playwright or Selenium)… picking stable references to page elements». S24 teaches OCR intake and its exam has no selector or browser item. Selectors are taught in S23, which neither badge requires. Fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: require S23 for these badges, or drop `selector_resilience` from them and add an OCR/intake badge that S24 actually evidences.
- Value: mixed. The judgement content is strong and relevant to a Lima back-office or data-analyst job: abstaining per critical field, PE comma decimals, day-first dates, «needs_review ≠ fraude», and reporting accuracy and coverage as a pair. But a section titled «OCR y Document AI» never touches an image or an OCR engine (Tesseract is only «si está instalado»), and its central DPI lesson contradicts its exercises. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: decent if she follows requirements[8], which asks for a README with how-to-run, fixtures and metrics. Neither tests nor a demo image are required, and a reviewer would see "OCR" with no OCR in it.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none in the lesson snippets (stdlib `re`, `datetime`, dicts). The playground `demos['rpa-advanced']` uses `time.sleep` and `random.seed(42)`. Its expected output relies on the first `random.random()` being ≥ 0.6, which holds in CPython's Mersenne Twister and in Pyodide, so I infer it is stable. It is off-topic, though (see Issues).

- [high] [O] The You Do golden set cannot be met by an honest implementation, and its fail-closed shapes contradict each other. `GOLDEN` gives `input` + `gold`, but `eval_golden` expects `{"pred","gold"}` rows, and nothing explains where `pred` comes from. To reach `exact` on row 1, the mock must output `"hallazgo": "mediana_ok"` and `"evidence_ids": ["e1"]`. In `mock_or_http`, the timeout path must return `{"status": "human_review"}`, which fails `SCHEMA_KEYS`, while the injection row expects `"hallazgo": "human_review"` with `evidence_ids ["inj"]`. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`GOLDEN`, `mock_or_http`, `eval_golden`) — evidence: «TimeoutError → {"status": "human_review"}» vs «"gold": {"hallazgo": "human_review", … "evidence_ids": ["inj"] …}» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s25-streamlit-dashboards.ts`: add a fixture of OCR fields with ids (`{"e1": {"n": 40, "mediana": 28.0}}`) that the mock reads from. - [high] [I] Building the «adapter HTTP local (fixture)» is the headline path of the You Do, and the starter's `call_local_endpoint` uses `urlopen`, but no section teaches running a local HTTP server. The starter also tells her to catch `TimeoutError`, while theory[5] says to catch the client's own exception. `urlopen` usually wraps a connect timeout in `URLError`, so her fallback would not fire. — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.starterCode` (`call_local_endpoint`, `mock_or_http` comment) — evidence: «captura el del cliente que estés usando» (theory[5].paragraphs[1]) vs «TimeoutError → {"status": "human_review"}» — fix: `s25-streamlit-dashboards.ts`: make the in-process mock the main path and the HTTP fixture an optional extension, or add a 15-line `http.server` fixture to iDo. Untaught or unscaffolded: the localhost HTTP fixture, how to produce `pred` from `input`, a golden set an honest mock can match, the «baseline comparado» (no baseline appears in any demo or exercise), and the README (no template). Nothing checks `field_match_rate`, the `/1000` cost formula, `issubset` direction or licence vs `not_for`, which are the computations the We Do drills.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase1_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; `progress_journey_completed`): no [O] — the bank is answerable by position (key always 1), so it certifies nothing. The progress badge's claim («including … section exam for each section») is literally met. The capstone credential's `critical_competencies` (`reproducibility_determinism`, `communication_audience_tuned`, `business_framing_judgment`) get no S25 items. Its `newbie_friendly_description` says the N2 capstones cover «RAG, deep learning», which S14–S26 never teach (grep: 0 hits).
- Value: mixed — the governance thinking is unusually good and interview-relevant: rule vs model vs LLM, licence ≠ ethics, schema-first, injection as design, and score ≠ fraud. But for a paying beginner, nine hours of "AI endpoints" with no AI call, no prompt written and a trivial exam is thin. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one script with three `NotImplementedError`s, a golden set that rewards hard-coding, and no tests folder, fixture file, README template or real or local model. A recruiter would read "AI assistant" and find a keyword `if`.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): `youDo.starterCode` `call_local_endpoint` (`urllib.request.urlopen` to localhost) cannot work in Pyodide, which has no sockets. `theory[4]` `transformers.pipeline` is commented out, correctly. Everything else is stdlib (`hashlib`, `re`, `json`, `dict | dict`), and its outputs are version-independent.

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] The S26 You Do and the CP-N2-C gate define different products, so Lucía can finish one and fail the other. CP-N2-C's acceptance tests require «aprobación expira → no se envía», «logs no contienen correos», «rerun del mismo job → mismo resultado» and an interface `rpa.run(job) -> RpaAudit` over an «Excel sintético de ventas mensuales». The S26 You Do has no expiry, no log redaction, no Excel ingest, no `rpa.run`, and a `ckpt` that stores step names, not job/item ids. Its stage names also differ from CP-N2-C's `demo.py` (`ingest_excel … human_approval, email_draft, completed`, with no AI step at all). «Sin idempotencia» and «Logs con PII» are P0s she is never pointed to. Expiry and recipient allowlists were taught in S22 (grep `expir`: 58 hits), but S26 never asks her to reuse them. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/BRIEF.md` («Criterios de aceptación», «Interfaz»), `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` vs `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «`rpa.run(job) -> RpaAudit` — Recibe job (dict con excel path + spec)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: make the You Do starter expose `run(job) -> dict` with `draft`, `approvals`, `audit_trail`, `rollback_available` and `logs_redacted`. Read an `.xlsx` the S20 way, and add S22's `expires_at` check and a `redact()` for logs. Or rewrite `course-state/capstones/CP-N2-C/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json,demo.py}` to the S26 7-step contract. **Issues**

Running the untouched starter prints seven `success`, `can_draft_after True` and `package pass 45`. iDo.steps[7] and theory[9] do the same thing with literals such as `print("audit", …, "fraud_labels", 0, "n2_regression", "pass")`, while the preamble asks her to predict «por qué `n2_regression` es `pass` (re-run real)». — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`advance`, `package_e2e`), `iDo.steps[7].code`, `theory[9].code` — evidence: «"n2_regression": "pass",  # re-run real: CP-N2-A/B/C + privacy + CF-2» — fix: `s26-integrator-phase1.ts`: make `approve` require an explicit `decide(action, actor, reason)` call from outside `run_all`. Compute `n2_regression` from an actual `subprocess.run` of the three capstone test files, and start `hitl` with pending items. (This is a tautological check: the section's own rule «no "planned"» is broken by its scaffold, and the "human" in HITL is the code.)
- [high] [O] The exam bank has the key at index 1 for all 24 items, and options are served unshuffled (`route.ts` l.116). **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — each piece (topo order, backoff, DLQ, create-once, superseded, `any` gate, audit, SLO alert) was drilled, and the starter even runs. But the We Do exercises are mostly one-token fixes on literals (e.g. `>100`→`>60`, `all`→`any`). Nothing makes her assemble resume-from-persisted-checkpoint, a real retry loop or a reject→requeue cycle, and the starter already prints success. CF-2, the N2 regression and the CP-N2-C criteria (expiry, redaction, Excel, `rpa.run`) are undefined or unscaffolded.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the 8 items cover path order, the `any` gate, `fraud_labels=0`, DLQ+owner, `run_id` immutability and compensation, all of which were taught. The distractors are mostly absurd («Borrar S14», «Exportados a prensa»), and nothing checks backoff arithmetic, idempotency or the cycle rule.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase1_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent`; `progress_journey_completed`): no [O] — the key is always index 1. `integrated_python_ai_capstone_independent` lists `prerequisite_badges` `applied_sql_query_development` (needs S37) and `applied_rag_llm_service_development` / `responsible_machine_learning_evaluation`. Those map to S20/S21 and S10/S23 through stale slugs ("rag", "fastapi", "sklearn", "computer-vision"), which are now Excel, Documents, CLI and Playwright. The Level-2 capstone credential therefore cannot be earned at the end of Level 2, and its description promises «SQL, RAG, deep learning», which S14–S26 do not teach. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: drop S37-dependent prerequisites, re-map the stale badges and rewrite the description from the real titles.
- Value: mixed — the operational ideas (DLQ with owner, compensation as `superseded`, three-queue HITL, «si esto se cae… ¿quién se entera?») are excellent and rare for a data-analyst track. But Level 2 closes on a self-approving starter, a hard-coded "regression pass", a positional exam and a gate that grades a different product. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a single script whose output is green before she writes a line. No data, no tests, no Excel, no AI step connected to S25. As shipped it would not survive a reviewer asking "what does `pass` mean here?".
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none in lesson code (stdlib only: `collections.deque`, dicts, `zip`; no pandas or numpy reprs). The S26 playground prints `time.time()` deltas, which are nondeterministic and have no `expectedOutput`. A real Excel ingest (needed for CP-N2-C) via `openpyxl` would need `micropip` in the browser, and file paths on disk will not exist there.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] Lucía is never shown pytest running, yet the You Do is graded as a pytest suite. Every snippet in the section calls test functions by hand and prints. `@pytest.fixture`, `pytest.raises` and `@pytest.mark.parametrize` appear only in comments, and no We Do asks her to write one. The section never shows `pip install pytest`, a real `pytest -q` output, or what a red failure looks like. A grep of S01–S26 finds `pytest` only in S10, as a `dev` optional dependency that is never run. The You Do then requires «Demo reproducible: `python -m pytest tests/ -q`», plus a `tests/` + `conftest.py` layout. This fails cognitive-walkthrough Q2 (the action is not on the page) and Q4 (she has never seen the success signal she must produce). — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[5].code`, `youDo.requirements[3]`, `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «En este lab, si aún no instalas pytest, modelamos el criterio con try/except» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s27-async-concurrency.ts`: add one I Do step that shows the install command (`python -m pip install pytest` in the S01 venv), a two-file `tests/` folder, and the literal output of `pytest -q` both green and red (one `F` with its assert diff). Turn at least three We Do exercises into real `def test_*` / `@pytest.fixture` / `pytest.raises(..., match=)` edits. (The section is titled «Estrategia de pruebas con pytest», and its outcomes 2–4 are about pytest mechanics she never performs.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam has the key at index 1 for all 24 items, and options are served unshuffled. Most distractors are absurd («Cambiar el logo del producto», «Un sleep infinito», «Ser un symlink a /etc/passwd»). Several items rely on things S27 never taught. Item 5's key says «Tolerancia/pytest.approx», but the section only teaches `math.isclose` (`pytest.approx` has 0 hits in S01–S27). Item 16's explanation uses «MTTR» and item 3 uses «teardown», both with 0 hits. Item 18 uses «ties, missingness». — where: `S27-async-concurrency.exam.json[0..23]`, items 3, 5, 16, 18 — evidence: «Tolerancia/pytest.approx o comparación en dominio discreto» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` block `'async-concurrency'` (l.7955): rewrite the items from the section's own cases. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — the logic (normalize, exact_match, three threshold bands, negative tables with messages, a hand mutant) is fully modelled and the starter runs. The pytest layer the You Do requires (`conftest.py`, fixtures, `pytest.raises`, `python -m pytest tests/ -q`) is shown only in comments, and she never sees a real pytest run.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — its 5 items cover the pyramid, oracle, mutant, the non-fraud boundary and the default fixture scope, all of which were taught. Nothing checks AAA, `isclose`, message fragments, tmp files or branch coverage, and three items have joke distractors («Fraude automático», «Load tests en prod»).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`reliable_async_python_development` [S27, S28, S31]; `integrated_production_python_practice`; `progress_phase2_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): no [O] — `reliable_async_python_development` claims «You can write async Python (using `async`/`await` and the `asyncio` library…) … test async code with `pytest-asyncio` … orchestrate LLM agents». S27 contains zero `async`/`await`/`asyncio` (the only hit is the stale id `async-concurrency`), and the exam is passable by position. The badge is mapped by the stale slug. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S27/S28 from `reliable_async_python_development` (or map it to whichever section actually teaches asyncio), and create or attach a testing competency (pytest, fixtures, mutation) that S27 really evidences.
- Value: mixed — the ideas are excellent and rare for a junior track: the oracle problem, «una prueba que nunca falló no ha probado que sabe detectar nada», hand mutants, and risk-ranked coverage. But nine hours yield 24 one-token edits (`+`→`*`, `min`→`max`, `==`→`!=`) and no pytest run, so she pays for pytest and practises print statements. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good shape on paper (`er_norm.py`, `tests/`, `conftest.py`, a README with the risk pyramid and the killed mutant). It would read well if she can actually get `pytest -q` green, but nothing in the section gets her there.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): lesson code is stdlib only (`copy`, `math`, `tempfile`, `pathlib`, `datetime`) and safe. The page's only runnable editor, `demos['async-concurrency']` («Practica concurrencia con asyncio»), is off-topic. It uses `time.sleep` and prints `time.time()` deltas with no `expectedOutput`: a nondeterministic demo in the section that teaches that flakes are «diseño incorrecto». Fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with an AAA + mutant playground that has an expected output.

The distractors are weak («Generar PII real… para el golden»), and selfCheck[4]'s explanation repeats the false Postgres-in-S29 claim.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`reliable_async_python_development` [S27, S28, S31]; `integrated_production_python_practice`; `progress_phase2_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): no [O] — `reliable_async_python_development` describes async/await, `pytest-asyncio` and orchestrating «LLM agents». S28 has 0 hits for `async`/`await`/`asyncio`/agent, because the badge follows the stale slug `llm-agents`. The exam is also positional. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: detach S28 from that badge, and attach it to a data-testing / reproducibility competency (`reproducibility_determinism` fits what S28 actually evidences).
- Value: fulfilled (with caveats) — for a junior data role this is one of the most transferable sections. Idempotence, golden-with-approval, «validate returns a list of errors», `C(n,2)`, NFC and seed discipline are what data-quality interviews probe. The caveats are that the drills are one-line toggles, and the exam is not worth the time. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: strong. Four named test files, a `README_suite.md` with seeds, clock and limits, synthetic data, and an sqlite integration test make a credible portfolio piece, if she can run pytest locally.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): [I] `sqlite3` is an unvendored stdlib module in Pyodide, and `CodePlayground.tsx` `REQUIRED_PACKAGES` has no entry for it. theory[8], iDo.steps[6], weDo.steps[18] and the You Do starter would therefore raise `ModuleNotFoundError` if pasted into the browser editor (these blocks render as static `local-python`). theory[9]'s `seeded_r 0.3238` depends only on CPython's Mersenne Twister and is safe. The page's only runnable editor, `demos['llm-agents']` («Practica razonamiento de agentes (simulado)», a ReAct Thought/Action loop), is off-topic. Fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with a seeded idempotence + golden-drift playground.
