I reproduced one inference about the You Do (a seed `INSERT` followed by an explicit `BEGIN` on CPython's default `sqlite3` connection → `OperationalError: cannot start a transaction within a transaction`) with a three-line stdlib check, not with lesson code. Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S28 `.json` for `JOIN`, `LEFT JOIN`, `GROUP BY`, `PRIMARY KEY`, `REFERENCES`, `FOREIGN`, `lastrowid`, `ROW_NUMBER`, `PARTITION BY`, `NOT EXISTS`, `ON CONFLICT`, `upsert`, `executescript`, `IntegrityError`, `ROLLBACK`, `isolation_level`/`with con`/`BEGIN` (S12 uses `BEGIN` + `commit`), `EXPLAIN`, `3NF`, `fan-out` (S17), `provenance`, `append-only` (S16, S26), `schema_migrations`, `Red Andina`, `CASO-LIM`, `Postgres`.

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] The You Do's core method is likely to crash the first time Lucía tests it, and nothing on the page explains why. `insert_decision_with_evidence` must do «BEGIN → decisión → lastrowid → evidence → COMMIT», as every demo does with `con.execute("BEGIN")`. Every demo issues `BEGIN` right after DDL, where Python's `sqlite3` has no transaction open. In the You Do she must first seed `entities` and `candidate_pairs` with ordinary `INSERT`s, which (by default) silently open an implicit transaction. Her explicit `BEGIN` then raises `OperationalError: cannot start a transaction within a transaction`. A grep of S12 and S29 finds 0 hits for `isolation_level`, `autocommit` or «implícit». — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`insert_decision_with_evidence` comment), `youDo.requirements[6]`, `theory[6].code` — evidence: «# Completa: BEGIN → decisión → lastrowid → evidence → COMMIT;» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s29-mlops.ts`: teach one pattern explicitly. Either `with con:` (a context manager that commits or rolls back), or `con.commit()` after seeding plus a callout: «el módulo sqlite3 abre una transacción implícita antes de un INSERT; haz commit del seed antes de tu BEGIN». Add a We Do whose starter has this exact bug. (The error message is cryptic, and it lands on the method that carries the section's main invariant.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam tests what the section explicitly says it does not teach. theory[6] states: «Niveles de isolation avanzados (`READ COMMITTED`, `SERIALIZABLE`)… No los damos por dominados… Aquí el foco es atomicidad, no isolation multi-conexión (eso se retoma en S38)». Yet the exam concept `acid-isolation` has two of its three variants on isolation: item 13 («Un nivel de aislamiento más estricto suele…») and item 14 («Lectura dirty… significa»). Other items use Postgres-only vocabulary the section never touches: «índice GIN», «VACUUM FULL», «COPY TO STDOUT», «bloat/vacuum», «nested loop», «WAL», «deadlocks», «cluster_id». All 24 keys are index 1, and options are not shuffled. — where: `S29-mlops.exam.json` items 1, 7, 9, 13, 14, 16, 20 vs `theory[6].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «Lectura dirty (dirty read) significa:» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` block `'mlops'` (l.8539): replace them with items on what S29 drilled. Examples: `NOT IN` with a NULL in the subquery, `COUNT(*)` vs `COUNT(col)`, `C(5,2)` with `a.id < b.id`, the PRAGMA per connection, `ROW_NUMBER` with and without `PARTITION BY`, upsert vs append-only, SCAN → SEARCH after `CREATE INDEX`. Shuffle options in `route.ts`. (She is examined on a topic deferred to S38, and the bank is passable by always choosing B.)
- [medium] [O] The section contradicts itself on where evidence hangs. theory[2] says «deja `evidence` como hija de la **decisión** (FK a `decisions.id`)», and youDo.requirements[4] says «sin pair_id redundante en evidence». But every transaction demo and exercise models `evidence(pair_id …)`: theory[6].code, iDo.steps[4] (`evidence(x int)`), weDo.steps[13]. None ever shows `cursor.lastrowid` (0 hits in S01–S29), which the You Do requires to link evidence to its decision. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[1]` vs `theory[6].code`, `weDo.steps[13].solutionCode`; `youDo.requirements[4,6]` — evidence: «CREATE TABLE evidence(id INTEGER PRIMARY KEY, pair_id TEXT, note TEXT)» — fix: `s29-mlops.ts`: change theory[6], iDo.steps[4] and weDo.steps[13] to `evidence(decision_id INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES decisions(id))`, using `cur = con.execute(INSERT decision); con.execute(INSERT evidence, (cur.lastrowid, …))`. (She would build the You Do from the pattern she practised, and it fails requirement 4.)
- [medium] [O] The section breaks its own rule against printing concepts. theory[0].callout promises «Cada afirmación… se verifica con consultas y código que imprime el resultado real, no con strings que solo nombran el concepto». Several demos then print hard-coded literals: `print("overwrite", False)`, `print("provenance", "ev_02")`, `print("antijoin", True)`, `print("upsert", True)`, `print("pattern", "repository")`, iDo `append_only True`, `cte True`, `repo True`. The preambles even ask her to «predice el booleano `append_only`», which is a constant. — where: `theory[3].code`, `theory[4].code`, `theory[7].code`, `theory[9].code`, `iDo.steps[1,2,5,7].code` — evidence: «print("append_only", True)» — fix: `s29-mlops.ts`: compute each flag, e.g. `append_only = len(labels) == 2 and labels[0] == "review"`, `antijoin = "p1" not in pending`, `upsert = row == ("Ana López", 2)`. Otherwise drop them. (S27/S28 call this «teatro de verde»; here the course does it itself.)
- [medium] [O] The plan demos print things that do not answer «SCAN vs SEARCH». theory[5] prints `plan_rows 1`, the number of rows in the plan, which says nothing about index use. theory[8] and iDo.steps[6] accept `"INDEX" in plan.upper()`, which would also be true for an unrelated covering index. Learning outcome 4 says «planes con EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN (SCAN vs. SEARCH)». — where: `theory[5].code`, `theory[8].code`, `iDo.steps[6].code` — evidence: «print("plan_rows", len(plan))» — fix: `s29-mlops.ts`: print the plan's `detail` column before and after `CREATE INDEX` (e.g. `SCAN pairs` → `SEARCH pairs USING INDEX idx_pairs_block (block_key=?)`), and assert `"SEARCH" in detail`. (She should see the plan, not a count of it.)
- [low] [O] The You Do's file layout conflicts with `:memory:`. Requirement 7 asks for `schema.sql`, `seed.py`, `repository.py` and `test_store.py`, but `connect()` creates the schema inline on `:memory:`, which vanishes when the process ends. A separate `seed.py` therefore cannot seed anything the tests see. The fixture «CASO-LIM-029» and the «Red Andina» case are referenced as if they existed, but no data are given. — where: `youDo.requirements[7]`, `youDo.starterCode`, `theory[2].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «Entregables: schema.sql, seed.py, repository.py, test_store.py (suite que falla hasta implementar) y README.md» — fix: `s29-mlops.ts`: specify `connect(path=":memory:")` reading `schema.sql`, `seed(con)` as a function imported by the tests, and ship 6–10 synthetic rows for CASO-LIM-029 in the starter. (Q1: she cannot tell what «seed.py» must do.)

Exam item 2 tests «anomalías de actualización», a term the section never uses. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «Normaliza a **3NF** para hechos del ER» — fix: `s29-mlops.ts`: add two sentences and a before/after mini-table, e.g. «tercera forma normal: cada dato vive en una sola tabla; si el nombre está copiado en cada par, al corregirlo en una fila quedan las demás desactualizadas (anomalía de actualización)». Most are flagged as «se profundiza en S38» but still appear in the body text. — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[9].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «**Migraciones** versionadas: expand (añadir columna/índice) → backfill → contract (retirar lo viejo)» — fix: `s29-mlops.ts`: gloss them in Spanish («ampliar → rellenar datos → retirar lo viejo»; «WAL: el diario de escrituras de SQLite»), or cut the S38 material. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — each building block (PK/FK/CHECK/UNIQUE with the PRAGMA, append-only inserts, the `NOT EXISTS` queue, `ROW_NUMBER`/`PARTITION BY`, upsert, `schema_migrations`, repository with `:memory:`) is demonstrated and drilled. But the central `insert_decision_with_evidence` needs `lastrowid` (never shown), a `decision_id` FK (contradicted by every transaction demo), and a `BEGIN` that will collide with the implicit transaction opened by her seed inserts.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O] — its 8 items cover canonical order, append-only, atomic decision+evidence, repository, no-drop-without-backup, the per-connection PRAGMA, `IS NULL` and SCAN→index. All of these are taught and practised. Missing: anything on CTE/`ROW_NUMBER`/`PARTITION BY`, the section's most job-relevant skill.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_mlops_pipeline_delivery` [S29, S43]; `integrated_ml_engineering_practice`; `progress_phase2_walked`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): no [O] — `applied_mlops_pipeline_delivery` claims «deploy a machine-learning model to production… monitor it for drift… trigger retrains automatically», with critical competency `mlops_fluency`. S29 has no ML, no deployment and no drift (1 hit for `mlops`, the stale id). Meanwhile `applied_sql_query_development`, whose description promises «window functions… CTEs», requires S19 + S37 and not S29, the section that actually teaches window functions and CTEs. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S29 from the two MLOps/ML badges, and add S29 to `applied_sql_query_development.required_sections` (with a rewritten S29 exam as its evidence).
- Value: fulfilled — for Lucía's goal (a junior data analyst role) this is the most directly job-relevant section of the three. Anti-joins without the `NOT IN`/NULL trap, `COUNT(*)` vs `COUNT(col)`, top-1 per group with `ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY…)`, append-only history, and reading a query plan are standard SQL interview material, and the examples are concrete and correct. It is undercut by an exam on isolation levels and a badge that labels the section «MLOps». GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a good portfolio shape (DDL, seed, repository, tests, a README with a defensible invariant). It needs the fixture data and the transaction pattern fixed, or her `test_store.py` will show the reviewer an `OperationalError`.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): [I] every code block in this section imports `sqlite3`, which Pyodide ships unvendored. `CodePlayground.tsx` `REQUIRED_PACKAGES` has no `sqlite3` entry, so any block pasted into the browser editor would raise `ModuleNotFoundError` (blocks render as static `local-python`). Pyodide's SQLite build supports window functions and `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE` (SQLite ≥3.25 / ≥3.24). Exact `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN` text varies by SQLite version, which the section itself acknowledges. The page's only runnable editor, `demos['mlops']` («Practica model registry y drift (simulado)», MLflow-style registry plus distribution drift), is off-topic and has no `expectedOutput`. Fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with an anti-join / `ROW_NUMBER` playground over a tiny `:memory:` schema, and add `sqlite3: 'sqlite3'` to `REQUIRED_PACKAGES`.

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] The CP-N3-A gate that S30 closes grades a different product from the one S30 teaches. The four CP-N3-A P0s include «Sin separación train/dev/test» and «Sin baseline determinista», and the acceptance tests require «hogar compartido no se fusiona automáticamente», «análisis de falsos positivos presente» and the interface `er.resolve(records) -> ClusterSet` with `fp_analysis` and `baseline`. S30 teaches a train/test/`cross_split` split with no dev set, never builds or names a deterministic baseline, never mentions address or household, and its You Do starter has no `resolve()`. The You Do as written is therefore an automatic P0 failure at the gate. The CP-N3-A BRIEF also lists stale prerequisites: «S27 (async/concurrency), S28 (LLM agents), S29 (MLOps), S30 (security infra)», while the real sections are pytest, data/property tests, advanced SQL and ER. Its `tests/test_demo.py` has 7 `assert True  # placeholder`, yet `execution.json` says «"tests_pass": true». — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-A/BRIEF.md` («Fallos críticos (P0)», «Interfaz»), `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` vs `youDo.starterCode` and `theory[8]` — evidence: «Sin baseline determinista» / «separación train/dev/test respetada» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s30-security-infra.ts`: add a `resolve(records) -> dict(clusters, review_queue, metrics, fp_analysis, baseline)` to the You Do starter. Add a deterministic baseline (exact email match) to theory[6] and a household fixture (same address, different people) to a We Do. Alternatively, rewrite `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-A/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json}` via `_generate_formal_packages.py` to train/test/`cross_split` and generate real asserts. (The gate must grade what the section built, and placeholder tests reported as passing are a tautological check.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] All 24 exam items have `correctIndex` 1. Options are served in stored order (`route.ts` returns `options: JSON.parse(q.options)` unshuffled), and most distractors are jokes, so the exam can be passed by position or by elimination. — where: `S30-security-infra.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «Un embedding de 768 dimensiones» / «Ejecutar DROP SCHEMA» / «Es requerido por ACID» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'security-infra'`): write distractors from the section's real errors (missing treated as disagree, union in the recall numerator, mixed pair counted as test, `sum(sizes)` as cost), and shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (A positional key gives no evidence for a badge.)
- [medium] [O] Several exam items test material that S30 does not teach, or contradict it. [14] asks what «Calibrar scores a probabilidades permite» and keys «Calibración alinea score con P(match)», but the section insists the score «**no es una probabilidad calibrada**» and never calibrates. [11] «Presupuesto de pares por entidad» and [17] «Entrenar pesos solo con pares fáciles» are never taught. In [3], the distractor «Que el nulo se ignora siempre sin peso» is the section's own scorer policy (theory[3]: «Omitir el campo de numerador y denominador es neutral… Esta sección usa la segunda»). — where: `exam[3]`, `exam[11]`, `exam[14]`, `exam[17]` — evidence: «Calibración alinea score con P(match)» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: replace these with items on blocking cost C(n,2), `filter_before_score`, the `cross_split` rule and the missing-omission policy. - [medium] [O] The headline rule «vacío no es desacuerdo» / «dos vacíos no son acuerdo» is broken by the section's own code. In iDo.steps[0], `exact("", "")` returns 1.0 (no missing guard), and `jac` returns `1.0` when both sides are empty. In theory[2], `edit_sim` returns `1.0` for two empty strings and `0.0` for one empty side, which is a disagreement. theory[3] promises «en T3 la verás escrita como `suma(sim·peso) / suma(pesos)` sobre campos observados», but theory[6]'s `pair_score` sums over all `weights` and would crash on a `None`. Only the You Do starter implements the omission. — where: `iDo.steps[0].code`, `theory[2].code` (`edit_sim`), `theory[6].code` — evidence: «return len(ta & tb) / len(ta | tb) if ta | tb else 1.0» vs «return None  # missing–missing: no es acuerdo 1.0» — fix: `s30-security-infra.ts`: return `None` on empty input in `edit_sim` and the iDo comparators, and make `pair_score` skip `None` as the You Do starter does. (Lucía is told to predict outputs from the prose, and the code teaches the opposite.)
- [medium] [O] A We Do edge case states the leakage that the section exists to prevent. weDo.steps[18] lists «par mixto train/test se va a test». **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. Every missing function in the starter (`edit_sim`, `date_sim`, `compare_field`, `entity_split`, `prf`, `error_slices`) exists almost verbatim in theory or iDo, so the engine is assemblable. - Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. The 9 items map one-to-one to T1–T4 (scope of ER, candidate recall, missing, OR/AND blocking, grey band, entity split, score ≠ verdict, filter_before_score, co-cluster completeness), with mostly plausible distractors. One explanation (selfCheck[2]) contradicts theory[3].
- Exam tests the badge claim (`production_python_hardening_practice`, `integrated_production_python_practice`, `progress_phase2_walked`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`, `progress_journey_completed`): no [O]. The two competency badges that require S30 claim «OWASP Top 10… OpenTelemetry tracing, Prometheus metrics… deploy to a cloud platform» because of the stale slug `security-infra`. S30 teaches entity resolution, and not one exam item touches security or observability. The exam is also positional (key always 1). Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S30 from both hardening badges and attach it to a data-quality or ER competency whose `public_claim` says «resolución de entidades con blocking medido y evaluación sin leakage».
- Value: fulfilled for content, shortchanged on assessment. ER with measured blocking, a missing-aware scorer, a clerical queue and entity-level evaluation is exactly what a junior data analyst in a bank or telecom in Lima would meet, and the ethics framing is clear and repeated without preaching. The positional exam and a gate that grades a different engine undercut that. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: good bones. A README with recall, P/R/F1, thresholds and a sample review item is required, and 3 pytest tests are suggested. It would read well if she adds a seeded synthetic gold generator (50–200 records, with accents, swapped name order and missing phones) and a `results.md`. As specified, the data section is the weak point.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All lesson code is stdlib (`collections`, `datetime`, `typing`), with no pandas or numpy reprs and no network or files. The CP-N3-A `demo.py` is also stdlib.

**Blockers**
None found.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The section's own canonical schema is contradicted by most of its code and by the exam. theory[1] fixes «`transfer` va de **cuenta a cuenta**, nunca de una persona a otra», and theory[2]'s code comment warns «colgar la transferencia de la persona borra ese salto». But theory[3].code, theory[5].code, iDo.steps[3].code and theory[9].code all model `transfer` between entities `E1→E2`, and theory[2] defines `E1` as `"kind": "entity"`. The exam keys «Una transacción sintética E1→E2 con monto PEN se modela típicamente como… Arista dirigida». — where: `theory[1].paragraphs[2]` vs `theory[3].code`, `theory[5].code`, `theory[9].code`, `iDo.steps[3].code`, `exam[8]` — evidence: «{"src": "E1", "dst": "E2", "etype": "transfer", "amount": 100.0» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s31-streaming-data.ts`: rename the endpoints in those snippets to account ids (`C1→C2` / `acc-1→acc-2`), and `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'streaming-data'`): reword exam[8] to «cuenta→cuenta». (She will copy the code, not the paragraph, into her You Do.)
- [medium] [O] Degree is defined one way and computed another. The paragraph insists that degree counts edges, not distinct neighbours, precisely because this section uses multigraphs. The code then computes `len(adj[n])` over a `set` of neighbours, which is the count of distinct neighbours. iDo.steps[5] and the We Do count edges, which is correct. — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[6].code` (`degree`) — evidence: «número de **aristas** que tocan un nodo, no de vecinos distintos… diez transferencias entre las mismas dos entidades dan grado 10 y un solo vecino» vs «def degree(n):\n    return len(adj[n])» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: build degree with a `Counter` over the edge list, as iDo.steps[5] does, and add a repeated edge to the fixture so the two definitions print different numbers. (Untested claim: the fixture hides the difference.)
- [medium] [O] The path contract demands evidence per hop, but every path function returns nodes only. theory[6] says a reproducible path lists nodes «y también las aristas que los unen, cada una con su `record_id`» and that «Un path que solo lista nodos dice que existe una conexión sin decir por qué». `bfs_path` in theory[6], iDo.steps[4], weDo.steps[14] and the You Do starter all return a list of node ids. — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[2]` vs `theory[6].code`, `youDo.starterCode` (`bfs_path`) — evidence: «el identificador citable ya viaja en el lab» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: have `bfs_path` return `(nodes, [record_id per hop])` from an edge store keyed by undirected pair. (The governing question «qué respalda cada paso» is never computed.)
- [medium] [O] weDo.steps[9] teaches collapsing raw edges into a `set` of tuples, which drops every `record_id`. That is exactly the loss theory[3] and theory[5] forbid. Its feedback presents the collapse as correct. — where: `weDo.steps[9].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[9].feedback` — evidence: «ce = sorted({(canon[a], canon[b]) for a, b in edges})» / «El set colapsa las dos aristas raw a una canónica E1–E2» vs theory[3] «**No** colapses a una sola arista sin guardar el detalle fuente» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: give the fixture `record_id`s and make the solution rewrite endpoints while keeping one detail row per raw edge (or aggregate with `records`). (Two lessons in a row contradict each other.)
- [medium] [I] The You Do's showcase path cannot be explained with the We Do's lookup. weDo.steps[22] looks evidence up as `ev[(a, b)]` in path order. The You Do path `E1 → ph:900 → E2` walks the second hop against its stored direction (`has_phone` is stored `E2 → ph:900`), so the taught pattern raises `KeyError`. Nothing tells her to look up both orientations of an undirected traversal. — where: `weDo.steps[22].solutionCode`, `youDo.context`, `theory[4].paragraphs[1]` — evidence: «records = [ev[(a, b)] for a, b in zip(path, path[1:])]» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: key the evidence store by `frozenset((a, b))` (or try both tuples) in weDo.steps[22], and add a fixture hop that runs against the stored direction. (H9: she will meet a bare `KeyError` in her portfolio piece.)
- [medium] [I] S31 is labelled the start of CP-N3-B, but it omits that capstone's P0s and acceptance tests. CP-N3-B requires «Sin distinción enlace directo/inferido» (P0), «filtro por autorización» and an `inferred (bool), uncertainty` field. S31's schema has no `inferred` flag even though it calls `shared_phone` a «atajo derivado», and no uncertainty or authorization filter. Several pointers say «workbench de S34», but S34 is «Métricas, desbalance, calibración y umbrales». — where: `theory[1].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[7].paragraphs[3]`, `youDo.portfolioNote` vs `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-B/BRIEF.md` — evidence: «el puente natural hacia el workbench de S34» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: add `inferred: bool` (True for `shared_*`) and `uncertainty` to the edge schema and You Do requirements, and say «el workbench del capstone CP-N3-B (se entrega en S34)». (She would look in S34 and find calibration.)
- [low] [O] Success lines are printed as literals, so the check they claim to make is tautological. The section's own We Do says «No hardcodees». — where: `weDo.steps[9]` («collapsed», True), `weDo.steps[10]` («detail_kept», True), `weDo.steps[13]` («ok», True), `weDo.steps[17]` («interpret_with_types», True), `weDo.steps[22]` («explainable», True), `theory[7].code` («not_guilt», True) — evidence: «print("detail_kept", True)» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: compute each one (e.g. `len(detail) == sum(len(v["records"]) …)`, `all(ev.get(hop) for hop in hops)`). (A green line that cannot be red is worse than no line.)
- [low] [O] The exam keys vary in position, but most distractors are absurd, so each item is answerable by elimination. — where: `exam[4]`, `exam[18]`, `exam[12]` — evidence: «El password del analista» / «Todo el internet» / «El AUC del modelo» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: use the section's real errors as distractors (degree as distinct neighbours, `tuple(sorted(e))` on a transfer, aggregate without records, a k=2 radius including D). (The exam should discriminate.)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] `deque` and `popleft` are never explained (0 hits in S01–S30; S31 prose mentions `deque` only inside weDo.steps[14]'s instruction and hints). The transfer exercise asks her to write BFS with it from scratch. «BFS» is glossed, but why a queue gives the shortest path is not. — where: `theory[6].code`, `weDo.steps[14].instruction` — evidence: «BFS con `deque` desde A; `seen` evita revisitas» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: add two sentences to theory[6]: «`deque` es una cola: `append` mete por la derecha y `popleft` saca por la izquierda; así se procesa primero todo lo que está a 1 salto, luego a 2». (Q2: needed knowledge not on the page.)
- [low] [O] Small unglossed items: «SNAP» (`theory[9].paragraphs[0]`, 0 hits earlier), `Counter.most_common` (0 hits, given only in hints), «out-strength» (introduced in `iDo.steps[0].why` before any definition), and «store» in `theory[2].paragraphs[1]`. — where: listed — evidence: «A escala tipo SNAP (miles o millones de nodos)» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: gloss each at first use. (English jargon friction.)
- [low] [O] The privacy demo labels the two entity nodes with the shared phone number and uses `etype "shared_phone"`, although theory[4] just made the canonical model `has_phone` to a phone node. — where: `iDo.steps[7].code` — evidence: «print("labels", [redact_phone("+51900000001"), "shared", redact_phone("+51900000001")])» — fix: `s31-streaming-data.ts`: label `E1`/`E2` with redacted names or emails and the middle node `ph:+51****01`, with evidence `has_phone` per hop. (She cannot tell which node is the phone.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. Builder, aggregation, ego-k, redaction and BFS are each drilled, and the starter gives `bfs_path` and `add_undirected`. But «records por hop» needs an edge store keyed for undirected traversal, which was never taught (see the `KeyError` issue). Theory itself contradicts the schema on transfers and degree, and the multigraph-plus-time requirement has no worked builder that fails on a missing `record_id` («falla si falta record_id» in the starter docstring only).
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. The 10 items cover centrality ≠ guilt, provenance, aggregate + detail, shared contact, path as hypothesis, multigraph, hop limit, the transfer schema, ego k=1 and weight units, all with plausible distractors. selfCheck[7] even reinforces the account→account rule that the code breaks.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`reliable_async_python_development`, `progress_phase2_walked`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`, `progress_journey_completed`): no [O]. The only competency badge requiring S31 claims «You can write async Python (using `async`/`await` and the `asyncio` library)… orchestrate LLM agents… process streaming data», because of the stale slug `streaming-data`. S31 has 0 `async`/`await` hits and teaches evidence graphs, and no exam item touches async or streaming. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: drop S31 from `reliable_async_python_development` and map it to a graph/relational-evidence competency.
- Value: mixed. The framing is unusually mature for a beginner track: contact as a node, «dos es una señal, cuarenta es una centralita», centrality ≠ guilt, and redaction by default. It would serve her well in compliance or BPO analytics. The code contradicting its own schema twice, and a path that never carries the evidence it promises, weaken the core lesson. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: promising. It requires a `graph.json`, 4 invariant tests, a redacted path view and a Spanish README with a render/summarize policy. A rendered PNG or HTML of a redacted ego-subgraph, which nothing asks for, would make it portfolio-visible.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All snippets use stdlib (`collections`). The `networkx` lines in iDo.steps[4] are commented out (Pyodide ships networkx anyway). The You Do's `list[str] | None` annotation is valid on Pyodide's Python 3.12.

#### S32 — Feature engineering y pipelines sin leakage

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica health checks y circuit breaker» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Data: `data/walk/S32.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S32-microservices.json` (tagline, learningOutcomes, jobRelevance, theory[0–10], iDo.steps[0–7], weDo.steps[0–23] incl. starter/solution/feedback, youDo incl. starterCode and rubric, selfCheck[0–9], resources) and all 24 items of `S32-microservices.exam.json`. Also read: badges in `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` listing S32, `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (option order), and the S33 title («ML supervisado y baselines responsables»). Checked earlier sections: grepped S01–S31 `.json` for `AUC` (0 hits in learner content; only an S31 exam distractor), `LogisticRegression|.fit(|train_test_split|RandomForest` (0), `sklearn` (S13, S14, both saying «no uses sklearn»), `ColumnTransformer` (0), `joblib` (0), `one-hot` (0), `i.i.d` (0), `z-score` (S16, as an outlier rule), `desviación estándar` (S18), `mediana` (S14+), `leakage` (S17, S19, S30), `skew` (S22, S24), `pickle` (S11 exam only), `Red Andina` (S29).

**Blockers**
- [blocker] [O] The You Do acceptance checks cannot pass with a correct implementation. With `decision_t = 5` and `window_w = 3`, the half-open window is `[2, 5)`. E1's events are at `ts` 1 and 2, so a correct `window_count` returns **1**, but the starter states «esperado: 2 (ts 1 y 2)». `time_group_split(rows, decision_t)` puts E2's `ts 3` row in train and its `ts 5` row in test, so overlap is **1**, but the acceptance asserts `ov == 0`, and nothing taught her how to isolate entities, only how to measure overlap. `youDo.requirements[4]` makes these checks mandatory. A diligent learner implements the theory exactly and sees red. She will either conclude she misunderstood half-open, or hard-code the numbers, which is the habit the section forbids. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`__main__` block and acceptance comments), `youDo.requirements[4]` — evidence: «n_e1 = window_count(e1, decision_t, window_w)  # esperado: 2 (ts 1 y 2)» / «# assert n_e1 == 2 and ov == 0 and leaky == []» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s32-microservices.ts`: either set `window_w = 4` (so `[1, 5)` contains ts 1 and 2), or change the expectation to 1 with the comment «ts 1 queda fuera: 1 < t−w». For the split, either use a fixture whose entities do not straddle the cut, or teach and require group isolation (assign each entity to the side of its first event) and assert `ov == 0` after that. (The acceptance test is wrong, not the learner.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] 23 of the 24 exam keys are index 1 (only item [2] is 0). Options are served unshuffled (`route.ts`: `options: JSON.parse(q.options)`), and distractors are absurd. Several items also test scikit-learn API details that the section deliberately avoided: «StandardScaler», «handle_unknown='ignore'», «get_params si va en GridSearch», «joblib/pickle controlado», «i.i.d.», «walk-forward», «target con cuidado». — where: `S32-microservices.exam.json[0..23]`, items 4, 5, 13, 16, 19, 20 — evidence: «La marca del teclado» / «fit/transform (y get_params si va en GridSearch) de forma sklearn-compatible» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'microservices'`): rewrite items from the section's fixtures (half-open count for `[1,2,3,5]`, t=5, w=3 → 2; REQUEST vs REJECT on a missing median; overlap for a straddling entity), and shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (The exam is passable by position and measures an API she never saw.)
- [medium] [O] The section contradicts itself on what counts as leakage. theory[4] says re-estimating μ/σ in serve «es leakage o skew», and that the μ=2 difference «es exactamente la fuga que la sección persigue». theory[8] says the opposite: «no es leakage… aplica una transformación distinta». theory[2] separates «contaminación del split» from leakage («pariente pero no es lo mismo»), yet theory[9] and the key of selfCheck[2] call entity overlap «leakage de identidad». — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[4].paragraphs[2]` vs `theory[8].paragraphs[0]`; `theory[2].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[9].paragraphs[0]`, `selfCheck.questions[2]` — evidence: «Si los recalcularas aquí obtendrías μ=2, y esa diferencia es exactamente la fuga» vs «conviene llamarlo por su nombre, porque no es leakage» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: keep the theory[2]/theory[8] taxonomy and rewrite theory[4] as «reestimar en serve es *skew*; calcularlos con train+test es *fuga*». Relabel entity overlap as «contaminación del split» in theory[9] and selfCheck[2]. (A dictionary section that breaks its own dictionary.)
- [medium] [O] The pipeline demo does the silent fill that theory[4] has just banned, and prints the flag the iDo says must never be a literal. `column_router` fills numeric `None` with 0 and no missing indicator, after theory[4] said «Con 0… escondes la ausencia… Ninguna de las dos es aceptable sin indicador». theory[7] ends with `print("fitted", True)`, while iDo.steps[4] says «Estado fitted se demuestra con fit real… no con un flag `fitted=True` prebakeado». — where: `theory[7].code`, `iDo.steps[4].code` (`column_router`), `iDo.steps[4].retrospective` — evidence: «out[c] = [(fill if v is None else v) * num_state["scale"] for v in batch[c]]» with «{"fill": 0, "scale": 2}» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: have the router emit `amount_missing` alongside the filled value using the train median, and print `imp.mode is not None` instead of `True`. (She learns from the code she runs.)
- [medium] [O] Most We Do «adverse» cases are a boolean flag in the record, not leakage detected from data. This contradicts the intro's promise. In weDo.steps[10]/[11] the invalid record has the same `events`, `t` and `w` as the valid one; only `"includes_t": True` differs, and the recompute is clean. weDo.steps[7]/[8] reject on `uses_label` and weDo.steps[13]/[14] on `try_before_fit`. Several «ok» conditions are always true: `shared in (0, 1) and degree >= 0 and path >= 0`, and `count >= 0 … and t is not None`. Eighteen of the 24 exercises are the same `assess`/`decide` → PASS/REJECT/MISSING|REQUEST template. — where: `weDo.intro`, `weDo.steps[7,8,10,11,13,14].solutionCode` — evidence: «no inviertes un booleano precomputado» vs «invalid = {…, "events": [1, 2, 3, 5], "t": 5, "w": 3, "includes_t": True}» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: make each adverse fixture leak in its data (an event at `ts == t`, a column literally named `decision_final` in the row, `transform` called before `fit` and caught), and delete the always-true conjuncts. (A tautological check gives a green result without evidence, and repetition replaces practice.)
- [low] [O] Two parallel vocabularies, plus an overstated interview claim. E2 exercises return `MISSING:<key>` and E3 exercises return `REQUEST_<X>` for the identical situation (key absent). theory[2] defines only `REQUEST_*`/`REJECT_*` and claims «Este vocabulario es relevante en entrevistas», but these codes are PyArcana conventions, not industry terms. — where: `weDo.steps[1,4,7,…].solutionCode` vs `weDo.steps[2,5,8,…]`, `theory[2].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «Este vocabulario es relevante en entrevistas y volverás a usarlo» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: use one code family, and say «fail-closed / pedir vs. rechazar es la idea relevante en entrevistas; los nombres `REQUEST_*` son del curso». (She may repeat course jargon to an interviewer.)
- [low] [O] The rubric marks the section's pass condition as a bonus. — where: `youDo.rubric[6]` vs `theory[1].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «fs-vN + ventana half-open + zero entity overlap + traspaso a S33» weight «bonus» vs «Si quedan timestamps futuros en las features, la sección no se considera superada» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: make it a gate criterion. (Grading must match the stated closure criterion.)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] S32 is a feature engineering for ML section that assumes ML she has not met. No earlier section trains a model (0 hits for `.fit(`, `LogisticRegression` or `train_test_split` in S01–S31; S13/S14 explicitly say «no uses sklearn»), and supervised ML arrives only in S33. Yet theory[0] opens with «El AUC era excelente», with AUC unexplained (0 learner-content hits before S32). The section leans throughout on «entrenar», «inferencia», «serve», «target», «modelo», «baseline» and «fit», and offers sklearn `Pipeline`/`ColumnTransformer` (0 hits) and «joblib o pickle» as analogies to tools she has never used. — where: `theory[0].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[7].paragraphs[0–2]`, `theory[8].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «El AUC era excelente. Se despliega, y el score colapsa.» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: add a 5-line «qué es entrenar y servir un modelo» box to theory[2]'s dictionary («fit = aprender parámetros de datos pasados; serve/inferencia = aplicar a un caso nuevo; AUC = qué tan bien ordena el score a positivos sobre negativos, 0.5 = azar»), and mark the sklearn analogies «lo verás en S33». Or move S32 after S33. (Q1 and Q2: she cannot know what effect is being protected.)
- [low] [O] The worked numbers in theory[4] look like an error. The train median is 2, yet the frozen train stats are «μ=0 y σ=2» for a series whose values are 1 to 3. Lucía will suspect she misread the mean. — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[2]`, `iDo.steps[1]` — evidence: «z aplicando los μ=0 y σ=2 **congelados en el fit de train**» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: use plausible train stats (e.g. μ=2.5, σ=1.0) and print the train series they came from. (Implausible givens break predict-then-check.)
- [low] [O] The tagline leaks an authoring note. — where: `tagline` — evidence: «Ritmo sugerido: unas 9 h para la ruta completa de esta sección según la calibración actual.» — fix: `s32-microservices.ts`: drop «según la calibración actual» (`estimatedHours` already carries it). (Learner-facing text should not reference internal calibration.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: no [O]. Each function stub (`window_count`, `fit_median`, `graph_feats`, `time_group_split`, `leak_scan`, `skew_alert`) is taught and nearly copyable, but the mandatory acceptance values (`n_e1 == 2`, `ov == 0`) contradict a correct half-open window and a correct time split on the starter's own data. The JSON `fs-vN` artifact with a «schema hash», requested in `youDo.context` and `portfolioNote`, is never demonstrated.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. The 10 items cover half-open windows, transform-before-fit, entity overlap, leaky names, train-only μ/σ, unknown keys, missing indicators, skew, version bump and REQUEST vs REJECT. Their framing matches the section, though selfCheck[2] reuses the «leakage de identidad» label that theory[2] rejects.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`production_python_hardening_practice`, `integrated_production_python_practice`, `progress_phase2_walked`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`, `progress_journey_completed`): no [O]. The two competency badges requiring S32 claim OWASP, observability, cloud deploy and «containerize it (Docker)», because of the stale slug `microservices`. S32 teaches feature leakage, and the exam is positional (23/24 at index 1) and partly about sklearn APIs that were not taught. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: remove S32 from the hardening badges and attach it to an ML-data-preparation competency whose claim is «construye features sin fuga temporal ni de etiqueta, con estado de train reutilizado en inferencia».
- Value: shortchanged. Leakage, half-open windows and train≡serve are the ideas most likely to come up in a junior data interview, and theory[0] tells the story well. But Lucía meets them before she has trained a single model. She practises them as 18 near-identical flag-routing exercises, and her You Do fails its own acceptance checks. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is a 4-event inline list with no data file, no tests beyond commented asserts, no feature table output (CSV or parquet) and no README requirement. A reviewer would see stubs and a JSON with one median.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All snippets use stdlib (`json`, classes, comprehensions). sklearn is named but never imported.

#### S33 — ML supervisado y baselines responsables

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] The You Do is labelled as CP-N3-B work, but the CP-N3-B capstone does not grade anything this section builds. Lucía will think her baseline project counts toward the capstone. CP-N3-B is «Mesa de Trabajo de Investigación de Relaciones» with interface `graph.investigate(query) -> GraphCase`. Its acceptance criteria are «búsqueda de camino → explicación», «profundidad limitada», «cada enlace responde 6 preguntas», «filtro por autorización» and «mismo caso → mismo grafo», and its P0 list includes «Sin distinción enlace directo/inferido». No baseline, dummy, beats_dummy, group CV or cost appears in `BRIEF.md`, `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` or `gate.json.acceptance_criteria`. Separately, `tests/test_demo.py` tests 0–5 are all `assert True  # placeholder`, so the capstone's own tests cannot tell her whether she passed. — where: `youDo.title`, `iDo.steps[0].preamble`, `weDo.intro` vs `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-B/BRIEF.md` («Criterios de aceptación») — evidence: «Baseline vs. modelo: framing + tracking (CP-N3-B)»; «Practicamos baselines responsables del workbench CP-N3-B» — fix: `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-B/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json,gate.json}`: add a criterion «modelo de priorización con dual baseline (dummy + regla), beats_* logueados y group CV por entidad» that consumes the S33 You Do. Also replace the `assert True` placeholders in `tests/test_demo.py` with real checks. Or, in `src/lib/course/sections/s33-advanced-models.ts`, drop the «(CP-N3-B)» label. (A gate must grade what the contributing sections taught. Placeholder tests turn an unverified claim into a green check.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do's "group CV" is computed from hard-coded numbers that have nothing to do with her model, and her own rubric forbids hardcoding. `fold_scores = [0.6, 0.7, 0.65]` and `train_ents, valid_ents = ["e1"], ["e2", "e3"]` are constants. `mean_fold(fold_scores)` and `disjoint` therefore always print the same thing, whatever thr or model she picks. The stump is also "trained" and scored on the same four rows, which is exactly the «Elegir profundidad solo mirando train» error that `theory[8].paragraphs[0]` calls «el error clásico». — where: `youDo.starterCode`, `youDo.rubric[1]`, `youDo.objectives[3]` — evidence: «Correctitud técnica: dummy, regla, modelo, costo y métricas calculados (no hardcode)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s33-advanced-models.ts`: give each row an entity, build folds with `train_ents`/`valid_ents` derived from the entity list, choose thr on train rows only and score on the valid fold, and compute `fold_scores` from those valid accuracies. (Otherwise the "group CV with disjunction" she puts on GitHub is decorative, and the leakage-prevention competency the badge claims is not exercised.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do's rule baseline is a strawman, so `beats_rule` is meaningless. `rule_preds(x, thr=1.0)` was copied from the theory, where x was 0/1. In the You Do `x` ranges from 0.05 to 0.4, so the rule never fires, and `rule_acc` equals the all-zero dummy (0.5). This breaks the section's own warning that «Ganar solo al dummy no basta si una regla simple ya es mejor». — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`r_pred = rule_preds(x, thr=1.0)`) — evidence: «x = [0.1, 0.4, 0.2, 0.05]  # score sintético al estilo S32» — fix: `s33-advanced-models.ts`: set the rule threshold to a domain-plausible value (e.g. 0.3) or ask her to justify it in the README. (A baseline that cannot fire cannot be "the best simple baseline".)
- [medium] [I] The tie-breaking rule is taught as a general property of `max`, but it is an accident of set ordering. The You Do labels `y=[1,1,0,0]` are exactly a tie, so this matters. `max(set(y), key=y.count)` returns the first maximal element in set-iteration order. For `{0, 1}` that happens to be 0, but it is not "the smaller class" in general: `y=[1,8,1,8]` returns 8 under CPython 3.12, and for strings the result depends on hash randomisation. — where: `theory[3].code.code` comment, `theory[3].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «max elige el menor valor de clase»; «con conteos iguales usa la clase **menor**» — fix: `s33-advanced-models.ts`: implement the stated policy explicitly (`maj = min(c for c in set(y) if y.count(c) == max(map(y.count, set(y))))`) or remove the claim. (The section asks her to state a fixed tie policy, so the code must actually implement it.)
- [medium] [O] Two We Do gates hard-code the fixture's expected value inside the "policy". Their own retrospectives say that is the mistake. T1-A-E2's solution requires `record["prevalence"] == 0.25` inside `assess`, while its retrospective says «Hardcodear 0.25 … esconde un pipeline que no recalcula desbalance en el siguiente lote». A wrong prevalence is also routed to `REJECT_FRAUD_TARGET`, which is the wrong code. T4-B-E2 does the same with `record["mean"] == 0.65`, rejecting as `REJECT_RANDOM_LEAK`. — where: `weDo.steps[1].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[1].retrospective`, `weDo.steps[22].solutionCode` — evidence: «and record["prevalence"] == 0.25» — fix: `s33-advanced-models.ts`: check `0 < prevalence < 1` (and that it was recomputed from `y`) and `0 <= mean <= 1`, and route a bad value to its own code. (Lucía copies this pattern into the You Do, and it teaches that a gate = an expected-output check.)
- [medium] [O] The exam is generic, guessable and partly untaught. 20 of 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and most distractors are absurd («El color del informe», «El número de GPUs», «Calibrar certificados TLS», «Solo el meme del equipo»), so "always pick B" passes. Several items test material the section never teaches: colinearity (`[8]`, `[9]`), Random Forest bagging/bootstrap (`[13]`), boosting on residuals with learning rate (`[14]`), «Brier perfectamente calibrado» and «early stop» (`[15]`, `[17]`), and «id de datos/versión» (`[19]`). The section's distinctive content (rolling origin and seasonal baseline, a losing run still being valid, prevalence before the fit, `C` as the inverse of λ) is never examined. — where: `S33-advanced-models.exam.json` `[0]–[23]` — evidence: «Boosting (p. ej. gradient boosting) construye modelos: … Secuencialmente enfocándose en errores residuales» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: rebalance correctIndex across 0–3, replace joke distractors with plausible misconceptions taken from the We Do DEFECTs, and swap the untaught RF/boosting/colinearity items for ones on beats_dummy=False validity, rolling-origin `max(train) < min(valid)`, and seasonal vs. "ayer" baselines. (Biggs: assessment must match the outcomes. A positional exam measures nothing.)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] "Supervised ML" is promised, but no model is ever fitted. The logistic regression's `w=1.0, b=0.0` are given, L2 is a string in a dict (`params = {"penalty": "l2", "C": 1.0}`) that no code uses, and sklearn is never imported (grep: S13/S14/S32 mention it, but no section fits a classifier). - [low] [O] The prose and the printed output disagree. The paragraph explains σ(0.2)≈0.55, but the code prints `round(sigmoid(2), 3)` → `0.881`, a number the text never mentions. - [medium] [O] A long PyTorch/TensorFlow history sits inside the "tracking mínimo" block, in unglossed English, with no exercise or check. Paragraphs 3–6 cover Meta, «*eager execution* frente a *graph execution*», «Papers with Code», `torch.nn.Linear`, `tf.keras.layers.Dense` and Tesla. None of it is practiced, it is not optional, and it interrupts the run-log topic. `theory[6]` also points to it as «el bloque de PyTorch y TensorFlow que cierra la sección», but it is not the closing block (`theory[11]` is). — where: `theory[9].paragraphs[3]–[6]`, `theory[6].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «Esta diferencia filosófica — *eager execution* frente a *graph execution* —» — fix: `s33-advanced-models.ts`: move these paragraphs into an `optional: true` block after `theory[6]`, gloss the English terms, and fix the cross-reference. - [low] [O] The English term «breach» is used as a technical label from `theory[2]` onward without a gloss, and grep finds it in no earlier section. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[0]`, `iDo.steps[0].why` — evidence: «un target llamado `fraud` o `is_fraud` es un **breach de producto**» — fix: `s33-advanced-models.ts`: «una **violación** (*breach*) del contrato de producto» at first use.

- [low] [O] The You Do objective on temporal splitting can be read two ways. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. Framing, dummy+cost, stump thr and the run log are all practiced. "Entrena un stump" means picking thr by hand on the 4 evaluation rows (no stump-fitting procedure is taught until S34's threshold search), and "group CV" is satisfied by hard-coded fold scores, so what she can submit does not demonstrate what the objectives claim.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O]. All 9 items map to taught blocks (target, dual baseline, scaled coefs, group CV, a losing run is valid, L2 as config, best baseline, time split, seasonal baseline). `selfCheck.questions[2]` uses «SHAP» only as a distractor, and S35 does cover it (5 hits).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_deep_learning_practice`; also progress markers `progress_phase2_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, and `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): no [O]. The badge's `newbie_friendly_description` claims «You can train deep neural networks (… CNNs … transformers, using PyTorch or TensorFlow)». The S33 exam has 0 deep-learning items, and the section's only DL content is an optional NumPy XOR toy. Its critical competencies are only partly examined: reproducibility in `[16]`/`[19]`, leakage only generically in `[21]`.
- Value: mixed. The baseline-first habit, cost framing, "a losing run is still evidence" and the rolling-origin block are genuinely job-relevant for a junior analyst. But 10 estimated hours yield no fitted model, no sklearn, and toy lists of 3–4 rows. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is a single script with 4 hard-coded rows, no data file, no tests scaffolded (rubric[3] asks for «Pruebas o casos de borde» but gives no harness), and the README is required but has no template.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): low. `theory[6].code` (`red_diminuta.py`) uses `np.random.default_rng(33).normal`, and its printed loss `0.001`/`0.6931` depends on the PCG64 normal stream being the same in numpy 1.26 and 2.2 [I: expected to match, but unverified in Pyodide]. It prints via `float()`/`.tolist()`, so no numpy-2 `np.float64(...)` reprs appear. Every other snippet is stdlib only.

Checked earlier sections (S01–S33 `.json`, exam files excluded) by grepping for: `ROC` (0 hits), `Brier`/`Platt`/`isotonic`/`SMOTE`/`estratific`/`class_weight` (0), `headcount`/`training-serving` (0), `recall` (S13, S24, S25, S30), `F1` (S11, S24, S25, S30), `prevalencia` (S30, S33), `matriz de confusión` (0), `entity resolution` (S13, S27–S31), `fail-closed` (many), `BFS`/`shortest_path`/`max_depth` (S31), `who_may_see`/`not_meaning`/`inferido`/`inferred`/`directo` (0 in S31–S34), `autorizaci` (S22; S31 has one sentence «records solo al revisor autorizado»).

**Blockers**
- [blocker] [O] S34 is the gate for CP-N3-B, but the capstone's critical requirements are taught in no contributing section (S31–S34), and S34 itself contributes nothing the gate checks. `GATE_MAP` maps `S34:'CP-N3-B'`. The capstone requires `graph.investigate(query) -> GraphCase` where every edge carries «source/meaning/not_meaning/age/who_may_see/correction». Its P0s include «Sin distinción enlace directo/inferido» and «Sin control de autorización». Grep for `who_may_see`, `not_meaning`, `inferido`/`inferred`/`directo` across S31–S34 finds nothing. Authorization appears only as one sentence in S31 and in general form in S22. Meanwhile, nothing S34 teaches appears in `BRIEF.md` or `RUBRIC.json`: no thresholds, calibration, abstention or precision@k. The capstone's own `tests/test_demo.py` tests 0–5 are `assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check`, and `STARTER/cp_n3_b.py` is a bare `raise NotImplementedError`. Lucía reaches the gate after S34 with no instruction for 2–3 of the 4 P0 criteria and no working tests to tell her whether she passed. — where: `weDo.intro`, `theory[0].callout`, `youDo.title` vs `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-B/BRIEF.md` («Criterios de aceptación», «Fallos críticos (P0)»), `tests/test_demo.py` — evidence: «Ahora te toca operar el Relationship Investigation Workbench sobre `CASO-LIM-034` … y cerrar **CP-N3-B**»; capstone: «cada enlace responde 6 preguntas», «Sin distinción enlace directo/inferido» — fix: `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-B/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json,gate.json,tests/test_demo.py}`: add S33/S34 deliverables as acceptance criteria («cola con thr-vN por costo/capacidad, Brier/reliability y abstain»), and replace placeholder tests with real ones. Then, either teach direct/inferred edges, the six edge questions and per-viewer filtering (one We Do block in `src/lib/course/sections/s31-*.ts` or S34), or drop them from the P0 list. (A non-compensatory gate cannot demand untaught P0s, and placeholder tests give a false green.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do selects the threshold, reports the confusion matrix and computes Brier, all on the same five points, with no holdout and no calibrator. That contradicts the section's central rule. `theory[6]` says «la medición va sobre datos no usados para el fit», and `theory[7]` says the thr «se elige sobre scores "calibrados"» from holdout. Yet `build_workbench_report` calls `choose_thr(scores, labels, …)` and then `confusion_counts(labels, pred)` and `brier_mean(scores, labels)` on the identical `SCORES/LABELS`. The T3-B affine calibrator never appears in the You Do. The T2 outcomes (class weights, resampling in-fold, prevalence) are absent too, so 3 of 8 learning outcomes are never assessed in the project. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`build_workbench_report`), `youDo.objectives[0–3]`, `learningOutcomes[2]`, `[3]`, `[5]` — evidence: «thr, cost = choose_thr(scores, labels, C_FP, C_FN, capacity)» … «"brier": round(brier_mean(scores, labels), 4)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s34-cv-ai-integration.ts`: split CASO-LIM-034 into `holdout_v1` (fit a, b and choose thr) and `test` (report confusion, Brier, precision@k), and add `prevalence` plus `calibrator_set` to the report dict. (As written, the project teaches the in-sample evaluation the theory forbids, and it is what she would show an interviewer.)
- [medium] [O] "Aplicar class weights o resampling" is never actually applied. The code and the We Do reduce to negating a boolean. `apply_rebalance_in_fold` copies train unchanged («aquí iría oversample/weights»), and T2-A-E1's whole task is `resample_train_only = not resample_global`. That is a tautological check: it passes without any rebalancing existing. Stratification («StratifiedKFold») is introduced in `theory[4].paragraphs[3]` and never practiced. — where: `learningOutcomes[2]`, `theory[4].code.code`, `weDo.steps[6]` — evidence: «rebalanced_train = list(train_idx)  # aquí iría oversample/weights» — fix: `s34-cv-ai-integration.ts`: implement a 5-line random oversample of minority train indices with a fixed seed, and assert that `test_idx` is unchanged and that the minority count in train went up. (AGENTS.md: tautological tests are worse than no test.)
- [medium] [O] The Platt contradiction. The theory carefully says the affine map is not Platt, but the code docstring, the I Do and the We Do starter all call it Platt. — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[7].code.code` docstring, `iDo.steps[5].description`, `iDo.steps[5].preamble`, `weDo.steps[15].starterCode` title comment — evidence: «conviene no llamarlo Platt» vs «Mapa afín + clip: implementación simplificada de Platt en holdout» and «(Platt simplificado)» — fix: `s34-cv-ai-integration.ts`: replace every «Platt simplificado» with «calibrador afín recortado (no es Platt)». (The section tells her not to confuse the two, then confuses them.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do report contains two decision policies that disagree, and nothing reconciles them. Lucía's search gives thr-v1 = 0.9 (only one case reviewed, `n_review=1`). `decide()` with the 0.3–0.7 band then sends 0.9 to review but puts 0.55 and 0.6 in `abstain`, and `precision_at_k(ranked, capacity)` reports the top-2. She cannot tell which rule actually fills the queue, or whether abstained cases count against capacity. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`choose_thr`, `decide`, `precision_at_k`), `youDo.objectives[3]` — evidence: «Elegir thr-v1 por costo/capacidad … y aplicar banda de abstención en 0.55» — fix: `s34-cv-ai-integration.ts`: add one theory paragraph and a report field stating the rule, e.g. «review si s ≥ thr-v1; abstain si LOW ≤ s < thr-v1 (va a segundo look, no consume capacity); skip si s < LOW». (Without it the integrated report is internally inconsistent.)
- [low] [O] The same fixture gives two different costs for the same threshold. T4-A-E1 establishes thr 0.6 → cost 0 on `CASO-LIM-034-4A`, while T4-A-E2/E3 use `"thr": 0.6, "cost": 16` for the same case id. — where: `weDo.steps[18].preamble` vs `weDo.steps[19].starterCode`, `weDo.steps[20].starterCode` — evidence: «thr==0.6, cost==0» vs `"cost": 16` — fix: `s34-cv-ai-integration.ts`: use `"cost": 0` in the E2/E3 valid fixtures.
- [medium] [O] The exam can be passed by position, and part of it is untaught. All 24 items have `correctIndex` 0 or 1 (16× B, 8× A), with throwaway distractors («Compilar Python», «La versión de Node», «El GIL de Python», «Latencia p99 vs pods»). Untaught terms are examined: «SMOTE» (`[8]`, 0 hits in S01–S34), «CV anidada» (`[17]`) and «calibración+refinamiento» (`[13]` explanation). There is also a typo, «Mayority» (`[10].explanation`). Section-specific skills are missing: search-under-capacity, the abstain band, AP vs accuracy, and the in-fold resample rule beyond one item. — where: `S34-cv-ai-integration.exam.json` — evidence: «SMOTE u oversampling con leakage de fold provoca» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: spread correctIndex across 0–3, use the We Do DEFECTs as distractors (recall÷k, F1=P+R, thr fixed at 0.5, calibrating on test), replace SMOTE with «oversample», and fix «Mayority» → «Majority». (Assessment must discriminate, and must only test what was taught.)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] ROC / ROC-AUC is never defined, yet two of the eight self-check items depend on contrasting it with PR. Grep finds `ROC` in no earlier section. `theory[2]` mentions it once in passing. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[1]`, `selfCheck.questions[0]`, `selfCheck.questions[6]`, exam `[2]` — evidence: «a diferencia de ROC, que se infla con muchos verdaderos negativos» — fix: `s34-cv-ai-integration.ts`: add two sentences defining the ROC curve (TPR vs FPR across thresholds) and why many TN flatter it, ideally with one number on the 4-point demo. (She cannot reason about an option she was never taught.)
- [low] [O] The figure in the precision@k block describes guards the section never teaches. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes, mechanically [O]. The three DEFECTs (fixed thr 0.5, `"thr_id": "default"`, band → review) and `accuracy_only` each map 1:1 to a We Do repair, and the starter's asserts tell her when she is done. Doing it correctly, though, means evaluating in-sample, without the calibrator or class-balancing outcomes (see Issues).
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. 6 of 8 items match taught blocks (resampling in-fold, calibrator on holdout, abstain, versioned thr, Brier, overload). `[0]` and `[6]` depend on ROC, which was never defined.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_deep_learning_practice`; progress markers `progress_phase2_walked` and `progress_journey_completed`; `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): partly/no [O]. The badge claims «train deep neural networks (… CNNs … transformers, using PyTorch or TensorFlow), quantify their uncertainty (… calibrated)». The S34 exam covers calibration (`[12]–[17]`) and leakage-safe resampling (`[7]`, `[8]`), which are one of its two critical competencies. It contains zero deep-learning items, and neither S33 nor S34 trains a deep network.
- Value: mixed, leaning fulfilled for the content and shortchanged by the integration. The metrics and threshold material (confusion, precision@k vs capacity, prevalence trap, Brier, cost-under-capacity search, abstention) is exactly what a junior analyst gets asked in interviews, and it is computed by hand clearly. But the capstone this section "closes" grades a different product with placeholder tests. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one script over five hard-coded scores, with no data file, no tests beyond inline asserts, and no README template. It would read as an exercise, not a project, unless she adds a holdout split and a short README.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none found. Every snippet is pure stdlib (no numpy, pandas or sklearn), and all printed outputs are Python floats, lists and dicts.

Separately, the capstone badge's description says the Phase 2 capstones cover «system design, and post-mortem practice», while S35 defines CP-N3-C as the case card (`theory[0].callout`: «Inicio CP-N3-C: la ficha distingue las cuatro capas»). — where: `badge_catalog.json` › `architecture_decision_practice.required_sections/required_activities`; `S35-system-design.exam.json` concepts — evidence: «'System design' = deciding how to break a system into services» — fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: drop S35 from `architecture_decision_practice` (keep S40, which teaches architecture/DDD). - [medium] [O] The first learning outcome says she will «Calcular ranking de importancia por permutación», but she never permutes anything. Every exercise (`weDo.steps[0–2]`) only takes `max()` over a hard-coded dict. sklearn was taught in S33, and Pyodide's scikit-learn 1.4.2 includes `sklearn.inspection.permutation_importance`, so the real computation is within reach. — where: `learningOutcomes[0]`, `theory[2].paragraphs[0]`, `weDo.steps[0–2]` — evidence: «Calcular ranking de importancia por permutación (drop de métrica)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s35-system-design.ts`: add one E3 that shuffles a column of a 20-row synthetic fixture with `random.Random(seed).shuffle` and recomputes precision@k. Otherwise reword the outcome to «Ordenar e interpretar drops de permutación ya medidos». (The outcome promises a skill she does not practise.)
- [medium] [O] The slice gate contradicts the theory it implements. Theory says `low_n` alone proves nothing and only a *strong claim* on low n is rejected: «Si la afirmación es fuerte con n bajo → `REJECT_LOW_N_CLAIM`». It also uses AQP n=8 as an honest, documented slice. The E2 solution rejects any slice with `slice_n < min_n` whatever the claim (the fixture has no claim field). The E3 solution's fall-through rejects `low_n` with `claim="report_only"` too. So the section's own AQP example, reported honestly, gets `REJECT_LOW_N_CLAIM`. — where: `weDo.steps[7].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[8].solutionCode` (last `return`) vs `theory[4].paragraphs[2]` — evidence: «if rep["flag"] == "ok_n": return "CONTINUE"\n    return "REJECT_LOW_N_CLAIM"» — fix: `s35-system-design.ts`: route `low_n` + `report_only` to a `CONTINUE_FLAGGED` (or `CONTINUE` with flag) and reject only `claim in {"parity","inequity"}`. Add a claim field to the E2 fixture. (Lucía would learn that small slices must be hidden, which is the opposite of «n visible junto a cada métrica».)
- [medium] [O] Several gates cannot accept a normal case and return a misleading code. The T3-B gates PASS/CONTINUE only when the case *is* OOD. An in-distribution case with `action="score"` gets `REJECT_AUTO_LABEL` in E2, although nothing was auto-labelled, and `REQUEST_OOD_POLICY` in E3. The T2-B gate PASSes only when `risk == "high"`, so a low-risk feature is rejected as `REJECT_PROXY_FEATURE`. — where: `weDo.steps[16].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[17].solutionCode` (final `return "REQUEST_OOD_POLICY"`), `weDo.steps[10].solutionCode` — evidence: «return "PASS" if max(abs(z) for z in record["zs"]) > record["threshold"] and record["action"] == "abstain" else "REJECT_AUTO_LABEL"» — fix: `s35-system-design.ts`: add an in-distribution branch (`not ood and action == "score"` → PASS/CONTINUE) and a low-risk branch (`risk != "high"` → PASS). (A gate that rejects every in-distribution case teaches the wrong policy and the wrong error names.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do auto-check is weaker than its objectives, and the "asserts" are prints. Objective 1 requires «coverage_claim implícito False y, si OOD, action=abstain con reason=ood». `ok_unc` checks only `hi > lo`, `ood` and `action`, never `reason` or the band values. The starter says «Tras corregir, estos asserts deben pasar:» but contains no `assert`, so `portfolio_ready` can print `True` while `reason` stays `None`. — where: `youDo.objectives[1]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`ok_unc`) — evidence: «ok_unc = hi > lo and c["uncertainty"]["ood"] is True and c["uncertainty"]["action"] == "abstain"» — fix: `s35-system-design.ts`: add `c["uncertainty"]["reason"] == "ood"` and `(lo, hi) == (0.5, 0.7)` to `ok_unc`, and turn the four flags into real `assert`s. - [medium] [O] The exam does not discriminate. With one variant per concept and a 70% pass mark (85% for badges), most items can be answered by eliminating absurd distractors. Examples: «Solo el emoji del analista en el chat» (`[22].options[1]`), «El número de GPUs del cluster de training» (`[7].options[2]`), «Un usuario pidió un color nuevo en la UI» (`[23].options[3]`), «Que el modelo publica solo a redes sociales» (`[21].options[0]`). **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O]. The three `fill_*` repairs are the We Do E1 fixes (`v*w`, `p±q`, `abstain`, `queue_rank`, non-empty `by`) almost line for line, so it checks recombination, not independent work.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes, mostly [O]. The 8 questions cover the 4 subtopics; Q6's key does not match its question (above).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`architecture_decision_practice`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`; `progress_phase2_walked` and `progress_journey_completed` are participation markers): no for `architecture_decision_practice`, which has no system-design or trade-off item. Partly for the capstone badge: `uncertainty_quantification` is covered, `system_design` and `tradeoff_articulation` are not.
- Value: mixed. The ethical framing (explaining is not accusing, n next to every metric, abstain on OOD, audited overrides) is real analyst value and well written. But the hands-on part is flipping booleans in dicts: no model, no data rows, no sklearn, even though S33 and S34 taught them. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one script with prints; it has no README (only asked for in the retrospective), no tests, no data file and no demo. A reviewer would see a toy.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All snippets are pure standard library with deterministic outputs.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] For Lucía's main goal, the SQL credentials are gated on a section with no SQL in it. `applied_sql_query_development` (`skill_nodes` «sql_fundamentals», «sql_window_ctes»; `required_activities` «S37-YOUDO», «S37-EXAM») requires S37. So do `integrated_data_analyst_practice` and `integrated_data_science_practice` (both `critical_competencies` include «sql_competency»). S37 is profiling and performance, and its exam's 8 concepts contain no SELECT, JOIN, window function or CTE. The badge's description promises «window functions … and CTEs», which are taught in S29. The other required section, S19, is «Visualización y comunicación accesible». Both look inherited from stale slugs (`dbt-bigquery`, `databases-orm`). As built, she can pass the "SQL" badge's section exams without writing a query. Or, if the badge integrator tests SQL, she will have been sent to the wrong sections to prepare. — where: `badge_catalog.json` › `applied_sql_query_development.required_sections/required_activities`, `integrated_data_analyst_practice.required_sections`, `integrated_data_science_practice.required_sections` — evidence: «You can write SQL … SELECT, JOIN, GROUP BY …, window functions …, and CTEs» — fix: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json`: replace S19/S37 with S12/S29 in `required_sections` and the matching `S29-YOUDO`/`S29-EXAM` activities. Put S37 on `performance_tuning` instead (it is already a node of the capstone badge). (Her goal is a data-analyst job. A SQL badge that never assesses SQL misleads her and employers.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do's core gate, «¿es más rápido, y sigue dando lo mismo?», is tautological and cannot fail. `before_path` and `after_path` both end with `return match_score(values)`, computed over all values whatever their pair loops did, and the loops only assign `_ = …`. `ok_same` is therefore `True` by construction, so the `assert ok_same` can never trigger. Blocking drops about 90% of pairs with no recall check, though objective 1 says «sin abandonar el recall como gate de matching». The section teaches that blocking has a recall cost (`theory[0].paragraphs[3]`) and then hands her a fixture where that cost is invisible. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`before_path`, `after_path`, `ok_same`), `youDo.objectives[1]` — evidence: «for j in range(i + 1, len(block)):\n                _ = block[i] + block[j]\n    return match_score(values)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s37-dbt-bigquery.ts`: have both paths return the *set of matched pairs*, e.g. `(i, j)` with `abs(values[i]-values[j]) <= 2` over shuffled synthetic records with a block key. Compute `same_result = before == after` and `recall = len(after & before) / len(before)`, and include a key choice that loses matches so the gate can go red. (A gate that cannot fail is a tautological test; AGENTS.md calls these worse than no test.)
- [high] [O] The You Do starter is already complete. `bench`, `all_pairs`, `blocked_pairs`, both paths, the budget check and the full `report` dict are written. The only instruction is «rellena dataset/hardware de tu lab», which needs editing two strings. Objective 2 («Aplicar caché/chunks con invalidación o bound de memoria») has no code or stub, and the rubric's «Pruebas o casos de borde documentados» (15%) has no scaffold. — where: `youDo.starterCode`, `youDo.objectives[2]` — evidence: «# Completa el reporte del gate de escala (rellena dataset/hardware de tu lab):» — fix: `s37-dbt-bigquery.ts`: leave `bench`, the blocked path and the cache as `NotImplementedError` stubs. Require a `test_perf.py` with one `same_result` test and one budget test that is shown failing at `blocks=1`, and ask for a cache keyed by `(feature_version, cutoff)`. (The You Do should produce evidence of her own work.)
- [medium] [O] The measurement lessons never show a measurement. Every timing snippet prints a predicate that is always true (`wall_ms >= 0`, `median_ms >= 0`, `peak >= 0`), and the budget exercises use hard-coded «measured» values (`budget, measured = 10, 9`). The section's own rule is «Un número sin `n` no sirve para decidir… solo entonces publicas los ms». Lucía writes measuring code but gets no feedback on whether it measures anything: a broken timer prints the same `True`. — where: `theory[2].code`, `theory[3].code`, `iDo.steps[0–1]`, `weDo.steps[1]`, `weDo.steps[3]` (expected output «True / n_runs 3 / warmup True»), `weDo.steps[18–19]` — evidence: «print(med_ms >= 0)  # predicado estable (ms exactos varían por máquina)» — fix: `s37-dbt-bigquery.ts`: print the real ms for her to read, but check a machine-independent *relation*. Examples: `median(n=20000) > median(n=200)`, or `speedup = before/after > 1` for the O(n²)→blocked pair, or a budget test that feeds the actually measured `after_ms`. (A predicate that is true for any input verifies nothing.)
- [medium] [O] The cache exercise teaches the opposite of its own warning. The preamble says a key without the cutoff *collides*: a false hit that serves stale scores. The starter's defect is the reverse, «key sin cutoff → miss falso», and the "fix" turns a miss into a hit. She practises "make it hit" while the lesson is "a short key hits when it should not". — where: `weDo.steps[15].preamble` vs `weDo.steps[15].starterCode` — evidence: «reutilizar features del triage con solo la versión de feature set colisiona: distinto cutoff, mismos scores viejos» / «# DEFECT: key sin cutoff → miss falso» — fix: `s37-dbt-bigquery.ts`: store under `("fs-v1",)` computed for cutoff `2026-01`, look up for cutoff `2026-02`, and show the stale hit. The fix adds the cutoff to the key and gets the correct miss. (The exercise must demonstrate the risk it names.)
- [medium] [O] The self-check has only 5 questions (other sections have 8) and leaves half the outcomes unassessed. (RSS)» and «¿qué debe llevar la clave de caché? (versión + cutoff)». (Biggs alignment: outcomes need assessment.)
- [low] [O] The warmup exercise can pass without a warmup. `discard_first` and `warmup` are printed literals and `ok` is `len(times) == 3`, so changing only the two prints yields the expected output. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes, trivially [O]. The starter is complete and only two strings need filling. The objectives on cache/chunks and recall are not exercised.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Its 5 questions cover warmup, blocking, budget, micro-optimisation and `n`; profiling tools, memory/dtypes and cache invalidation are untested.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_sql_query_development`, `integrated_data_analyst_practice`, `integrated_data_science_practice`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`; plus participation markers): no for all three SQL-bearing badges, which have zero SQL items. Partly for the capstone badge's `performance_tuning` node: profiling, blocking cost and budgets are covered, by an exam passable by elimination.
- Value: mixed. The ideas are the right professional habits: wall vs. CPU, `tracemalloc` vs. RSS, warmup plus median, counting pairs, budgets that can fail, same-result before speedup. They are explained clearly, and the theory is better than average. But the practice prints `True` instead of numbers, and the capstone check cannot fail, so she never feels a real speed-up or a real regression. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is a pre-written script plus a dict, with no README, no tests, no data file and no chart of before/after times.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): [I] Browser timers are coarse: `performance.now` precision is reduced in browsers, and in WASM `time.process_time` resolution is implementation-defined. Small works (`sum(range(1000))` in `weDo.steps[0]`, `range(2000)` in `weDo.steps[3–4]`) may measure 0.0 ms. The `>= 0` predicates hide this, so the printed output still matches. [I] The You Do `budget_ms = 50.0` against `after_ms` was tuned on native CPython. Under Pyodide's slower interpreter `budget_pass` may flip to `False`; the output is not pinned, but she may think she broke something. [G] `tracemalloc` and `cProfile` are assumed available in Pyodide's stdlib build; if either is excluded, `theory[2]`, `iDo.steps[0]` and `weDo.steps[1]` would raise `ModuleNotFoundError` in the browser. Worth one smoke run.

Without `ver`, a v3 retry would match the v2 key and be **skipped** as a duplicate, not reapplied. — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[0]`, `iDo.steps[6].preamble`, `iDo.steps[6].why`, `weDo.steps[19].feedback` — evidence: «`c1:features:v1` identifica el intento» — fix: `s38-performance-extreme.ts`: say «identifica la operación» everywhere. State that `ver` changes when the step's logic changes, and that without `ver` the new logic is silently skipped. Add a We Do that shows `apply_once(..., "v2")` after `"v3"` logic ships.
