**Confusions**
- [medium] [I] The section asks for a pool it never shows. `youDo.objectives[0]` asks for «ThreadPoolExecutor o ProcessPoolExecutor local», and theory[2] says «el You Do pide ensayar el executor en tu entorno local». No snippet in S01–S38 constructs an executor: whole-word grep for `Executor`, `concurren` and `multiprocessing` in S01–S37 finds 0 hits, and S38's own code only mentions them in comments. On her Windows laptop, `ProcessPoolExecutor` needs an `if __name__ == "__main__":` guard and a top-level picklable function. theory[3] only hints at this («callable importable y argumentos picklables»). — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[2]`, `theory[3].paragraphs[2]`, `youDo.objectives[0]` — evidence: «aquí medimos bytes y preferimos compacto sin lanzar procesos en el playground» — fix: `s38-performance-extreme.ts`: add one local-python I Do with `ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=4).map(fake_fetch, ids)` and one `ProcessPoolExecutor` with the `__main__` guard and a Windows note. (Q2: the action she must take is not on the page.)
- [medium] [O] "Medir primero" is the section's first rule, yet every bound decision uses hard-coded numbers (`measure_bound(wall_ms=100, cpu_ms=95)`) and no snippet measures anything. S37 did teach `perf_counter`/`process_time` (grep: 34 and 17 hits in S37), but S38 never connects the two. — where: `theory[2].code`, `iDo.steps[0].code`, `weDo.steps[2]` — evidence: «Mide primero (wall vs. CPU en el path caliente…)» — fix: `s38-performance-extreme.ts`: in iDo.steps[0], time a sleep-based fake I/O and a pure-Python loop with `time.perf_counter()` and `time.process_time()`, then feed the real numbers to `measure_bound`. (Otherwise the "measurement" is a literal.)
- [low] [O] Concurrency vocabulary arrives with no earlier ground. Whole-word grep of S01–S37 finds 0 hits for `GIL`, `IPC`, `Queue`, `async`-as-concept (1 incidental hit in S27) and `thread` as an execution thread (S22's 2 hits are email threads). theory[0] glosses GIL in one sentence, but «IPC», «bytecode», «proceso» (OS sense), «event loop», «TOCTOU» (in a code comment) and «OOM» (S37 only) appear without a Spanish gloss. Some English is left unglossed in feedback: «Silence under hang es el anti-patrón» (weDo.steps[11].feedback), «shippear features», «drill», «flood». — where: `theory[2].heading`, `theory[3].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[4].code`, `weDo.steps[11].feedback` — evidence: «Silence under hang es el anti-patrón que el gate CP-N3-C rechaza.» — fix: `s38-performance-extreme.ts`: add a 5-row mini-glossary to theory[0] (hilo, proceso, GIL, IPC, bucle de eventos) and translate the English phrases. (A beginner reading English slowly loses the thread in the feedback meant to help her.)
- [low] [O] The two "units" hints contradict each other. weDo.steps[9].hint talks about converting ms to s, which is the useful advice, while `hints[0]` says «seconds>0 evita hang infinito». The starter's actual defect is a returned constant, not a unit mismatch, so the first hint sends her looking for a bug that isn't there. — where: `weDo.steps[9].hint` vs `hints[0]` — evidence: «Los dos valores están en unidades distintas…» — fix: `s38-performance-extreme.ts`: make `hint` match the defect («la función ignora `latency_ms` y devuelve siempre ok»). (H9: feedback should point at the real error.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — `measure_bound`, `pick`, `fetch_policy` and `runbook` are drilled almost verbatim in We Do, so filling the `NotImplementedError`s is easy. The untouched starter already prints `retry False` / `side_effects 1`, because `__main__` never calls the unimplemented functions. The parts that make it a real resilient pipeline were never demonstrated: a real executor, a durable checkpoint on disk, a kill-and-resume drill and a refilling rate limit.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the 9 items match theory (GIL→processes, backpressure, idempotency, redaction, timeout, error budget, DLQ, measure-first, resume_from). Every distractor is absurd («Quitar timeouts», «Logs con PII», «Duplicar cobros»), so they are answered by elimination. Nothing checks backoff arithmetic, the bucket count or the key-with-`ver` rule.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`production_python_hardening_practice`, `integrated_production_python_practice`, `progress_phase2_walked`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): no [O] — about 70% chance of passing by always picking A. `production_python_hardening_practice.newbie_friendly_description` promises «OWASP Top 10», «OpenTelemetry tracing, Prometheus metrics», «deploy to a cloud platform (AWS, GCP, or Azure)». Grep of S27–S39 finds 0 hits for OWASP, Prometheus and AWS, and S38 says «No usamos OpenTelemetry real». Its `critical_competencies` (`type_safety_production_hardening`, `reproducibility_determinism`) are not examined. Its `required_sections` S30/S32 resolve through stale slugs ("security-infra", "microservices") to Entity resolution and Feature engineering. Its prerequisite `reliable_async_python_development` (S27/S28/S31 = pytest, data tests, graphs) claims `asyncio`/`pytest-asyncio`, with 0 `asyncio` hits before S38. Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: re-map the sections and rewrite the claims to what S27–S38 teach.
- Value: mixed — the ideas are excellent and rare for an analyst track: measure before choosing, backpressure, timeout + DLQ, redacted logs with a correlation id, SLO with an error budget, and idempotency keys. For Lucía's goal (a first data-analyst job), this is SRE material taught entirely through dict-and-flag simulations. Its core guarantee (resume without duplicating effects) is asserted, never run. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: as shipped, a single stdlib script with in-memory "persistence", no tests, no data, no measured numbers. A reviewer asking "kill it and show me" gets nothing. With a JSON/SQLite checkpoint, a pytest test that runs twice and asserts one side effect, a real `ThreadPoolExecutor` over a fake slow provider and the runbook as `RUNBOOK.md`, it would be a solid portfolio piece.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): low. All lesson snippets are stdlib (`queue`, `json`, dicts) and no printed output depends on pandas 3, numpy 2 or sklearn ≥1.5. `queue.Queue` works single-threaded in Pyodide. The You Do's «ThreadPoolExecutor o ProcessPoolExecutor» cannot run in the browser: Pyodide has no real threads or subprocesses, so `ProcessPoolExecutor` raises. It is labelled `local-python`, but nothing warns her if she tries the playground.

**Blockers**
- [blocker] [O] The S39 You Do and the CP-N3-C gate define different products, so Lucía can finish the level-closing project and still fail its gate. CP-N3-C (`BRIEF.md`, `RUBRIC.json.tests_required`, `catalog.ts`) requires «baseline determinista», «calibración reportada», «umbral por costo → revisión humana», «no fuga de datos» (train/eval separation), «desempeño por subgrupo», «abstención soportada» and the interface `triage.score(case) -> TriageDecision`. Its P0s include «Sin calibración» and «Fuga de datos». The S39 You Do starter's "model" is `score = min(1.0, 0.35 + 0.45 * bool(case["shared_signal"]))`. It has no training data, no baseline, no calibration, no cost-based threshold, no split and no per-subgroup performance, and it exposes `triage(case, *, threshold, …) -> Tuple`. S39's own tagline promises «con baseline, calibración, abstención». Calibration and baselines were taught in S33–S35 (grep `calibra` S34: 99 hits, `baseline` S33: 97), but S39 never asks her to reuse them. — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`triage`), `tagline`, `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-C/BRIEF.md` («Criterios de aceptación», «Interfaz») — evidence: «score = min(1.0, 0.35 + 0.45 * bool(case["shared_signal"]))» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s39-integrator-phase2.ts`: make the You Do train a S33 baseline + simple model on a seeded synthetic set with a time-based split. It should report a reliability table (S34), choose the threshold from declared `cost_fp`/`cost_fn`, report metrics per `subgrupo`, and expose `score(case) -> TriageDecision`. Or rewrite `CP-N3-C/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json,FINAL_INTERFACE.md}` to S39's packet/audit/bundle contract. (The gate must grade what the section built.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do starter writes hard-coded performance numbers into the model card and declares a regression that never runs. `model-card.md` is written with the literals «canal_app false_queue=0.08 override=0.10; canal_web false_queue=0.11 override=0.14» and «precision_at_k≈0.55; median_review_s≈90». No line of code computes them. The manifest sets `"regression_scope": "S27-S39"` as a string. Running the untouched starter (`build_bundle(Path("out/normal"))`) ends with `status = "ready_for_human_acceptance"` and a SHA-256 "bundle digest", which looks like verified evidence. That contradicts theory[9]'s own rule against a «card de una línea» and the jobRelevance promise of «evidencia reproducible». — where: `youDo.starterCode` (`model-card.md` text, `manifest`) — evidence: «"Operational value: precision_at_k≈0.55; median_review_s≈90.\n"» — fix: `s39-integrator-phase2.ts`: compute `precision_at_k`, `override_rate` and per-slice `false_queue` from the run's own `audit.jsonl`/labels and template them into the card. Replace `regression_scope` with the result of `subprocess.run([sys.executable, "-m", "pytest", ...])` over named S27–S38 You Do tests. (This is a tautological check: a model card that states numbers its code never measured is the exact failure a responsible-ML capstone exists to prevent.)
- [high] [O] «Regresión S27–S39», a promotion requirement, is never operationalized. It appears 11 times, but S39 never lists which checks to run, from which sections, or how. S39 has 0 hits for `pytest` and `test_`, although S27 taught pytest (79 hits). weDo.steps[19] treats it as a string equality (`notes["regression_scope"] != "S27-S39"`). S38 says «S39 los integrará en el Case Triage», but S39 has 0 hits for `S38`, `checkpoint`, `idempot` and `DLQ`. — where: `theory[0].callout`, `theory[8].paragraphs[0..2]`, `weDo.steps[19].solutionCode`, `youDo.requirements[3]` — evidence: «Promoción N3 = CP-N3-A/B/C + regresión S27–S39 + CF-3 con revisión externa» — fix: `s39-integrator-phase2.ts`: add a `regression.md` table (section → You Do artifact → command → expected), make the You Do run it, and include S38's idempotent-resume drill. - [medium] [O] The exam is passable by position, and several items go beyond the section. 17 of 24 keys are index 1, and options are unshuffled (`route.ts` l.116). Always picking B gives an expected 70.8% and P(≥6/8) ≈ 0.57 against `PASS_THRESHOLD = 70`. Distractors are jokes («Solo el font del PDF», «Orden alfabético del password», «Solo un GIF»). That prints `throughput_qps: 12500 [WARN]` and `latency_p99_ms: 87 [WARN]` against one shared threshold, with no triage, packet or override. — where: `SectionView.tsx` `demos['integrator-phase2']` — evidence: «status = "OK" if value < 80 else "WARN"» — fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with `priority_bucket` + `decide(..., human="skip")` + `mode(incident, drift)` from theory[4,5,7]. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O] — the starter already runs end to end and writes packets, audit, three cards and a manifest. Every added objective (owner/semver registry, appeal with a second reviewer, risk checklist, incident > drift modes, three demo paths, blameless post-mortem) was drilled as a dict predicate in weDo.steps[0–23], so she can bolt them on. The gate-critical parts (calibration, cost threshold, subgroup performance, leakage-free split, the S27–S39 regression, CF-3) are not scaffolded in S39, and CF-3 is undefined.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the 5 items (label_space, external promotion, packet contents, incident → human_only, breaking → major) match theory, but each has three absurd options («culpable», «Claves de API de producción»). Nothing checks override precedence, appeal, slice fairness, OOD abstention, value metrics or the post-mortem.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase2_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied`): no [O] — the exam has the positional key described above. `integrated_python_ai_capstone_advanced_applied` lists `prerequisite_badges` `applied_mlops_pipeline_delivery` (`required_sections` S29 **and S43**, Level 4) and `applied_deep_learning_practice` («CNNs… transformers… PyTorch or TensorFlow»), so the L3 credential cannot be earned at the end of L3. Its `newbie_friendly_description` describes the L3 capstones as pulling together «async Python, MLOps, security infrastructure, microservices, system design». Those are stale slug names; the real S27–S38 titles are pytest, data tests, SQL, entity resolution, graphs, features, ML, metrics, explainability, clustering, profiling and concurrency. `catalog.ts` assigns `badgeId 'capstone_advanced_applied'`, which has 0 hits in `badge_catalog.json`. Fix in `badge_catalog.json` + `catalog.ts`: drop the S43-dependent prerequisite, rewrite the description from the live titles, and use the real badge id.
- Value: mixed — the responsible-ML framing is genuinely strong and rare: score ≠ verdict, evidence packet over a bare number, override with audit, appeal to a second reviewer, incident > drift > normal, blameless post-mortem, and an «umbral operativo» kept separate from calibration. It is also close to the analyst/risk-ops jobs Lucía is aiming for in Lima. But the level closes on a starter whose "model" is one boolean, whose card cites unmeasured metrics, and whose regression is a string. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: it writes real files (packets, `audit.jsonl`, cards, a manifest with SHA-256), which looks good, but it has no tests, no dataset, no README and no computed metric. A reviewer asking "where does 0.55 come from?" ends the conversation.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): low. Lesson snippets are stdlib dict logic with no pandas, numpy or sklearn reprs. The You Do uses `pathlib`, `shutil.rmtree` and file writes. It is labelled `local-python`; in the browser the files would go to Pyodide's in-memory filesystem and vanish. The S39 playground uses `time.sleep` and `random` (seeded 42), so output is stable but off-topic.

**Blockers**
None found. Every We Do exercise can be solved from S40's own theory and I Do, and all the code is stdlib.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The You Do "evidence" is hand-ticked or invented. The starter's gate is `readiness(evidence)` over booleans, and the instructions tell Lucía to flip one by hand: `evidence["context_map"] = True  # tras revisar disjuntos + traducciones`. The QA scenario has `"observed_ms": None,   # rellena con medición del lab`, but S40 builds no system she could measure, so the only way to fill it is to type a number. That contradicts the section's own rule «no inventes» and the portfolioNote «Marca evidence en True solo con artefactos reales». (This is a tautological check: READY would certify a flag she typed.) — where: `youDo.starterCode` (qa_scenarios, comment block before `status, missing = readiness(evidence)`) — evidence: «# evidence["context_map"] = True  # tras revisar disjuntos + traducciones» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s40-architecture-ddd-decisions.ts`: compute every evidence flag from the artifact, e.g. `context_map` = pairwise `isdisjoint` over all rows plus a translation for every shared business term, as in weDo.steps[12–14]. Give the QA scenario a declared synthetic `observed_ms` fixture with its provenance noted («medición sintética del fixture CASO-LIM-040-1A»), or replace it with a timing she runs locally (`time.perf_counter` around a stub). (The gate should measure the dossier, not her honesty.)
- [high] [O] The You Do asks for things S40 never teaches or drills. requirements[5] wants «un caso normal, uno de breach (`BLOCK_ARCHITECTURE`) y uno incierto (`REVIEW_ADR`)». Those two tokens appear nowhere in theory, I Do or the 24 We Do steps, which use `REDRAW_BOUNDARY`, `INVERT_DEPENDENCY`, `RETURN_ADR_TO_DRAFT` and others, and the starter has no function that could emit them. requirements[2] wants a context map of «intake/ER/relación/triage/reporting/IA», but no block defines a "relación" or "IA" context. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. The QA, context-map, C4 and ADR templates mirror weDo.steps[0], [12], [18] and [21], so she can fill them. The `BLOCK_ARCHITECTURE`/`REVIEW_ADR` automation, the "relación"/"IA" contexts and the real measurement are not taught, and the starter accepts hand-ticked flags.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Items [4]–[7] test ports, C4 container, ADR fields and additive evolution. Items [0]–[3] are generic «evidencia / no PII» templates with absurd distractors («datos personales reales para que parezca auténtico»), and [1] keys on `BLOCK_ARCHITECTURE`, which appears only in the You Do. Nothing tests min-cost trade-offs, the forbidden layer edges, the context map/ACL, or entity versus value object.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`architecture_decision_practice` [S35+S40], `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): partly [O]. Keys are balanced (6 per index), but options are not shuffled and almost every distractor is a joke («Un color de UI obligatorio», «El salary band del equipo», «Prueba colusión entre empresas»), so elimination is enough. `architecture_decision_practice` claims «pruebas ocultas y puertas críticas no compensables» and, in its description, «code review» and service decomposition. S40 has no code-review content, and the exam is 8 recognition MCQs. Its prerequisite `reliable_async_python_development` requires S27/S28/S31, which are actually pytest, data tests and graphs, not async. `progress_phase3_walked` says «all 13 sections… S40 through S52» but lists S40–S51 (12). Fix in `badge_catalog.json` (descriptions and prerequisites) and in `prisma/seed.ts` (scenario distractors, e.g. two plausible ADRs where only one has rollback).
- Value: mixed. ADRs with alternatives and rollback, measurable QA scenarios and «missing ≠ breach» are genuinely useful habits, and an ADR is a document a junior analyst can write in her first job. But DDD tactical patterns and C4 are far from a data-analyst entry role, and 24 near-identical «fix the inverted predicate» drills teach the template more than the judgment. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. The deliverable is one Python file of dicts whose C4 is a list of strings. It has no rendered diagram (Mermaid/C4-PlantUML), no `docs/adr/0001-*.md`, no tests, and an `observed_ms` she had to invent. A reviewer would see a checklist, not a dossier.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All snippets are stdlib (`typing.Protocol`, sets, dicts, PEP 585 generics, all fine on Python 3.12), with no pandas/numpy/sklearn reprs, network or files.

**Blockers**
None found. The You Do is a stdlib lab that the We Do E1 steps fully prepare her for.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] A section titled «APIs con FastAPI» never shows a line of FastAPI. There is no `from fastapi`, `@app.post`, `Depends(`, `async def`, `response_model` or `TestClient` call in any code block of theory, I Do, We Do or the You Do. FastAPI was never taught earlier either (S01–S40 hit is S21's stale id only). theory[0].paragraphs[4] says «Los recursos enlazan el equivalente en FastAPI», which leaves Lucía, a paying learner, to learn the framework from English docs. Yet youDo.requirements[5] asks her to «Documenta el mapeo mental a FastAPI (`@app.post`, `Depends`, OpenAPI)», and the exam grades FastAPI API surface (see next item). — where: `title`, `theory[0].paragraphs[4]`, `youDo.requirements[5]` — evidence: «Trabajas primero con la biblioteca estándar… Los recursos enlazan el equivalente en FastAPI» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: add one theory block (`environment: local-python`) with a ~25-line FastAPI app mirroring `status_for`/`idempotent_create`/`handle`. It should use `POST /v1/jobs` with `status_code=201`, a Pydantic `JobCreate`, `Depends(get_store)`, an automatic 422, and a `TestClient` test. Add an I Do that maps each stdlib function to its FastAPI line. (The title, the outcomes and the badge all promise a framework the learner never sees.)
- [high] [O] The exam can be passed without reading the section, and it tests FastAPI details the section never shows. All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and `route.ts` returns options unshuffled, so always choosing B scores 8/8. Items 6–9, 13, 17 and 19 ask about `Depends()`, `response_model`, «router con prefix=/v1/jobs», `BackgroundTasks`, «Startup/shutdown events» and «deps override». `response_model`, `router`, `BackgroundTasks` and `dependency_overrides` have 0 hits in S41. — where: `S41-llm-finetuning.exam.json` items 0–23 (keys), items 8, 9, 13, 19 — evidence: «response_model en FastAPI controla: … *1 Qué campos se serializan/validan en la respuesta» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'llm-finetuning'`): spread the keys across 0–3 and write items from S41's own cases. Examples: which status `status_for("GET","/v1/jobs")` returns on an empty list (200); the label for the same key with a different body (conflict); which test level catches a seeded 200-on-create (contract); `used=110, limit=100` (429 + `retry_after_s`); and the timeout cascade order. Also shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` and remap `correctIndex`. (A positional key is a tautological test of reading.)
- [medium] [O] theory[2]'s code contradicts its own paragraph. paragraph[1] says 405 must not mask «un path desconocido (**404**)», but `status_for` returns `405` for any path it does not know. `status_for("GET", "/v1/unknown")` falls through every branch to `return 405`. — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[1]` vs `theory[2].code` (same function in `iDo.steps[0]` and `weDo.steps[0]`) — evidence: «return 405  # método no permitido en este path (no confundir con 422 de body)» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: add `KNOWN = ("/v1/jobs", "/v1/health")`, return 404 when the path matches no route and 405 only for a known path with the wrong method, and add the `("GET","/v1/unknown")` → 404 pair to the matrix. (The demo teaches the exact anti-pattern the text forbids.)
- [medium] [O] weDo.steps[13] and [14] mark a correctly offloaded CPU job as a breach. PASS requires `record["work_kind"] == "io" and … record["cpu_offloaded"] and record["durable_job"]`, so a `cpu` job that was offloaded and enqueued returns `MOVE_WORK_OFF_EVENT_LOOP`. That contradicts theory[6] and iDo.steps[4], where `cpu_heavy → background` is the right answer. The step's retrospective has to explain the oddity away: «¿por qué el fixture PASS exige flags True aunque el work_kind sea io?». — where: `weDo.steps[13].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[14].solutionCode` — evidence: «return "PASS" if record["work_kind"] == "io" and record["uses_await"] and record["cpu_offloaded"] and record["durable_job"] else "MOVE_WORK_OFF_EVENT_LOOP"» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: PASS iff `(kind == "io" and uses_await) or (kind in {"cpu","durable"} and offloaded and durable_job)`, and add a "cpu + offloaded" fixture that passes. (The answer key disagrees with the theory it drills.)
- [medium] [O] The uncertainty token in weDo.steps[5] contradicts idempotency itself. When `version` is missing, the solution returns `REPLAY_STORED_RESPONSE`. theory[3] says replay is only for «la misma clave + el mismo body», and «sin version no inventes v1» in the same preamble. Replaying a stored response because evidence is missing is the silent success the section forbids. — where: `weDo.steps[5].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[5].feedback` — evidence: «Faltar version exige `REPLAY_STORED_RESPONSE`» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: rename the missing-branch token to `REVIEW_IDEMPOTENCY_RECORD` (human or escalation route). (The feedback teaches the wrong reflex on the section's central concept.)
- [medium] [O] theory[3] states the correct idempotency identity, and no code ever implements it. The text says the record is indexed by «(cliente o tenant, operación, clave)» and that `if key not in store` followed by a write is not safe under concurrency. Every implementation (theory[3], iDo.steps[1], weDo.steps[3], the You Do `idempo`) keys by `key` alone with exactly that `if key in store` pattern and compares raw dicts, not a «hash canónico». — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[0–1]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`idempo: dict[str, dict]`) — evidence: «El registro se indexa por (cliente o tenant, operación, clave)» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: key the You Do store by `(client_id, "POST /v1/jobs", key)`, add a `canonical_hash(body)` via `hashlib.sha256(json.dumps(body, sort_keys=True))` (both taught: `hashlib` in S08+, `json` in S08), and add a readiness check that another client reusing the key gets `created`, not someone else's job. (A stated rule with no practice is a Biggs misalignment.)
- [low] [O] The We Do template contradicts itself and leaves dead code. All 8 E3 instructions (weDo.steps[2,5,8,11,14,17,20,23]) open with «Salida: debe devolver el PASS del contrato», but their outputs are `CONTINUE …` with no PASS. E1 solutions end with an unused `meets_contract = …` line. In weDo.steps[3] that line, `meets_contract = len(store) == 1 and idempotent_create(store, "k1", {"x": 1}) == "replay"`, evaluates to False and adds a second key to the store the step just asserted had length 1. — where: `weDo.steps[2].instruction`, `weDo.steps[3].solutionCode` — evidence: «idempotent_create(store, "k1", {"x": 1}) == "replay"» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: drop the trailing `meets_contract` lines from E1 solutions and replace the E3 prefix with «Salida: las tres decisiones en orden». (A solution line that contradicts the assert above it confuses anyone reading it to learn.)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] «Event loop», «async», «await», «coroutine» and «CPU-bound» carry a learning outcome, two theory blocks, six We Do steps and three exam items, but are never defined. S38 only says «sin red ni event loop en el playground». S41 shows no `async def` (0 hits in the section). — where: `learningOutcomes[4]`, `theory[6].paragraphs[0]`, `iDo.steps[4].preamble` — evidence: «El contrato HTTP puede ser correcto y aun así **bloquear el event loop**.» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: open theory[6] with a 3-sentence definition (one waiter serving many tables; `await` = "while I wait, serve someone else"; CPU work = the waiter cooking, so nobody else is served). Add a 10-line `asyncio` demo with two `await asyncio.sleep` calls versus one CPU loop, timed with `time.perf_counter`. (Q2: the concept the exercise depends on is not on the page.)
- [medium] [O] OpenAPI and Pydantic are judged but never shown. «OpenAPI» has 0 hits in S01–S40; S41 says «OpenAPI debe **coincidir** con status y shape reales», and weDo.steps[10–11] gate on a boolean `openapi_matches` she has never seen computed. — where: `theory[5].paragraphs[0]`, `weDo.steps[10].instruction` — evidence: «si el código devuelve 422 y el doc dice 400, regenera el contrato» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: show a 10-line OpenAPI fragment for `POST /v1/jobs` (`responses: {201, 422}`) and derive `openapi_matches` by comparing its status keys with `status_for` outputs. (Otherwise the flag is a tautology she types by hand.)
- [low] [O] Ops jargon is left in English: «control plane», «edge», «replay», «keyset», «throttle», «gateway», «seams». «Side effects» and «PII» are glossed in jobRelevance; the rest are not. — where: `iDo.steps[7].retrospective`, `weDo.steps[22].preamble` — evidence: «Throttle real + log limpio cierran el edge del control plane.» — fix: `s41-llm-finetuning.ts`: «limitación de tasa», «borde de la API», «repetición (replay)», «paginación por cursor (keyset)». (H2.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O]. The starter's `readiness()` checks behaviour (create, replay with the same `job_id`, conflict, 422, no `secret`, a single side effect, GET 200), and weDo.steps[3], [9] and [21] drill each piece. This is one of the better-designed You Dos: it starts BLOCKED and cannot be faked with flags. Two gaps: requirements[4] names tokens `REJECT_REQUEST`/`RETRY_OR_ESCALATE` that appear nowhere else, and requirements[5] (FastAPI mapping) is untaught.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. The 8 items match T1-A, T1-B (×2), T2-A, T2-B (×2), T3-A and T4-B, and their explanations agree with theory. Nothing covers T3-B (timeout cascade, Problem Details, `finally`) or T4-A (which test level catches which seeded bug). Distractors are weak («301 a /docs», «responder 500 para forzar reintento»).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`llmops_production_delivery` [S41+S42+S43], `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O]. `llmops_production_delivery` publicly claims «LLMOps production delivery — fine-tuning, graph-RAG, drift monitoring, observability». S41 teaches HTTP contracts and S42 teaches schemas and security: the badge was mapped from the stale slugs `llm-finetuning` and `graph-rag`. The exam is also passable by always choosing B. Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: re-scope this badge to «HTTP service contracts and service security» with S41+S42 (and S43 only if it really teaches LLMOps), and rewrite `public_claim`/`newbie_friendly_description` from the live titles.
- Value: mixed, leaning shortchanged. The HTTP semantics are solid and transferable: 201 versus 200, 404 versus an empty list, 422, idempotency keys with conflict, keyset pagination, 429 with `retry_after_s`, Problem Details, the timeout cascade and the test pyramid. But Lucía paid for «APIs con FastAPI» and finishes without having written or run a FastAPI app, so she cannot honestly put FastAPI on her CV from this section. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one stdlib file with `readiness()` asserts: no server, no `app.py`, no `tests/` with `TestClient`, no exported `openapi.json`, no README. A recruiter searching the repo for "fastapi" finds nothing. The portfolioNote's optional FastAPI rewrite should be the actual deliverable.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for the lesson code (stdlib dicts and functions; outputs are plain dict reprs). The portfolioNote's «reescribe el lab con FastAPI + TestClient» cannot run in the browser: FastAPI/uvicorn/httpx are not in Pyodide 0.26.2 and there is no network. The page should say "run locally" if that block is added.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. The starter already prints READY, so the literal task is trivially "done". The parts that would make it real (svc-scope matrix, secrets and pins, retention, live-derivative purge, threat model) are drilled only as boolean predicates in weDo.steps[9–23], or, for the threat model, not at all.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Only 5 items: [0] T1-A, [1] T2-A, [2] the gate, [3] T1-B, [4] T3-A. Nothing on scopes or service identities (T2-B), secrets/pins/CVE (T3-B), minimization and retention (T4-A) or purge and pseudonymization (T4-B). [4] keys the name-only allowlist that theory[6] calls insufficient. Distractors are absurd («datos personales reales para que parezca auténtico»).
- Exam tests the badge claim (`llmops_production_delivery` [S41+S42+S43], `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O]. `llmops_production_delivery` publicly claims «fine-tuning, graph-RAG, drift monitoring». S42 (stale slug `graph-rag`) teaches schemas, authz, SSRF and privacy. The exam has a positional key and one wrong key. `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery` describes Phase 3 as «LLMOps, multimodal systems, infrastructure-as-code, GPU computing…», which also comes from stale slugs. Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: rewrite claims from the live titles and re-map the LLMOps badge.
- Value: mixed. The content is the most job-relevant of S40–S42 for a data role in Peru: authn versus authz, resource binding, deny-by-default, «missing ≠ breach», minimization by purpose, finite retention and purging derivatives (CSV exports) apply directly to an analyst handling customer data. But the drilled controls are weaker than the theory (name-only SSRF, unresolved path prefix), and the You Do arrives finished. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: poor as shipped. It is instructor code with a hard-coded READY, `purge_ok` over empty dicts, no `tests/`, no `threat_model.md` and no README. With a BLOCKED starter, an `ipaddress`-based SSRF check, a real derivative purge and a threat-model table, it would make a convincing small security repo.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. Stdlib only (`hashlib`, sets, string ops). The `sha256` fingerprints in theory[7] (`ed24bc0bb03d`) and theory[9] (`d6e07b73dc2ab4b4`) are deterministic. No network: the DNS "resolution" is a dict fixture.

**Blockers**
- [blocker] [I] The You Do and the gate need a working Docker install, and the course never shows her how to get one. Neither S43 nor any earlier section shows how to install or run Docker on Windows. Grep of S01–S43 finds 0 hits for `WSL`, `Docker Desktop`, `docker build`, `docker run`, `Podman` and `Codespaces`, and `docker compose` appears only as a resource label. Every lesson snippet is a stdlib simulation: iDo.steps[0].why says «Sin daemon Docker, el modelo stdlib basta», and theory[0] says «Practicas el contrato con la biblioteca estándar». The You Do still requires a real «Dockerfile multi-stage», a real «Compose con API/worker/DB/caché» and «comandos locales reproducibles», and the gate requires that these «pasan en entorno nuevo». Doing that for free on her Windows laptop takes four things the section never names. (1) Docker Desktop, which is free for personal use but needs WSL 2 and hardware virtualization enabled in the BIOS. (2) Around 8 GB of RAM to run four services. (3) Pulling `python:3.12-slim`, Postgres and Redis images. (4) Or a GitHub Codespaces free-tier alternative. Lucía will either stall or hand in Dockerfiles she has never built, which the gate forbids. — where: `youDo.requirements[1,2,6]`, `theory[0].callout.content`, `iDo.steps[0].why` — evidence: «Demostrar el gate: build repetible, usuario no root, límites de recursos y shutdown limpio pasan en entorno nuevo.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s43-llmops.ts`: add a «Prepara tu entorno» block before T1. It should cover Docker Desktop + WSL 2 on Windows with a RAM/BIOS checklist, GitHub Codespaces as a zero-install fallback (free monthly quota), and the four commands `docker build`, `docker run --user`, `docker compose up --wait` and `docker history`. Then add one iDo demo that runs them with expected output. (She cannot produce evidence with a tool she was never given; H10 help/docs.)
- [high] [O] The S43 You Do and the CP-N4-A gate it opens define different products. In `catalog.ts`, BRIEF.md and RUBRIC.json, CP-N4-A is an API service with tests_required «request válido → response 200», «rate limit excedido → 429», «sin auth → 401», «logs redactan tokens» and interface `service.serve(request) -> ApiResponse`. The S43 You Do asks for container files plus a readiness checklist. It mentions no API, auth, rate limit or redaction, and it uses a new fictional case (`CASO-TRU-043`, Trujillo) rather than the Cusco service from S42 (`CASO-CUS-042`). So Lucía can finish the S43 You Do and still fail every CP-N4-A test. — where: `youDo.context`, `youDo.requirements[0]` vs `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-A/RUBRIC.json.tests_required` — evidence: «Usa exclusivamente fixtures sintéticos identificados por `CASO-TRU-043`.» — fix: `s43-llmops.ts`: make the You Do containerize the S41/S42 service (the API she already built), with the 200/401/429 contract tests running inside the container. Or rewrite `CP-N4-A/{BRIEF.md,RUBRIC.json,FINAL_INTERFACE.md}` around S43's container contract. (The gate must grade what the section built: constructive alignment.)

(Reading cost for an ES-first learner.)
- [low] [I] The 24 We Do exercises are one template repeated eight times: «flip an inverted boolean», then «add a MISSING branch», then «substring-match a text». She never writes a Dockerfile line, a Compose healthcheck or a SIGTERM handler, though `signal` is linked in resources. Practice is shallow next to a You Do that needs real files. — where: `weDo.steps[0..23]` — evidence: «TAREA: corrige la condición defectuosa; no cambies los datos del fixture» — fix: `s43-llmops.ts`: replace four E1s with authoring tasks: write the Dockerfile, a Compose `healthcheck:` block, a `signal.signal(SIGTERM, …)` drain loop, and a `docker history` grep. (The practice should match the transfer task.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: no [I] — the starter runs, but a correct submission needs real Dockerfile/Compose/runbook artifacts built and run «en entorno nuevo». The section never teaches Docker installation or any `docker` command, and S43 only ever simulated them with string predicates. CP-N4-A also needs API behaviour that the S43 You Do never mentions.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the 5 items match theory (layer order, breach code, gate, runtime secrets, source-before-lock), but each has 2–3 absurd options («datos personales reales para que parezca auténtico»), and none touches digest-vs-tag, readiness vs liveness, limits > 0 or expand/contract.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`applied_mlops_pipeline_delivery`, `llmops_production_delivery`, `integrated_ml_engineering_practice`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`, `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`): no [O] — the answer key is always index 1. `llmops_production_delivery` (S41, S42, S43) claims «fine-tuning, graph-RAG, drift monitoring». The live S41–S43 are FastAPI, schemas/security and containers, and drift has 0 hits in S41–S43; the badge is built on stale slugs. `applied_mlops_pipeline_delivery` and `integrated_ml_engineering_practice` claim model serving, drift and retraining, none of which is in S43. `progress_phase3_walked` claims «all 13 sections… S40 through S52» but lists S40–S51. Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: rewrite claims from the live titles (containers/reproducibility for S43).
- Value: mixed — the content is genuinely useful and current (layer order, non-root, runtime secrets, readiness ≠ liveness, expand/contract, multi-stage, limits), and a junior data person who can containerize her own API stands out in Lima. But she pays for 9 hours of string predicates about Docker without ever running Docker, and the gate then demands real artifacts. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a repo with a built Dockerfile, a `compose.yaml` with healthchecks, a runbook and a CI-run Trivy report would read very well. As specified, though, the starter prints `BLOCKED` until she flips booleans, and the course gives her no path to real artifacts.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for printed output. All snippets are stdlib (`re`, dicts, sets), with no pandas/numpy reprs. Every iDo step is labelled `environment: local-python`, while theory[0] claims «verificable en local o en el navegador»; that is harmless. The real risk is the You Do, which needs a Docker daemon that neither the browser nor a stock Windows install has.

#### S44 — CI/CD y seguridad de la cadena de suministro

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica CLIP y Whisper (simulado)» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 5 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 2 console/page/network errors (first: `console.error: Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 409 (Conflict)`); no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S44.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S44-multimodal.json`: header, jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] (paragraphs, code, output, callouts), iDo.intro + steps[0–7] (code, output, why, retrospective), weDo.steps[0–23] (titles, instructions, edgeCases, tests; full starter/solution for steps 6, 12, 21, 22, 23), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck[0–4] and resources. Also all 24 items of `S44-multimodal.exam.json`. Also checked: `badge_catalog.json` entries listing S44, `catalog.ts` (S44 feeds CP-N4-B, gated at S47), and `route.ts` (options unshuffled). Checked earlier sections: grep of S01–S43 `.json` (excluding exam files) for `GitHub Actions`, `.github/workflows`, `git commit`, `git push`, `pull request`, `YAML`, `mypy`, `ruff`/`flake8`, `gitleaks`, `syft`/`cyclonedx-py`/`pip-audit`, `cosign`, `SBOM`, `canary`, `staging`, `attestation`, `branch protection`/`rama protegida`. Grep of S44 for `OIDC`, `fork`, `PAT`, `required reviewers`, `conventional`.

**Blockers**
- [high] [I] A solo learner on a free GitHub account cannot produce several things the gate requires, and the section never says so. (a) «aprobación independiente del autor del PR» needs a second person, and GitHub does not let a PR author approve her own PR. (b) Branch protection, rulesets and environment required reviewers are free only on **public** repos; private repos need a paid plan. (c) Artifact attestations (`actions/attest-build-provenance`) are likewise free only on public repos. (d) «demuestra rollback en staging» and a canary «al 10%» need a deployed service with traffic. The course has not given her one, and the You Do forbids «servicios externos obligatorios». GitHub Actions itself is free on public repos, but S01–S43 never showed a real workflow file (0 hits for `.github/workflows` before S44), and S44 only models one as a Python string. — where: `youDo.objectives[1]`, `theory[6].paragraphs[0]`, `youDo.requirements[2–4]`, `youDo.objectives[3]` — evidence: «Demostrar el gate: el pipeline reproduce el artefacto, exige aprobación y demuestra rollback en staging.» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s44-multimodal.ts`: add a «Haz esto gratis» block covering four things. (1) Make the repo public. (2) Use the exact workflow YAML (checkout pinned by SHA, `setup-python`, ruff/mypy/pytest matrix, `upload-artifact`, `cyclonedx-py` or `syft` for the SBOM, `attest-build-provenance`). (3) Use an environment `staging` with herself as reviewer, and state plainly that this is not independent approval (record a peer or mentor review as an alternative). (4) Simulate canary/rollback with two tagged image digests run locally via `docker run`, with timings logged. (She cannot meet a gate whose prerequisites are paywalled or need a team without being told.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The T4-B "auditable failure" predicate is logically wrong, so the exercise teaches a broken rule. The solution's PASS condition requires `record["critical_failure"]` to be True. A clean pipeline with no critical failure therefore returns `STOP_SILENT_FAILURE`, and in E3 a pipeline that just hit a critical failure returns `CONTINUE`. Both contradict theory[9] («el pipeline **bloquea** el lanzamiento»). The correct form is `not critical_failure or (pipeline_blocked and logs_redacted and owner and evidence_retained)`, with an explicit `BLOCK` outcome. — where: `weDo.steps[21].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[22].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[23].solutionCode` — evidence: «return "CONTINUE" if record["critical_failure"] and record["pipeline_blocked"] and …» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: rewrite the T4-B predicate as above and add a green-pipeline fixture to E2. (The gate she copies into her You Do would fail every healthy run.)
- [high] [O] Every one of the 24 exercises labels the **valid** fixture as the adverse one. `edgeCases[1]` reads «fixture adverso: tres gates y matriz exactamente soportada», «fixture adverso: el mismo digest probado y la aprobación independiente» and «fixture adverso: fallo crítico bloquea con logs redactados y dueño». Those are the happy-path descriptions. — where: `weDo.steps[0..23].edgeCases[1]` — evidence: «fixture adverso: SBOM y provenance enlazados al mismo digest» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: replace each with the real adverse case (e.g. «adverso: digest del SBOM ≠ digest del artefacto → REJECT_ATTESTATION»). (Q3: the edge-case list tells her the opposite of the test.)
- [high] [O] S44 accepts toy digests that S43 just taught her to reject. S43 theory[3]/[8] insist a digest is `sha256:` + 64 hex and that `startswith("sha256:")` approves garbage. S44 approves `sha256:def` as publishable (theory[3]), uses `tested.startswith("sha256:")` as the promote check (iDo.steps[4], youDo.starterCode `promote_ok`) and uses `sha256:abc`/`sha256:aaa` as valid fixtures throughout. Meanwhile it validates action SHAs to exactly 40 hex (theory[4]). — where: `theory[3].code`, `iDo.steps[4].code`, `youDo.starterCode` (`promote_ok`, `supply_chain_ok`) — evidence: «ok = success and digest.startswith("sha256:") and retention_days >= 7» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: reuse S43's `DIGEST = re.compile(r"^sha256:[0-9a-f]{64}$")` and make the fixtures 64-hex. (Consistency across sections; the digest is the whole point of the section.)
- [medium] [O] «Independent approval» is promised but never checked. The theory says «La aprobación es independiente del autor del PR», but no exercise or starter has an `author` field. Any truthy `approved_by` passes, including the author approving herself. — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[0]`, `weDo.steps[12].solutionCode`, `youDo.starterCode` `promote_ok` — evidence: «meets_contract = … and bool(record["approved_by"]) and record["tested_digest"] == record["promoted_digest"]» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: add `pr_author` and require `approved_by and approved_by != pr_author`. (The learning outcome «aprobación independiente» is never exercised.)
- [medium] [O] Demos print hard-coded values that pose as evidence. iDo.steps[6] has `print("reviews", 2)` and `print("conventional", True)`. «conventional» is never explained. theory[8] has `print("branch", "main_protected")`. theory[9] `on_critical_fail` always returns `audit=True`. — where: `iDo.steps[6].code`, `theory[8].code`, `theory[9].code` — evidence: «print("conventional", True)» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: derive each value from inputs, and drop or explain «conventional» (Conventional Commits). (In the very section about not trusting unverified claims, a hard-coded True is a bad model.)
- [medium] [O] The exam can be passed by always picking option B. All 24 keys are index 1, and options are not shuffled (`route.ts`). Distractors are jokes («continuous recklessness», «Un meme con el deploy», «Salarios del equipo»). Items 6 and 13 test OIDC, PATs and forks, which S44 never teaches (0 hits for `OIDC` and `PAT`). The section's own sharp rules (40-hex pin vs `@v4`, same digest staging→prod, canary 8% > 5% → rollback, MISSING → human review) are only loosely covered. — where: `S44-multimodal.exam.json[0..23]`, items 6 and 13 — evidence: «Buena práctica de continuous recklessness» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'multimodal'`): spread the keys, write plausible distractors, and draw items from S44 fixtures. Shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (An exam that a positional strategy passes does not measure anything.)
- [low] [O] Small contradictions. selfCheck[4] asks about the digest «Antes de promover a staging», but the theory's promote is staging → production. theory[6] uses `prod` in code and `production` in the contract. The You Do rubric (six weighted criteria, no descriptors) cannot be scored and grades «SLO, observabilidad», which S44 does not teach. — where: `selfCheck.questions[4].question`, `theory[6].code` vs `theory[6].paragraphs[1]`, `youDo.rubric` — evidence: «Antes de promover a staging, el digest del artefacto testeado y el promovido deben…» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: «antes de promover de staging a producción»; use one environment name throughout; add 0–3 descriptors. (H4 consistency.)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] The tools the You Do needs are never introduced. The section names gitleaks (only as `echo gitleaks-stub-ok`), SPDX/CycloneDX, SLSA, cosign and «attestation», but never shows the command that produces an SBOM or a provenance file. Grep of S01–S44 finds 0 hits for `syft`, `cyclonedx-py` and `pip-audit`. Yet the starter expects real `dist/sbom.spdx.json` and `dist/provenance.json`. — where: `youDo.starterCode` `ARTIFACTS`, `theory[4].code`, `theory[5].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «"sbom": "dist/sbom.spdx.json",\n    "provenance": "dist/provenance.json",» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: one iDo step that runs `pip install cyclonedx-bom` + `cyclonedx-py environment -o dist/sbom.json` and `sha256sum dist/*.whl`, with expected output, plus the GHA attestation step. (Q2: the action is not available to her.)
- [medium] [O] There is dense unglossed English and jargon: «provenance», «attestation», «subject digest», «canary/blue-green», «least privilege», «stub», «fail-closed», «happy path», «release-owner», and the gate tokens (`REVOKE_AND_ROTATE`, `INSPECT_WORKFLOW_CONDITION`). The jobRelevance glosses SBOM, provenance and RTO once, but «canary», «blue-green» and «attestation» are never glossed. — where: `theory[5].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[7].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «Una **attestation** firmada permite verificar esa cadena» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: gloss at first use («canary: liberar la versión nueva a una fracción pequeña del tráfico y vigilarla»; «attestation: certificado firmado que dice qué build produjo este archivo»). (H2.)
- [low] [I] As in S43, the 24 exercises are one template repeated (invert a boolean, add a MISSING branch, map to a code). She never writes a YAML workflow, a `permissions:` block or an SBOM check against a real file. The You Do then needs exactly those. — where: `weDo.steps[0..23]` — evidence: «Starter: PASS con pred invertido (DEFECT).» — fix: `s44-multimodal.ts`: turn the E3s into "write the workflow YAML fragment / parse a real SBOM JSON" tasks. (The practice should match the transfer task.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [I] — the starter's predicates are all practised, but the deliverable is real files (workflow, SBOM, provenance, canary/rollback log). Those need tools never shown, a public repo (for free branch protection and attestations), a second person for independent approval, and a deployed staging target that the course does not provide.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — items 0, 1 and 4 match theory (40-hex pin, fail-closed, same digest). Items 2 and 3 are near-giveaways with absurd options («subir tokens al repositorio»), and item 4 mislabels the promotion direction.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`; no competency badge requires S44): no [O] — the answer key is positional. The synthesis badge's description still says the phase covers «multimodal systems, infrastructure-as-code, GPU computing…», which are stale slugs; S44 is CI/CD. `container_platform_engineering_practice` claims «via CI/CD (automated deployment pipelines)» but requires only S45 and S46, not S44. Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: add S44 to that badge's `required_sections` and rewrite the synthesis description from the live titles.
- Value: mixed — supply-chain hygiene (40-hex action pin, `contents: read`, the same digest promoted rather than rebuilt, release notes with rollback) is current and rare in junior portfolios, and the Piura framing is relatable. But a logically inverted failure gate, 24 mislabelled edge cases and toy digests undercut the trust the section preaches. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a public repo with a real `.github/workflows/ci-supply-chain.yml`, a green badge, a downloadable SBOM and an attestation would be strong evidence for a data/platform role. As taught, she has no recipe for any of those files.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for printed outputs; all snippets are stdlib dict, string and set logic, labelled `local-python`. The You Do's real work (GitHub Actions, SBOM generation) cannot run in the browser at all, and the section does not say where it runs.

#### S45 — Cloud, almacenamiento, colas e infraestructura

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica Terraform concepts» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 7 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 2 console/page/network errors (first: `console.error: Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 409 (Conflict)`); no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S45.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S45-iac.json`: header (title, tagline, estimatedHours, jobRelevance), learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] (all paragraphs incl. the Docker/Kubernetes block in theory[6].paragraphs[3–6], code, output, callouts), iDo.intro + steps[0–7] (full code for step 2), weDo.steps[0–23] (titles, instructions, edgeCases, outputs; full starter/solution for steps 3 and 9), youDo (context, objectives, requirements, starterCode, portfolioNote, rubric, retrospective), selfCheck[0–6] and resources. Also all 24 items of `S45-iac.exam.json`. Also checked: `badge_catalog.json` entries listing S45, `catalog.ts` (S45 feeds CP-N4-B, gated at S47), and `route.ts` (options unshuffled). Checked earlier sections: grep of S01–S44 (excluding exam files) for `Kubernetes`/`k8s`, `Redis`, `backoff`, `orquestador`, `red privada`, `staging`, `RTO`, `SSRF`. Grep of S45 for `OIDC`, `pub/sub`, `cold start`, `scale-to-zero`, `workspace`, `SSRF`, `S3`, `exactly-once`, `Terraform`, `lifecycle`.

**Blockers**
None found. [O] Unlike S43 and S44, S45 is fully runnable for free on her Windows laptop or in the browser. theory[0] says «sin cuenta cloud, sin credenciales y sin salida de red real», and the You Do starter is stdlib dicts and sets. The cost of that choice is noted under Value.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] An exam item has a wrong answer key. Item 17 asks «Roles temporales (OIDC) son preferibles a:» with options `["Access keys de larga vida en CI", "MFA de humanos", "Secret managers", "Network policies"]` and `correctIndex: 1`. That marks «MFA de humanos» as correct. The intended answer, which matches its own explanation «Short-lived credentials.», is index 0. A learner who knows the material is marked wrong. — where: `S45-iac.exam.json[17]` — evidence: «"correctIndex": 1, "explanation": "Short-lived credentials."» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'iac'`): set `correctIndex: 0`. Also note that OIDC appears 0 times in S45, so the item tests untaught material: replace it with an S45 case (e.g. `allow("iam:admin", …)` → False). (A wrong key directly costs her exam points.)
- [high] [O] The Docker/Kubernetes block added to theory[6] contradicts S43 and contains a broken Dockerfile instruction. It teaches `FROM python:3.12-slim` (S43 theory[3]: «Un tag no es un pin») and `COPY . /app` before `RUN pip install` (S43 theory[2]: copying source before the lock «invalida el caché en cada commit»). It also gives `CMD ['python', 'main.py']`. With single quotes this is not valid JSON exec form, so Docker falls back to shell form and the container fails to start. The block also re-introduces Docker from zero («En 2013, un pequeño startup…»), two sections after S43 taught it. It sits under «Compute, autoscaling y redes» with no exercise attached, and has a typo («cambiaria»). — where: `theory[6].paragraphs[3–6]` — evidence: «`COPY . /app` copia tu código; `RUN pip install -r requirements.txt` instala dependencias; `CMD ['python', 'main.py']`» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s45-iac.ts`: delete paragraphs[3–4] (Docker is S43's job) and keep a short, accurate Kubernetes paragraph that refers back to S43. If an example stays, use `CMD ["python", "main.py"]`, a digest-pinned base and requirements-before-source. (She would copy a Dockerfile that undoes S43 and does not run.)
- [medium] [O] The workload estimate contradicts itself: `estimatedHours: 9`, but theory[0].callout says «Las ~20 h se reparten en ~6 h de teoría y demos, ~8 h de weDo y ~6 h del youDo», and theory[1].paragraphs[3] says «Alrededor de veinte horas». For a learner budgeting evenings around a job, an 11-hour gap matters. — where: `estimatedHours`, `theory[0].callout.content`, `theory[1].paragraphs[3]` — evidence: «Las ~20 h se reparten en ~6 h de teoría y demos» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: reconcile to one number (the stdlib You Do is small; ~9–10 h looks right) and remove the callout breakdown. (H1 visibility of status: she plans her month from it.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do is almost pre-solved, and its "measured" controls are constants. The solution to `process_once` is the four commented lines immediately below («# object_store[key] = …  # return "ACK"»), so "Completa process_once" means uncommenting. `RESTORE_TESTED = True` and `PORTABLE_EXPORT = True` are literals fed into `gate_budget_ok()`. That is the «booleans mágicos» pattern S43's portfolioNote forbids, and it contradicts the S45 gate «backup y recuperación quedan medidos». — where: `youDo.starterCode` — evidence: «RESTORE_TESTED = True\nPORTABLE_EXPORT = True» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: remove the commented solution and make her write `restore_drill()`. The drill should dump `job_status`/`object_store` to JSON, clear them, reload, time it with `time.perf_counter()` and compare against `rto_minutes`. Derive `RESTORE_TESTED` and `PORTABLE_EXPORT` from that run. (A tautological gate in the capstone-feeding project.)
- [medium] [I] The idempotency pattern is check-then-act, and the demo itself shows the case where that fails. In iDo.steps[2] and the You Do, `if key in effects: return "SKIP_DUP"` runs before `effects[key] = "done"`. The demo's slow job (45 s > visibility timeout 30 s) is exactly when a redelivered copy runs **concurrently** with the original. Both copies pass the check, and the PDF is printed twice. Nothing in S45 mentions an atomic conditional write (insert-if-absent / unique constraint) or a lease. — where: `iDo.steps[2].code`, `youDo.starterCode` `process_once` — evidence: «if key in effects:\n        return "SKIP_DUP"\n    # efecto durable primero» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: add one sentence plus code where dedup is claimed atomically: `INSERT … ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` in SQLite (taught in S29, «SQL avanzado y modelado relacional», 17 hits for `ON CONFLICT`), or `dict.setdefault` under a `threading.Lock`, before the side effect. (Expert reviewers, e.g. Kleppmann's DDIA, which is in this section's resources, flag this exact race.)
- [medium] [O] The exam can be passed by always picking option B, apart from the broken item 17. 23 of 24 keys are index 1, and options are unshuffled (`route.ts`). Distractors are meta-jokes («El correctIndex del banco», «Implica fraude», «Probar parentesco»). Items 8, 14, 16, 17 and 19 test pub/sub, cold starts/scale-to-zero, SSRF, OIDC and IaC workspaces, which S45 never teaches (0 hits each). — where: `S45-iac.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «Autoscale de compute reacciona a: | El humor del equipo / … / El correctIndex del banco» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `'iac'`): spread the keys, write plausible distractors, and draw items from S45 cases (backup every 24 h vs RPO 6 h → data-loss risk; ack before effect → NACK; `m1,m1,m2` → processed {m1,m2}). Shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (A positional strategy passes the exam.)
- [low] [O] The T2-B "dedup" check cannot detect duplication. weDo.steps[9] proves dedup with `set(record["message_ids"]) == record["processed_ids"]` where `processed_ids` is already a set, so a doubly-processed `m1` is indistinguishable from a deduped one. It also hard-codes `len(record["processed_ids"]) == 2` to the fixture. The retrospective claims «Dedup se demuestra con conjuntos». — where: `weDo.steps[9].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[9].retrospective` — evidence: «meets_contract = set(record["message_ids"]) == record["processed_ids"] and len(record["processed_ids"]) == 2» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: model `side_effects: list` and require `len(side_effects) == len(set(message_ids))`. (It teaches that a set comparison is evidence of no double side effect.)
- [low] [O] Small inconsistencies. In theory[7] the worker «solo escribe al bucket del caso» but is granted only `object:get`; the You Do grants `object:put`. weDo.steps[3].hints[1] says «consistencia del status no vacía», while the solution requires `== "read-after-write"`. Several demos print literals as evidence (`print("order", "per_partition")`, `print("network", "private")`), which theory[7].callout itself rejects («Un print de `least_privilege=True` sin denegaciones no es evidencia»). The You Do rubric has weights and no descriptors. — where: `theory[7].code`, `theory[5].code`, `theory[6].code`, `youDo.rubric` — evidence: «allowed = {"object:get", "queue:ack"}» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: grant `object:put` in theory[7], align the hint, and derive or drop the literal prints. (Small, but each one teaches a looser rule than stated.)

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] Many distributed-systems terms appear for the first time unglossed, densely, in English: «visibility timeout», «poison», «backpressure», «lag/backlog», «hot-path», «read-after-write», «monotónica o lineal», «drift», «egress», «allowlist», «control plane», «warm-up» and «FinOps». The jobRelevance glosses job asíncrono, DLQ and estado durable well, and theory[2] glosses «side-effect» in the You Do. «monotónica o lineal» is a graduate-level consistency distinction given in one clause. — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[6].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[4].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «exige una garantía más fuerte, monotónica o lineal, y hay que pedirla aparte» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: gloss at first use («backpressure: dejar de aceptar trabajo nuevo cuando ya no das abasto»; «visibility timeout: tiempo durante el cual la cola esconde un mensaje tomado»), and cut or footnote «monotónica o lineal». (H2: reading load for an ES-first learner.)
- [low] [I] A section titled «Cloud… e infraestructura» never shows a cloud console, CLI or IaC file. Terraform appears only in `terraform_only_topic: False` and a resource link, and «plan/apply» are modelled as Python sets. The jobRelevance is honest («no aprender un vendor»), but the title sets a different expectation. — where: `title`, `theory[8].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «IaC declara el estado deseado (cola, bucket, rol) en código versionado» — fix: `s45-iac.ts`: retitle («Colas, almacenamiento y fiabilidad de jobs (modelo local)»), or add one free hands-on: MinIO + SQLite + `queue` locally, or LocalStack with a 10-line Terraform file. AWS/GCP free tiers need a credit card, which is a real barrier in Peru, so they should not be the default. (H2 match with the real world.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: yes [O] — every piece (ack after effect, SKIP_DUP, DLQ at max attempts, PAUSE_AND_INSPECT for missing bytes, budget in PEN) was drilled in theory[2–9] and weDo, and it runs with stdlib alone. If anything it is too easy: the solution is in comments, and restore/export are hard-coded True.
- Self-check tests what was taught: yes [O] — the 7 items map to T1-A, T2-B, the gate, T3-A, T2-B, T3-B and T4-B, with plausible distractors in items 0, 1, 2 and 6 («descartar el mensaje tras N intentos y registrar el conteo»). That is better than S43/S44. Item 0's explanation mentions PII that none of the options contain.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`container_platform_engineering_practice` [S45, S46], `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O] — the key is positional, item 17 has the wrong key, and five items cover untaught material. `container_platform_engineering_practice` claims she can «deploy software using containers (Docker…) and orchestrate them at scale (Kubernetes…) … via CI/CD … on a cloud platform (AWS, GCP, or Azure), including for GPU workloads». S45 deliberately uses no cloud account, has no Kubernetes exercise and no GPU, and the badge does not require S43 (Docker) or S44 (CI/CD). Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: set `required_sections` to S43, S44 and S45, and rewrite the claim to «containerize a service, gate it in CI, and model queue/storage/IAM/cost contracts locally». Otherwise drop «Kubernetes», «cloud platform» and «GPU».
- Value: mixed — this is the most coherent of S43–S45: one honest question («si el proceso muere justo aquí, ¿qué queda y qué se repite?»), the kitchen-rail analogy for ack, and RPO/backup cadence explained correctly. Idempotency, DLQ and restore drills are directly useful for a data-pipeline job. She is shortchanged on the "cloud" in the title, though: she ends with no AWS/GCP/Azure hands-on to name in a Lima interview, and the time estimate is off by 2×. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: a small, self-contained repo (`process_once` + tests for ACK/SKIP_DUP/DLQ/PAUSE + a README with the PEN budget line) would read cleanly. It needs a real restore drill and pytest tests to avoid looking like a toy.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. All snippets are stdlib dict/set/arithmetic, and none uses pandas/numpy reprs, network or files. Every iDo step is labelled `local-python`, but all would also run in Pyodide.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. `classify_event`, `merge_incremental`, `is_acyclic`, `backfill_ok` and `lineage_facet` are each a near-copy of a theory/iDo function, so she can fill the starter. But the token map contradicts the weDo, «RTO medido» and the schema-drift case have no inputs, and the partition-layout note (objectives[5]) relies on S15 pruning (verified in S15) with no worked example here.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. All 5 items (event vs processing time, QUARANTINE on drift, SLI vs SLO, 100 vs wm 110, cycle) match theory. selfCheck[3]'s correct option is hedged («LATE … si 110 − 100 > 5; ALLOWED_LATE si la gracia alcanza») although the stem gives the numbers, and the distractors are absurd. Nothing covers idempotent merge, backfill overlap or lineage.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`container_platform_engineering_practice`, `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O]. The exam is answerable by always choosing B, has a wrong key on item 2, and tests «tumbling», «sensors» and «error budget», which S46 never teaches. `container_platform_engineering_practice` requires S45+S46 and claims «deploy software using containers (Docker…) and orchestrate them at scale (Kubernetes…) … including for GPU workloads», none of which S46 teaches. `progress_phase3_walked` claims «all 13 sections … S40 through S52» but lists S40–S51. The synthesis capstone description still lists «GPU computing, open-source practice». Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: drop S46 from the container badge, or rename the badge to data orchestration and rewrite its claim; correct the phase-3 range.
- Value: mixed. The concepts are genuinely job-relevant for a data analyst or analytics engineer: event vs processing time, idempotent incremental merge (second run = 0 changes), data contracts with an owner, lineage, and freshness SLI/SLO. theory[0] explains them in good Spanish with concrete Huancayo examples. But 24 exercises with the identical "flip an inverted boolean, then three routes" shape cost a paying learner a lot of hours for little new skill, and none of them touches a CSV, a table or pandas. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one stdlib script printing tokens, with no data file, no tests, and a README asked for only in the retrospective. It would read as a toy unless she adds a seeded events CSV, a re-run test and a short README.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for lesson code (stdlib `collections` only, deterministic dict/list reprs). The playground uses `time.time()`, so its printed timings vary per run; it has no `expectedOutput`, so nothing breaks.

**Blockers**
- [blocker] [O] S47 is CP-N4-B's gate section (`GATE_MAP` S47 → CP-N4-B), but its You Do builds something the gate does not accept. CP-N4-B requires `platform.deploy(model) -> DeployRecord` with «lineage, approval, canary_result, slo, rollback_available, rollback_proven, train_serve_consistent». Its P0s are «Sin rollback demostrado», «Sin aprobación», «Sin consistencia train/serve» and «Sin SLOs», and the BRIEF insists «Debe PROBAR rollback, no solo documentarlo». The S47 You Do starter has five predicates over literal booleans (`rollback_ready("1.2.0", "1.1.0", True, True, {"1.0.0"}, True)`). It has no registry state that changes, no `deploy()`, no `DeployRecord`, no trained model, no measured SLO, and its "rollback" never restores anything. gate.json adds «Orquestación de stages: train → register → serve → monitor», which S47 never builds. The package cannot help her either:
  - `CP-N4-B/demo.py` itself has no approval step, no SLO and no train/serve check, so it fails three of its own four P0s.
  - `demo.py` hard-codes `"rollback_ok": True`.
  - 7 of the 8 tests are `assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check`. Running them prints «8 passed, 0 failed», and `execution.json` records `"tests_pass": true`.
  - `STARTER/cp_n4_b.py` only raises `NotImplementedError`.
  - `data/generate.py` returns «Ana Sintetico»/«Bruno Demo» payment-like rows unrelated to models.
  - `catalog.ts` assigns `badgeId 'capstone_integrated_mastery'`, which has 0 hits in `badge_catalog.json`.
  - There are four different task definitions with incompatible scoring: S47 `youDo.rubric` (6 rows, %), `RUBRIC.json` («Promedio ponderado >= 2.4/3»), `gate.json.scoring_weights_reference` (`pass_threshold` 0.8), and `YOUDO.md` («Agrega SLO… consistencia train/serve… registros de incidentes»).
  — where: `youDo.starterCode`, `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-B/{BRIEF.md,FINAL_INTERFACE.md,demo.py,tests/test_demo.py,execution.json,RUBRIC.json,gate.json,YOUDO.md}`, `catalog.ts` l.99–101 — evidence: «Debe PROBAR rollback, no solo documentarlo.» vs «"rollback": rollback_ready("1.2.0", "1.1.0", True, True, {"1.0.0"}, True),» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s47-*.ts`: make the You Do build a `Registry` class whose `deploy(model)` returns a `DeployRecord` dict (lineage, approval, canary_result, slo, rollback_proven, train_serve_consistent). It should train two tiny seeded models (S33 skills), block deploy without approval, fail a canary on purpose and restore `active_version` to last-known-good, then assert on the state. `_generate_formal_packages.py`: emit one real test per `tests_required`, derive `tests_pass` from an actual run, use an existing badge id, generate one rubric, and generate prerequisites from live titles. (Lucía finishes the gate section and still fails every P0 of the gate.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The exam is passable by position. All 24 items are keyed `1`, and `route.ts` serves options unshuffled, so always choosing B scores 100%. Distractors are jokes («Solo el nombre del café», «El wallpaper del IDE», «El sol sale»), and all explanations are in English («Tracking reduces folklore.»). — where: `S47-opensource.exam.json[0..23]` — evidence: «Experiment tracking registra: … "Solo el nombre del café"» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (block `opensource`): write items from S47's own fixtures. Examples: is delta 0.005/tol 0.01 reproducible; `split=train` 0.90 vs baseline 0.78; `stage=production` without approve; canary 5% with quality_delta −0.2/max 0.05. Rebalance keys, and shuffle options in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts`. (The gate section's exam certifies nothing.)
- [high] [O] The You Do starter is already "finished" and models the flag-flipping that its own note forbids. Run as given, it prints `normal_ok True`, `breach_action STOP_CANARY` and `uncertain_action REVIEW_RETIREMENT`. The "uncertain" case is the literal `uncertain = {"audit_entry": None}` tested against `None`. `signature_ok` is passed as `True`, and feature parity compares the strings `"features_v3" == "features_v3"`. The only TODO is a comment. No run is trained, no metric is measured, and requirement[6] («dependencias fijadas») has nothing to pin. — where: `youDo.starterCode`, `youDo.portfolioNote` — evidence: «# Completa: un segundo breach (p. ej. skew batch/online) y un missing de hooks o approved;» vs «no pases a READY solo flipando flags sin evidencia» — fix: `s47-*.ts`: start from failing stubs plus a seeded synthetic dataset, and have `log_run` actually train and evaluate (sklearn from S33) twice with the same seed. (This is a tautological deliverable: the evidence is typed in, not computed.)
- [medium] [O] The rollback token is used in two opposite senses, and the self-check keys the wrong one. In theory, a canary breach → `STOP_CANARY`, and `ROLLBACK_TO_LAST_GOOD` is the result when the rollback itself is unsafe («incompatibilidad o rollback no probado»), which tells her to perform the very action the check just found unsafe. selfCheck[1] then keys «Si el canary rompe el SLO» → `ROLLBACK_TO_LAST_GOOD`. — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[9].paragraphs[1]`, `selfCheck.questions[1]` — evidence: «Error local: incompatibilidad o rollback no probado → `ROLLBACK_TO_LAST_GOOD`.» and «Si el canary rompe el SLO o el rollback es necesario … emitir ROLLBACK_TO_LAST_GOOD» — fix: `s47-*.ts`: rename the T4-B breach token to `BLOCK_UNSAFE_ROLLBACK` (or `FIX_ROLLBACK_PATH`). Have canary breach → `STOP_CANARY` then `ROLLBACK_TO_LAST_GOOD` as the documented sequence, and re-key selfCheck[1]. (Q3: one token cannot mean both "do it" and "it is unsafe to do".)
- [medium] [O] The S47 playground teaches packaging and CI, not MLOps. `demos['opensource']` («Practica empaquetado y CI») shows `pyproject.toml`, semver and a CI matrix, with no run, registry, canary or rollback. The learner PDF labels the section «47. OSS». — where: `SectionView.tsx` `demos['opensource']`, `PdfReport.tsx` l.101 — evidence: «title: 'Practica empaquetado y CI'» — fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with a registry/canary/rollback mini-lab built from theory[4,8,9]. `PdfReport.tsx`: «47. MLOps». (A stale slug drives the only hands-on sandbox.)
