**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. The starter already runs, and adding "a second breach and a missing case" uses the weDo predicates directly. But what the gate requires is not taught in S47 and is not scaffolded: training a run, a registry with mutable state, `deploy() -> DeployRecord`, a demonstrated rollback and a measured SLO.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Items 4–7 (production without approval, skew/leakage, canary 100% without hooks, thin card) match theory. Items 0–3 are generic templates with absurd distractors («datos personales reales para que parezca auténtico»). selfCheck[1] keys the wrong token for a canary breach.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`ai_governance_code_review_practice`, `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O]. The positional key makes it non-evidence. `ai_governance_code_review_practice` (S47+S48+S49) claims she can «review AI-generated code critically», «contribute to open-source projects … via Pull Requests» and «design data contracts». S47 teaches MLOps gating and nothing about code review or open source. The CP-N4-B credential id in `catalog.ts` does not exist in `badge_catalog.json`. Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: retitle the badge to "MLOps and evidence-grounded AI practice" and rewrite its claim from S47–S49's real titles, or move the code-review/OSS claim to sections that teach it.
- Value: mixed. The ideas are the right ones and are explained in clear Spanish: reproducible runs within a tolerance, honest comparison on the same holdout, an approval separate from the digest, train/serve skew, a canary budget, rollback without deleting evidence. For a junior data role in Lima, "I can tell you why this model may not be promoted" is a real interview asset. But Lucía never trains, registers or rolls back anything; every number is typed into a dict. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: poor. It is a 40-line script of booleans with no data, tests, README or measured metric. CP-N4-B as shipped would show a reviewer green placeholder tests and a hard-coded `"status": "pass"`.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for lesson code (pure stdlib arithmetic and dict comparisons; float outputs are rounded, e.g. `round(0.82 - 0.78, 2)` → 0.04). The playground is stdlib with a fixed `expectedOutput` and is safe, but off-topic.

#### S48 — LLM applications y RAG con evidencia

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica bias y fairness» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 7 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 2 console/page/network errors (first: `console.error: Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 409 (Conflict)`); no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S48.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S48-ai-governance.json` (jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] incl. code/output/callouts/figures, iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23] incl. starter/solution/output/feedback, youDo incl. full starterCode + rubric, selfCheck[0–6], resources) and all 24 items of `S48-ai-governance.exam.json`. Also read: `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` (CP-N4-C and its sub-gates S49/S50/S51), `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/BRIEF.md` plus a grep of that package for API keys, the S48 playground (`SectionView.tsx` `demos['ai-governance']`; I reproduced its seeded output under CPython), `PdfReport.tsx` labels, `src/lib/glossary/terms.ts` (`rag`), `route.ts` option order, and the badges in `badge_catalog.json` listing S48. Checked earlier sections: grep of S01–S47 `.json` (non-exam) for `embedding`, `cosine/coseno`, `dot product/producto punto`, `BM25`, `TF-IDF`, `Recall@`, `MRR/nDCG`, `faithfulness`, `groundedness`, `ACL`, `tombstone`, `rerank`, `chunk`, `allowlist`, `inyecci/prompt injection`, `LLM`, `REQUEST_CLARIFICATION`, `CP-N4-C-RAG`. Searched S48 for `openai|anthropic|api key|requests.|sentence-transformers|faiss|chroma|pip install|import`.

**Blockers**
None found. Paid API keys: none needed [O]. Every S48 snippet is stdlib (`hashlib` is the only import). theory[0] says «las demos usan la biblioteca estándar como almacén conceptual, sin llamar a modelos reales». The You Do forbids «servicios externos obligatorios», and the downstream capstone CP-N4-C is designed «**without** requiring a paid LLM key». The flip side is that S48 never runs an LLM or a real embedding model at all (see Confusions). Its first two resources are OpenAI pages, one of them the paid platform's embeddings guide (`platform.openai.com/docs/guides/embeddings`), which may lead Lucía to think she needs a key. Nothing requires money, a GPU or a network. The You Do gaps below are high, not blocking, because she can satisfy them with a crude scorer of her own.

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The dedup rule in the prose is contradicted by the code printed directly below it. theory[4] explains why hashing only the text is dangerous (a chunk from a document she is not allowed to see can survive dedup) and states that the hash includes provenance. The code, and the iDo copy of it, hash only `text`. The figure caption repeats the warning. — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[4].code`, `iDo.steps[2].code` — evidence: «El hash de dedup incluye la procedencia —`doc_id`, sección y versión—, no solo el contenido.» vs «digest = hashlib.sha256(text.encode("utf-8")).hexdigest()[:12]» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s48-*.ts`: hash `f"{doc_id}|{section}|{version}|{text}"`, and add a two-document fixture with identical text and different ACL showing both chunks survive. (She will copy the code, not the paragraph, into her You Do: the exact permission leak the section warns about.)
- [high] [O] The exam is passable by position, and one key is wrong. All 24 items are keyed `1`, and options are served unshuffled (`route.ts`), so always choosing B scores 100%. Item 8 is mis-keyed: dedup is keyed to reduce «La necesidad de embeddings», while the correct option, index 0, is «Diversidad inútil de near-duplicates en el contexto». Items 5, 12 and 14 test «MRR, nDCG», «BM25» and a learned «reranker … modelo más costoso», none of which S48 teaches (BM25/MRR/nDCG: 0 hits S01–S48). — where: `S48-ai-governance.exam.json` items 8, 5, 12, 14 — evidence: «Deduplicar chunks cercanos reduce:» keyed «La necesidad de embeddings» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (l.~14178, block `ai-governance`): item 8 `correctIndex: 0`. Replace items 5/12/14 with S48 cases (ACL before rank, the empty `evidence_ids` vacuous-truth trap, the 0.6/0.4 hybrid top, abstain on support). Shuffle options in `route.ts`. (A learner who knows the answer loses the point; a guesser gains it.)
- [high] [I] "Ignore injected instructions" is never exercised; it is a boolean the fixture asserts about itself. learningOutcomes[6] promises she will «ignorar inyección en documentos». Every grounding exercise checks `record["injected_instruction_ignored"]`, which the fixture sets to `True`, and no code ever reads a chunk's text, detects «envía secretos», or separates data from instructions. CP-N4-C, which S48 feeds, requires a `wrap_as_data()` injection defence. — where: `learningOutcomes[6]`, `theory[8].code`, `weDo.steps[18..20]` — evidence: «injected_instruction_ignored debe ser True (el corpus hostil no manda).» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: add `wrap_as_data(chunk) -> str` (delimit the text and strip or flag imperative patterns such as «ignora», «envía», «revela»). Compute `injection_ignored` from whether the answer's claims came only from wrapped data, and test with a poisoned chunk. (This is a tautological test: the safety property is assumed, not shown.)
- [medium] [O] The You Do refers to tokens and a gate that do not exist. Requirement[5] asks for an uncertain case `REQUEST_CLARIFICATION`, which has 0 hits anywhere else; the section taught `REQUEST_AUTHORIZED_CONTEXT`, `TUNE_RETRIEVAL_OR_BUDGET` and others. Its breach token `ABSTAIN` is neither of the taught `ABSTAIN_UNCITED`/`ABSTAIN_WITH_REASON`. objectives[1] and selfCheck[2] name a «gate CP-N4-C-RAG». `catalog.ts` has CP-N4-C with sub-gates C.1 (S49), C.2 (S50) and C.3 (S51), and no RAG gate. — where: `youDo.requirements[5]`, `youDo.objectives[1]`, `selfCheck.questions[2]` — evidence: «Automatiza un caso normal, uno de breach (`ABSTAIN`) y uno incierto (`REQUEST_CLARIFICATION`).» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: use the taught tokens and say «contribuye a CP-N4-C.1 (S49)». (Q2: the action she must emit is not on the page.)
- [medium] [O] `support` changes type between blocks. In theory and the iDo it is a score (0.8 answers, 0.2 abstains, threshold `thr=0.5`). In weDo.steps[21–23] it is a boolean (`"support":True`, «support False»). — where: `theory[9].paragraphs[2]`, `iDo.steps[7].code` vs `weDo.steps[21].starterCode` — evidence: «support 0.8 con recall y faithfulness en umbral responde; support 0.2 se abstiene» vs `"support":True` — fix: `s48-*.ts`: keep `support: float` with `support >= thr` throughout. (Q3: her E1 predicate `support >= 0.5` breaks on `True`, or silently works because `True >= 0.5`.)
- [medium] [O] The output schema changes key between blocks. theory[8] validates `{"claim", "evidence_ids"}`, while iDo.steps[6] and weDo.steps[18–20] validate `{"answer", "evidence_ids"}`. The You Do docstring wants `{status, claim?, evidence_ids?}`. theory[9]'s `route` returns lowercase `"answer"`/`"abstain"`, while the callout and the weDo use `ABSTAIN_WITH_REASON`. — where: `theory[8].code`, `iDo.steps[6].code`, `youDo.starterCode` (`answer` docstring) — evidence: «if set(answer.keys()) != {"claim", "evidence_ids"}:» vs «if set(out) != {"answer", "evidence_ids"}:» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: one schema (`answer`, `claims[{text, evidence_ids}]`, `status`) used in all four places. (Schema exactness is the lesson, and the lesson's own schemas disagree.)
- [medium] [O] The S48 playground teaches fairness in credit scoring, not RAG. `demos['ai-governance']` («Practica bias y fairness») simulates a gender-biased credit model and prints a model card. It is deterministic (I reproduced 76.0% / 50.0% / 0.658), but it has nothing on retrieval, ACL, citations or abstention. The learner PDF labels the section «48. Governance». — where: `SectionView.tsx` `demos['ai-governance']`, `PdfReport.tsx` l.102 — evidence: «title: 'Practica bias y fairness'» — fix: `SectionView.tsx`: replace it with a mini RAG made from theory[5–9] (ACL filter → hybrid rank → claims ⊆ cited → abstain). `PdfReport.tsx`: «48. RAG con evidencia». (Stale slug; the only sandbox is off-topic.)
- [low] [O] The rubric is copied from S47 and grades things S48 never teaches. Rows include «Operación: SLO, observabilidad y rollback» (15%) and «Seguridad, privacidad y least privilege»; there are no level descriptors, and no row for citations or abstention, which are the section's core. — where: `youDo.rubric[0..5]` — evidence: «"criterion": "Operación: SLO, observabilidad y rollback"» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: rows for ACL-before-rank (deny path returns 0 chunks), per-claim citation, abstention correctness and Recall@k vs baseline, each with 0–3 descriptors. (Biggs: the assessment does not match the outcomes.)

**Confusions**
- [high] [O] The You Do needs three mechanisms the section never shows how to compute. (1) Requirement[2] asks for a «baseline lexical», and the starter's `retrieve(query: str, …)` takes a raw query string. But every lexical score in S48 is a hand-typed dict (`{"d1": 0.9, "d2": 0.2}`); no code turns a query plus text into a score (BM25/TF-IDF: 0 hits S01–S48), and `CORPUS` has no vectors. (2) `answer()` must return a claim, but there is no generator, and nothing says where the claim text comes from (copy the chunk? template?). (3) Requirement[4] asks for answer evals, but «faithfulness» is never defined beyond the parenthesis «answer eval (faithfulness/groundedness)», and «support» is never defined. Both are typed numbers in every fixture. — where: `youDo.requirements[2,4]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`retrieve`, `answer`), `theory[6].code`, `theory[9].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «Incluye baseline lexical y retrieval híbrido con Recall@k documentado.» / «def retrieve(query: str, roles: set, k: int = 2) -> list:» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: add a theory/iDo block with a 10-line term-overlap scorer (`len(set(q.split()) & set(text.lower().split()))`). Define `support` as the share of claim tokens found in the cited chunk, and `faithfulness` as the share of claims with an allowed citation. State that the "answer" is extractive (the cited sentence). (Q2: the action is not available from what was taught.)
- [medium] [O] The title promises «LLM applications», but no LLM, prompt or real embedding appears. Embeddings are hand-made 2-D vectors (`[0.8, 0.2]`), the «tokens» budget is never related to how text becomes tokens, and «LLM» is unglossed in the title. theory[0] does say «sin llamar a modelos reales», but nothing tells Lucía how to try a free local model if she wants to (resources list Sentence Transformers only as «Embeddings locales didácticos», below two OpenAI links). — where: `title`, `iDo.steps[0].code`, `resources.docs[0..1,6]` — evidence: «docs = {"d1": [0.8, 0.2], "d2": [0.1, 0.9]}» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: title «Aplicaciones con LLM y RAG con evidencia (sin modelo real)». Add an optional, clearly marked local snippet (`sentence-transformers` `all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, free, CPU) and say explicitly «no necesitas clave de pago». Move the OpenAI links below the free ones. (H2/H10: set expectations about money and scope.)
- [low] [O] Two figures carry the wrong text for their block. Under «Embeddings y similitud», theory[2]'s figure caption is about the dedup hash. Under «Límites, versiones y evaluación», theory[3]'s caption is about dedup survivors while its alt describes a user-to-documents permission graph. — where: `theory[2].figure`, `theory[3].figure` — evidence: «Deduplicar por el texto puede dejar como superviviente el fragmento que el lector no tenía derecho a ver.» (under Límites/versiones) — fix: `s48-*.ts`: move both figures to theory[4] (chunking) and theory[5] (ACL), and align caption with alt. (H4.)
- [low] [O] A magic version rule and the repeated wrong instruction header. weDo.steps[6–8] accept a chunk only if `source_version.endswith("-v3")`, so a `-v4` document would fail. The «unique hashes» check compares against a count the fixture itself declares (`unique_hashes: 2`) instead of the chunk count. All 24 instructions open with «Salida: debe devolver el PASS del contrato.» even where the target is a triplet. — where: `weDo.steps[6].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[0..23].instruction` — evidence: «and record["source_version"].endswith("-v3")» — fix: `s48-*.ts`: compare `source_version == requested_version`, check `len(set(hashes)) == len(chunks)`, and generate «Salida» from «Éxito». (Q3: an arbitrary rule she cannot generalise.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. ACL filtering with tombstones, hybrid fusion given scores, Recall@k, claims ⊆ cited, and abstain-by-threshold are all taught and directly reusable. The starter is honest: it raises `NotImplementedError` and starts BLOCKED. But lexical scoring from a query string, producing the answer text, computing support/faithfulness, and real injection handling are never shown, and the uncertain-path token she is asked for does not exist.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Items 3–6 (injection as data, ACL before ranking, unsupported claim → abstain, fusion ≠ recall improvement) match theory well. Items 0–2 are generic templates; item 1 asks «Si ocurre la condición de error de S48» without saying which condition, and item 2 tests the name of a gate that does not exist. Nothing checks chunk provenance in the hash, the vacuous `set() <= allowlist` trap, or the holdout/cost decision of T1-B.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`ai_governance_code_review_practice`, `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O]. The exam has a positional key, a wrong key on item 8, and three untaught items. `ai_governance_code_review_practice` (S47+S48+S49) claims code review of AI-generated code, open-source PRs, data contracts and stakeholder communication. S48 contributes only «AI governance» in the narrow sense of ACL plus abstention, and its exam checks none of the rest. Fix in `badge_catalog.json`: rewrite the claim to "builds a permission-aware RAG pipeline that cites or abstains", with critical competencies matching S48, and move code review/OSS elsewhere.
- Value: mixed, leaning fulfilled on concepts. "Filter permissions before ranking", "similarity orders but doesn't prove", "empty evidence list is vacuous truth", "abstaining is a correct answer" and "recall vs faithfulness are separate gates" are exactly what a Lima employer piloting an internal document assistant needs to hear. The explanations (theory[0], theory[8], theory[9]) are some of the clearest Spanish in the level. But she pays for «LLM applications» and never touches an LLM or a real embedding, and the code under the key dedup rule teaches the opposite of its paragraph. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: possible. The starter is a real skeleton with three named tests, and if she adds a 10–20 document synthetic corpus, a term-overlap baseline, a deny-path test and a README showing one cited answer and one abstention, it would be a credible portfolio piece. As scaffolded, it has no data, no tests and no demo.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none. Lesson code uses only `hashlib` and builtins, outputs are deterministic (`cosine` → `1.0`/`0.0`, rounded hybrid scores), and no numpy/pandas reprs appear. The playground uses `random.seed(42)`, whose output is identical in Pyodide.

#### S49 — Agentes, herramientas y context engineering

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica agentes y tools» (on topic): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `mismatch`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Autocheck: 7 questions, no exam, self-check only (W02); completion lost after «Marcar como completada» (W01). 2 console/page/network errors (first: `console.error: Failed to load resource: the server responded with a status of 409 (Conflict)`); no horizontal scroll at 390 px. Data: `data/walk/S49.json`.

Read: all fields of `/tmp/claude-0/sections/S49-data-contracts.json` (jobRelevance, learningOutcomes[0–7], theory[0–9] incl. code/output/callouts/figures, iDo.intro + steps[0–7], weDo.intro + steps[0–23] incl. starter/solution/output/feedback/hints, youDo incl. full starterCode + rubric, selfCheck[0–6], resources) and all 24 items of `S49-data-contracts.exam.json`. Also read: `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` (CP-N4-C, `SUB_N4C`), `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/{SUBGATES,BRIEF,RUN,YOUDO,WEDO}.md`, `STARTER/README.md`, `harness/STARTER/README.md`, `RUBRIC.json`, the head of `harness/commercial_model_adapter.py` and of `tests/adversarial/test_n4c_harness.py`, `src/components/course/CapstonesPage.tsx` (sub-gate card), `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` (no option shuffling), and the badges in `badge_catalog.json` whose `required_sections` include S49. Checked earlier sections: grep of S01–S48 `.json` for `LLM`, `prompt`, `ADR`, `idempoten`, `least privilege`, `holdout`, `HITL`, `SRP`/`responsabilidad única` (0 hits), `side effect`, `sandbox`, `checkpoint`, `provenance`, `SLO`, `retrieval`, `last-known-good`/`LKG`, `compaction` (0 hits), `evaluator` (0 hits), `ACL`. Grep of S49/S50 + exams for `SERP`, `adapter`/`adaptador`, `fallback`, `wrap_as_data`, `multi-agente`, `harness`, `course-state`, `API key`, `OpenAI`, `fingerprint`.

**Paid API keys: none needed.** [O] The lesson code is stdlib only; theory[0].paragraphs[4] says «sin llamar a modelos reales». The CP-N4-C harness runs keyless: `LOCAL` is the default, `COMMERCIAL_TEST` returns canned replies, and only `COMMERCIAL` raises `MissingApiKey` (`RUN.md` «Notas»). The flip side is a finding below: Lucía never sees a real model choose a step.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] Sub-gate CP-N4-C.1, which `catalog.ts` attaches to S49, is not taught in S49. `SUB_N4C[0]` reads «Runtime multi-agente, adaptadores, RAG, tools, web/SERP, presupuestos y aprobación». Its focus is «adaptadores local+comercial, RAG con citas y control de acceso, … web/SERP con proveniencia». `SUBGATES.md` lists the evidence: `local_model_adapter.py`, `commercial_model_adapter.py`, `provider.py` (retry classification, commercial→local fallback), `orchestrator.py` (`Copilot.run`, loop detection via `step_fingerprint`), `web_adapter.py` (`wrap_as_data()`). The S49 JSON has 0 hits for `SERP`, `adapter`/`adaptador`, `fallback`, `wrap_as_data`, `multi-agente`, `harness`, `course-state` and `fingerprint`. The You Do (`youDo.starterCode`) is a separate single-file mini-lab that does not touch `copilot.run(task)`. She finishes S49 without knowing what the sub-gate expects or where its code is — where: `youDo.context` vs `catalog.ts` `SUB_N4C[0]` — evidence: «Construye un **mini-lab de agente acotado** (stdlib)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s49-data-contracts.ts`: add a theory block and a You Do bridge that points to `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/harness/` and has her read and extend `tools.py`, `budget.py` and `orchestrator.py`, or narrow `SUB_N4C[0]` in `catalog.ts` to what S49 actually teaches (the gate she is held to has to be one the section prepares her for).
- [high] [I] The You Do asks for agent metrics that she cannot produce honestly. No section gives her an agent that runs. `youDo.requirements[1]` asks for «métricas baseline/agent en el ADR». The whole section says «no inventes métricas» (`weDo.steps[0].preamble`) and treats a missing `agent_success` as `RUN_AGENT_BASELINE`. But no snippet, starter or harness module produces an `agent_success` number. The section uses no model, and the harness's local adapter is «rule/template based» (`BRIEF.md`). Lucía has two options: type 0.90 by hand, which breaks the section's own rule, or leave the ADR at `need_evidence` forever — where: `youDo.requirements[1]`, `youDo.starterCode` (`decide_mode(True, 0.96, 0.90, …)`) — evidence: «Incluye decisión workflow versus agente con métricas baseline/agent» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: ship a small synthetic task set and have her measure `baseline_success` and `agent_success` by running the deterministic workflow and the CP-N4-C `LocalModelAdapter` over it. Say plainly that the "agent" here is simulated. (Otherwise the ADR she publishes is built on numbers she made up.)

**Issues**
- [high] [O] The We Do teaches the compaction rule that theory names as wrong. Theory says compaction must keep the *critical* set and is meant to drop the rest. weDo.steps[15–17] require `facts_before <= facts_after`, so every real compaction fails. Their "valid" fixture passes only because it has no noise to drop (`facts_before == facts_after`) — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[1]` vs `weDo.steps[15].preamble`, `weDo.steps[15–17].solutionCode` — evidence: «el punto de compactar es precisamente que `facts_before` **no** se conserve entero» vs «corregir `compaction_ok` (`facts_before ⊆ facts_after`, retención ≤7…)» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: change the predicate to `CRITICAL <= record["facts_after"]` (as in `theory[7].code` and `iDo.steps[5]`), add a noisy fact to `facts_before` in the valid fixture, and reword the hint «`facts_before <= facts_after` en Python significa ⊆» (a learner who masters this exercise has learned the wrong invariant).
- [medium] [O] The SRP rule contradicts itself between I Do and We Do. `iDo.steps[2]` allows `search_docs` (schema `["query"]`). From weDo.steps[6] on, `is_srp_tool` requires `schema_fields == {"case_id"}`, which would disable `search_docs` and any tool that does not take a `case_id`. That is a fixture-specific equality presented as the general rule — where: `weDo.steps[6].preamble`, `weDo.steps[7].hints[1]` — evidence: «PASS solo si is_srp_tool: responsibilities==1, schema {case_id}, sin side_effect y typed_errors True» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: express the rule as «schema mínimo declarado para esa tool» (e.g. `record["schema_fields"] == record["declared_schema"]` or `len(...) <= 2`), consistent with `theory[4].code` (SRP is about one duty, not about one specific field).
- [medium] [O] The "missing field" branches send her to actions that do not match the absent field. They are template output, and Lucía will learn the mapping literally. A missing `typed_errors` maps to `SPLIT_TOOL_CONTRACT` (splitting a tool does not supply an error classification). A missing `replayed_effects` maps to `REQUEST_HUMAN_APPROVAL`. A missing `last_known_good` maps to `REVIEW_COMPACTION_LOSS` — where: `weDo.steps[8].instruction`, `weDo.steps[23].instruction`, `weDo.steps[7].feedback` — evidence: «Missing → SPLIT_TOOL_CONTRACT»; «Faltar typed_errors es MISSING de contrato: … el revisor pide SPLIT» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: route each missing field to an action that fixes that field (e.g. `CLASSIFY_TOOL_ERRORS`, `AUDIT_REPLAY_LOG`), or explain in one sentence why the chosen action is right.
- [medium] [O] The You Do rubric cannot be scored, and one criterion is not taught in S49. It lists six criteria with a percentage each and no level descriptors. «Operación: SLO, observabilidad y rollback» (15%) is not covered anywhere in S49's theory, I Do or We Do. The CP-N4-C `RUBRIC.json` has 0–3 anchors, but they cover different criteria (`correctness`, `robustness`, `maintainability`, `responsible_use`) — where: `youDo.rubric[0–5]` — evidence: «Operación: SLO, observabilidad y rollback» / «15%» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: replace with 0–3 anchors that map to S49's own gates (bounded loop, SRP and idempotent tools, JIT and LKG, budget stop, HITL), or reuse `RUBRIC.json` (the learner and the reviewer need the same scale).
- [medium] [O] The READY path contradicts itself. The `evidence` dict starts with every value at `False`. The only way to reach READY is to set the values to `True` in the file. The portfolio note forbids exactly that and gives no mechanism to «enlazar artefactos» — where: `youDo.portfolioNote`, `youDo.starterCode` (`evidence = {name: False …}`) — evidence: «conviértela en READY enlazando artefactos reales … no cambiando asserts a True a mano» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: make `readiness` check that real paths exist (e.g. `Path("ADR.md").exists()`, a tests directory) rather than read booleans (otherwise the checklist rewards exactly the self-certification it warns against).
- [medium] [O] The capstone files she would follow are wrong or hand her the answer. `CP-N4-C/RUN.md` says to run the adversarial suite from inside the capstone folder, but the file is at repo-root `tests/adversarial/test_n4c_harness.py`; `CP-N4-C/tests/` holds only `test_demo.py` and `test_otel_export.py`. `RUN.md` also lists `RAG.py` and `incidente.py`, while the real files are `rag.py` and `incident.py`. `STARTER/README.md` tells her to complete the TODOs «by following the real implementation kept for reference», so the solution ships next to the exercise — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/RUN.md` «Cómo correr», `STARTER/README.md` step 3 — evidence: «cd course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C … python3 tests/adversarial/test_n4c_harness.py» — fix: `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/RUN.md`: use repo-root paths and the real filenames. In `STARTER/README.md`, move the reference implementation out of the learner's path (a broken first command is an H9 failure, and a shipped answer key makes the gate meaningless).
- [low] [O] Figures sit under the wrong headings. Under «Workflow vs. agente» the figure is about idempotency keys on reads. Under «Routing, planner/worker…» the figure is about compaction — where: `theory[2].figure.caption`, `theory[3].figure.caption` — evidence: «Exigir clave a un get_case_status denegaría todas las lecturas»; «Compactar puede borrar pasos ruidosos» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: move `S49-tool-effects` to theory[5] and `S49-context-budget` to theory[7] (a figure that does not match its text costs her time working out the connection).

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The English density is high even for this level. The jobRelevance glosses agent, tool and context engineering, but the body uses unglossed «side effect», «god tools», «holdout», «tool choice», «replay», «retryable vs. terminal», «breach», «promote», «evaluator–optimizer» and «attention budget». «SRP» is never expanded (0 hits in S01–S48, and only the heading in S49 says «responsabilidad única»). The CP-N4-C BRIEF, YOUDO and WEDO are in English with inline Spanish glosses that break the sentences («a provider-neutral contrato (esto es, un acuerdo explícito …) and a determinista (esto es, …) no-key path») — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[5].paragraphs[0]`, `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/BRIEF.md` — evidence: «divide en tools SRP» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: gloss or translate each term at first use. Translate `BRIEF.md`, `YOUDO.md` and `WEDO.md` into Spanish rather than interleaving glosses (Lucía reads English prose slowly).
- [low] [O] Every We Do instruction begins «Salida: debe devolver el PASS del contrato», including the E2 and E3 exercises. Those print three codes (e.g. `CONTINUE SANDBOX_AND_STOP REQUEST_HUMAN_APPROVAL`) and never print PASS — where: `weDo.steps[1].instruction` … `weDo.steps[23].instruction` — evidence: «S49-T4-B-E3 · Salida: debe devolver el PASS del contrato. 1. Missing → REQUEST_HUMAN_APPROVAL.» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: drop the boilerplate or replace it with the exact expected line.
- [low] [O] The self-check and You Do use codes that the section never emits. `STOP_AGENT` appears only bare in youDo.objectives[4], youDo.requirements[5] and selfCheck[1]; the code the lessons emit is `STOP_AGENT_LOOP`. selfCheck[1] asks about «la condición de error de S49» without saying which condition — where: `selfCheck.questions[1]` — evidence: «Si ocurre la condición de error de S49, ¿qué respuesta preserva…» — fix: `s49-data-contracts.ts`: name the condition and use `STOP_AGENT_LOOP`.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O/I] — every mechanism in the starter (`decide_mode`, `call_tool` with approval and idempotency, `within_budget`, `compact_ok`) was drilled in the We Do, so she can build the stdlib mini-lab. She cannot honestly produce the baseline-vs-agent metrics it requires, the rubric has no anchors, and nothing in S49 prepares her for the CP-N4-C.1 harness (adapters, SERP, fallback, multi-agent) that the catalog attaches to this section.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O] — the seven items match theory topics (ADR, gate, sandbox, budget, compaction). Each has two or three absurd distractors («datos personales reales para que parezca auténtico», «abrir network=open automáticamente»), so she can answer by elimination. selfCheck[1] is vague and uses the unemitted token `STOP_AGENT`.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`ai_governance_code_review_practice`, `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O] — `ai_governance_code_review_practice` is the only competency badge, and its `newbie_friendly_description` promises reviewing AI-generated code, contributing to open source and «design data contracts». That comes from the stale slug `data-contracts`, and S49 teaches none of it. The exam does test S49's eight concepts, and its keys rotate 0→3. But the distractors are giveaways («El color del theme», «La VPC», «Entrenar LoRA», «El usuario parpadea»), so it measures recognition, not design. Exam item 15 option 0 is garbled («Todo el historial sin pérdida nunca garantizada mágicamente»). Fix: `badge_catalog.json`: rewrite that badge's claim for S47–S49 as taught. `prisma/seed.ts` (`data-contracts` block): replace the absurd distractors with plausible misconceptions (e.g. «reintentar la tool de escritura sin clave porque el schema ya validó»).
- Value: mixed — the framing is current and useful: workflow before agent, tools with one duty, idempotency keys, budgets with a stop reason, per-call human approval, and never re-running effects on recovery. It also avoids any paid key, which matters on her budget. But in 9 hours she never sees a model pick a step. The "agent" is a set of dict predicates, and the work is far from the analyst role she is aiming for. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak. It is one script that prints `BLOCKED`, with no tests directory, no data, no ADR template and no README scaffold. A reviewer would see a decision table, not an agent. The CP-N4-C harness would read better, but it ships complete, so a fork shows nothing she wrote.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none found — all snippets are stdlib dicts and sets with `str | None` and `dict[str, dict]` annotations (fine on 3.12). They use no pandas, numpy or sklearn, no network and no file I/O. Every step is labelled `local-python`.

#### S50 — Evals, red teaming y fiabilidad de IA

*Browser walk [O]:* playground «Practica design doc y postmortem» (**off topic** (W03)): ran in Pyodide, verdict `match`; after a one-string edit `match`. I Do: 8 demos, output pre-written (W04). We Do: 24 exercises, 0 editable (W05). Data: `data/walk/S50.json`.

Checked earlier sections: grep of S01–S49 `.json` for `p95`, `\bRTO\b` (glossed S44), `canary`, `prefix cache` (0), `order bias` (0), `LLM-judge`/`juez` (0 in S40–S48), `exfiltra`, `poisoning` (0), `alucina`/`hallucina`, `groundedness`, `slice`, `holdout`, `trayectoria`/`trajectory`, `\bP0\b` (S26 only, undefined), `SLO`, `runbook`, `error budget`, `WAF` (0), `\bIAM\b`, `\bACL\b` (glossed S48), `temperatura`/`temperature` (0). **Paid API keys: none needed.** [O] Every lab is stdlib and every "LLM judge" score is a hard-coded list (`iDo.steps[2]`: `llm = [2, 3, 1, 1]`). The CP-N4-C.2 evidence (`harness/evaluation.py`, the ten adversarial tests) runs on the keyless `LOCAL` path. The resources link tools that need a provider key to run against a real model (OpenAI Evals, Promptfoo, Garak), but only as optional reading. The flip side is the second blocker below.

**Blockers**
- [high] [O] Sub-gate CP-N4-C.2, attached to S50, is not reachable from S50. `SUBGATES.md` defines its evidence as `harness/evaluation.py` (`run_holdout`, `evaluate_trajectory`, `build_red_team_cases`), `harness/rollback.py` and ten adversarial tests in `tests/adversarial/test_n4c_harness.py`. S50 never mentions the harness, `Copilot`, `evaluation.py`, `rollback.py` or that test file. Its You Do is a hand-typed three-row `scorecard()` with no link to any agent run. Following `CP-N4-C/RUN.md` fails on the first try: it says `cd course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C` and then `python3 tests/adversarial/test_n4c_harness.py`, but that file is at repo root; the capstone's own `tests/` holds only `test_demo.py` and `test_otel_export.py` — where: `youDo.context` vs `catalog.ts` `SUB_N4C[1]`, `course-state/capstones/CP-N4-C/RUN.md` — evidence: «Suite de evals, red team y rollback sobre el copiloto sintético de operaciones de Ica (continuación del agente con tools de S49)» — fix: `src/lib/course/sections/s50-tech-leadership.ts`: add a You Do step that runs `harness.evaluation.run_holdout` and the adversarial suite, and feeds their output into `scorecard()`. Fix the path in `RUN.md` (`python3 ../../../tests/adversarial/test_n4c_harness.py`, or run it from repo root). (The section claims to continue the S49 agent, but the evidence the gate counts is never produced here.)
- [high] [I] The You Do requires calibrated LLM graders, but she has no LLM. `youDo.requirements[2]` asks for «graders deterministas/humanos/LLM calibrados (acuerdo o flag de calibración)». No section gives her a way to get LLM-judge scores: there is no model, no key and no local option. Every agreement figure in the section comes from literal lists. To meet the requirement she must invent the LLM column, the same «inventar evidencia» that selfCheck[1] marks as wrong — where: `youDo.requirements[2]`, `iDo.steps[2].code` — evidence: «Incluye graders deterministas/humanos/LLM calibrados» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: either supply a fixed, labelled synthetic judge-output file (say it is simulated), or offer a free local judge path (a small Hugging Face model, already met in S25) as optional. Reword the requirement so a simulated judge is acceptable when it is declared as such (an honest learner should not have to fabricate data to meet a rubric line).

**Issues**
- [high] [O] Theory and the figure say the dataset check is coverage, not sum. Theory's own code, the I Do and all three We Do exercises enforce the sum. Lucía reads that requiring the sum «rompería datasets correctos», then is graded on writing exactly that check — where: `theory[2].paragraphs[1]`, `theory[2].figure.caption` vs `theory[2].code`, `iDo.steps[0]`, `weDo.steps[0].instruction`, `weDo.steps[1–2].solutionCode` — evidence: «la comprobación es de **cobertura**, no de suma … exigir que lo hagan rompería datasets correctos» vs «2. Cambia a `sum(slices.values()) == tasks`.» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: change the predicate to a coverage check (every task id in at least one slice: `set().union(*slices.values()) == set(task_ids)`) with overlapping slices in the fixture. Delete «slices que suman» from `iDo.steps[0].preamble` (the contradiction comes in the first subtopic and makes everything after it suspect).
- [high] [O] The S50 exam answer key is positional. In 23 of 24 items the correct answer is option index 1, and only item 23 uses index 0. `drawOnePerConcept` shuffles question order but not options, so every attempt shows at least 7 of 8 questions with the second option correct. Always choosing the second option scores ≥ 87.5% against a 70% pass mark — where: `S50-tech-leadership.exam.json` items 0–22 `correctIndex: 1` — evidence: item 0 «1 Definición de tarea, dataset y rúbrica de éxito»; item 1 «1 Lo bastante objetiva…»; item 2 «1 Sesgo de “demo happy path”…» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts` (`tech-leadership` block): rotate the correct positions, or shuffle options server-side in `src/app/api/exam/start/route.ts` and remap `correctIndex` in scoring (the exam currently certifies pattern-spotting).
- [medium] [O] The red-team gate requires `severity == "P0"` to PASS. A clean run passes only if it is labelled P0, the most severe class, and a clean record tagged «P1» or «none» would be blocked. That inverts what severity means in theory[6] — where: `weDo.steps[13].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[14].solutionCode` — evidence: `… and record["tool_args_valid"] and record["severity"] == "P0" else "BLOCK_SECURITY_P0"` — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: drop `severity` from the PASS predicate and keep it only as a required field for the trace (or check `severity in {"P0","P1","P2"}`).
- [medium] [O] The corpus gate gets weaker between E1 and E2/E3. E1 and theory require every poisoned chunk removed (`poisoned_removed < poisoned_total` → quarantine). E2 and E3 accept `poisoned_chunks_removed >= 1`, have no total field, and read `tool_permissions` from the record itself. So a document that grants its own permissions passes — where: `theory[7].code` vs `weDo.steps[16].solutionCode`, `weDo.steps[17].solutionCode` — evidence: `record["poisoned_chunks_removed"] >= 1 and record["requested_permission"] in record["tool_permissions"]` — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: add `poisoned_chunks_total`, require equality, and compare against a fixed `SESSION_ALLOWED` as E1 does (otherwise the We Do contradicts the least-privilege lesson it drills).
- [medium] [O] The You Do rubric cannot be scored. It is the same six criterion-and-weight lines as S49, with no anchors. That is ironic in a section whose first outcome is «rúbrica 0–3, anclas observables». The CP-N4-C `RUBRIC.json` has 0–3 levels for different criteria — where: `youDo.rubric[0–5]` — evidence: «Correctitud del contrato y gate» / «25%» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: write 0–3 anchors per criterion, or point to `RUBRIC.json` `criteria.robustness` and `criteria.correctness` (the section should practise what T1-A preaches).
- [low] [O] The missing-field routes do not fit the missing field. A missing `holdout` maps to `CALIBRATE_RUBRIC`, a missing `min_agreement` to `ADJUDICATE_DISAGREEMENT`, and a missing `requested_permission` to `REDUCE_TOOL_PRIVILEGE`. The feedback calls the first «incertidumbre de política», which does not explain why the rubric needs calibrating — where: `weDo.steps[2].instruction`, `weDo.steps[8]`, `weDo.steps[17]` — evidence: «sin `holdout` → `CALIBRATE_RUBRIC` (no CONTINUE)» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: route to `SEAL_HOLDOUT`, `SET_AGREEMENT_THRESHOLD`, and so on, or justify each mapping in one line.

**Confusions**
- [medium] [O] The exam explanations are English fragments, so feedback after a wrong answer does not help her (H9). Item 0: «Task+data+rubric.» Item 1: «Operational rubrics.» Item 10: «True holdout.» Item 22: «Rollback + learn.» Several stems mix English as well («Human grading se usa cuando», «LLM-as-judge requiere»), and item 1 has a typo («Ambígua») — where: `S50-tech-leadership.exam.json` `explanation` fields — evidence: «Task+data+rubric.» — fix: `prisma/seed.ts`: one Spanish sentence per explanation that says why the key is right and why the most tempting distractor is wrong.
- [medium] [O] Terms are used unglossed at first use, and grep found them in no earlier section. «prefix cache» and «hit-rate … con ACL» appear in learningOutcomes[7] and theory[9], with no explanation of what a cache ACL protects. «temperatura» (a model parameter) appears in theory[5] and selfCheck[5]. «WAF» appears in `iDo.steps[4].preamble`. «IAM» appears in `iDo.steps[5].retrospective`. «P0/P1» run through the tagline, theory and You Do but are never defined; S26 used «P0» once without a definition — where: `theory[9].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «hit-rate de prefix cache con ACL» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: gloss each term at first use. Add one line defining P0 (blocks promotion, no compensation) vs P1.
- [low] [O] The judge-agreement theory disagrees with its code. The paragraph says inputs are «scores … en [0,1]» and quotes «acuerdo 0.78». The code beneath counts exact matches of 0–3 integers and prints 0.75. A label also misleads: `weDo.steps[3]` prints `min_dim` but the value shown is `min(scores.values())`, not the threshold — where: `theory[4].paragraphs[1–2]` vs `theory[4].code` — evidence: «Entrada: scores det/humano/LLM en [0,1]» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: align the prose with the code (or show both a 0–3 exact-match rate and a [0,1] score), and print `min_score` vs `min_dim` separately.
- [low] [O] Self-check items use tokens or topics that are not in the section. selfCheck[1] keys `BLOCK_CANDIDATE`, which appears nowhere else in S50; the You Do emits `BLOCK`. The selfCheck[3] explanation talks about ER («ER solo propone correspondencia de entidad»), which has nothing to do with S50. The selfCheck[5] explanation reads as a non-sequitur — where: `selfCheck.questions[1]`, `[3].explanation`, `[5].explanation` — evidence: «Order bias invalida al juez; no se usa solo para bloquear promote sin calibración.» — fix: `s50-tech-leadership.ts`: use `BLOCK`, rewrite the two explanations in S50 terms.

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O/I] — the starter already prints `BLOCKED` with `P0_trajectory`, `P0_injection`, `P0_hallucination` and `P1_latency_slo`, so adding rows is easy. The required uncertain path (`HUMAN_ADJUDICATION`) is not in the starter or the We Do. The LLM-grader requirement cannot be met without inventing data, and the CP-N4-C.2 evidence (harness evals and ten adversarial tests) is never linked.
- Self-check tests what was taught: mostly yes [O] — the ten items cover dataset/rubric, trajectory P0, order bias, abstention, indirect injection, SLO/RTO and the scorecard, and the keys rotate. They are weakened by the absurd distractors («subir la temperatura del modelo de producto»), the untaught `BLOCK_CANDIDATE` and two off-topic explanations.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`, `progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`; no competency badge requires S50): no [O] — the positional key (23 of 24 at index 1) and joke distractors («Un meme», «El color del chart», «Un OOM de GPU») mean a pass shows nothing. The verified credential's `newbie_friendly_description` lists «data contracts, and tech leadership», which are the stale slugs; S50 is evals and red teaming. `progress_phase3_walked` says «S40 through S52» while its `required_sections` stop at S51. Fix: `prisma/seed.ts` and `badge_catalog.json`.
- Value: mixed, leaning fulfilled on concepts — this is the most transferable L4 section for Lucía's analyst goal. Versioned slices, 0–3 anchored rubrics, a sealed holdout, agreement rate, order bias and abstention are statistics she can use in any job and in interviews. The self-contradiction on slice coverage, the positional exam and the hand-typed scorecard undercut it. GitHub-readiness of the You Do: weak as shipped. It is one script with three literal rows, no dataset file, no judge outputs, no tests and no README. It would read well only if she wires it to the CP-N4-C harness evals, which the section never asks for.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none found — stdlib only (`frozenset[str]`, `list[tuple[int, int]]` annotations are fine on 3.12). No pandas, numpy or sklearn reprs, no network, no files. Every step is labelled `local-python`.

All 24 items have `correctIndex: 1`, and `route.ts` passes `options: JSON.parse(q.options)` unshuffled (l.116; only question order is shuffled, l.215–219). Always picking the second option scores 8/8 = 100% against `PASS_THRESHOLD = 70`. Spread the keys across positions, write the explanations in Spanish, and shuffle options in `route.ts`. (The exam for a level-closing gate measures nothing.)
- [high] [O] The cost explanation contradicts its own code. theory[3] says retrieval and tool tokens are not billed at the model's token price, and that multiplying everything by one price is the classic error. Every cost function in the section then does exactly that: `total = prompt_t + retr_t + gen_t; cost_usd = round(total / 1000 * PRICE_PER_1K, 6)`. This holds in theory[3].code, iDo.steps[1] and weDo.steps[5] (`estimate_cost_usd(total_tokens)`), and the feedback praises it («costo = f(tokens, precio)»). — where: `theory[3].paragraphs[0]` vs `theory[3].code`, `iDo.steps[1].code`, `weDo.steps[5].solutionCode` — evidence: «Un dashboard que multiplica todo por un precio por token miente hacia abajo.» — fix: `s51-integrator-final.ts`: price only `prompt_t + gen_t` per token and add `retrieval_cost = n_queries * PRICE_PER_QUERY`. Keep reconciliation of token counts separate from billing. (Q3: she will copy the code, not the paragraph.)
- [high] [O] S51 teaches RTO as "rollback minutes", and S52 calls that exact equation the error. S51 theory[7] says «el rollback vuelve al last-good pinneado dentro del **RTO**», and the gate is `within_rto = rec["rollback_minutes"] <= rec["rto_minutes"]` (theory[7].code, iDo.steps[5], weDo.steps[15–17], You Do `slo_incident_ok`). S45 had already taught the restore-based version (`restore_minutes > rto_minutes`). — where: `theory[7].paragraphs[0]`, `theory[7].code`, `youDo.starterCode` (`slo_incident_ok`) — evidence: «rollback a `copilot-6` en ≤10 min (RTO)» — fix: `s51-integrator-final.ts`: rename the field to `service_restored_min` and gloss RTO as «tiempo hasta que el servicio vuelve a atender», in line with S45/S52. **Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [O]. Every helper in the starter is a copy of a weDo predicate, so filling the dicts to reach READY takes minutes. The three automated routes (normal / `ROLLBACK_AND_CONTAIN` / `CONVENE`/`ASK`) are not scaffolded: `readiness()` returns only READY/BLOCKED. The real gate (CP-N4-C harness, `SYSTEM_CARD.md`, CF-5) is not reachable from the section.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. The five items match theory (PII → quarantine, rollback, redaction, gate wording), but every item has three absurd options («datos personales reales para que parezca auténtico», «Inferir fraude del usuario»). Nothing checks error-budget burn, pinning/`latest`, dual control, the RTO clock, or contrast thresholds.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`progress_phase3_walked`, `progress_journey_completed`, `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery`): no [O]. The exam is passable by always picking B (see above).
  - `progress_phase3_walked` lists `required_sections` S40–S51, but its `public_claim` says «all 13 sections … S40 through S52».
  - The newbie description of `integrated_python_ai_capstone_integrated_mastery` describes CP-N4-A/B/C with stale slug topics («multimodal systems, infrastructure-as-code, GPU computing, open-source practice, AI governance, data contracts, and tech leadership»). The real S44–S50 titles are CI/CD, cloud, data engineering, MLOps, LLM/RAG, agents and evals.
  - `catalog.ts` gives CP-N4-C `badgeId 'evidence_grounded_ai_systems'`, which has 0 hits in `badge_catalog.json`; the closest badge (`evidence_grounded_ai_systems_capstone`) is wired to S52.
  - Fix in `badge_catalog.json` + `catalog.ts`.
- Value: mixed. The ideas are senior-grade and well chosen: the redact-before-export rule, the PII-vs-secret distinction (notify vs rotate), pinning against `latest`, segregation of duties, biased feedback, contain → rollback → blameless post mortem, uncertainty plus resolvable citations, and contestability. But Lucía is aiming at a junior data-analyst job. S51 is AI-platform governance taught as 24 near-identical "fix the inverted boolean" drills on hand-typed dicts, with the cost and RTO lessons wrong in the code.
  - GitHub-readiness of the You Do: poor. It is one script that prints `CASO-MOQ-051 READY` from flags she typed, with no README, data, tests or demo. The CP-N4-C harness it should have pointed to is the strongest code package in L4 (see S52 «L4 capstones»).
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for the lesson snippets. They are stdlib only, with walrus and `dict[str, bool]`, which Python 3.12 handles; the blocks are labelled `local-python`. The playground is stdlib only. The CP-N4-C package is local only: it uses files and `state.json`, and its tests need `pytest`.

The comment that forbids this («No pases a READY solo volteando booleans») offers no other path.

- [medium] [O] The title, tagline and most code identifiers are English: «Enterprise Relationship & Operations Intelligence Platform», «evidence bundle», «claims sourced», «tabletop», «disaster exercise», «defense_notes», and code comments in English (theory[7].code «# recovery_min is time until the service is serving again…»). Most of the terms were taught earlier (`bounded context` S40, `C4` S40, `RPO` S45, `OpenAPI` S41, `contract test` S47). The new ones («tabletop», «disaster exercise», «TTR») are glossed thinly or only inline. «TTR» is never expanded: S27 has 1 hit, and S52 uses it 16 times. — where: `title`, `theory[7].code`, `theory[8].paragraphs[0]` — evidence: «Problema: TTR de revisión 90 min en cola sintética multi-región» — fix: `s52-career-strategy.ts`: use a Spanish title («Plataforma integrada de relaciones y operaciones: capstone final»), expand TTR («tiempo hasta resolver») at first use, and translate code comments. (H2.)
- [low] [O] The artifact count drifts. theory[8].paragraphs[2] lists «(C4, README, ADR, cards, LICENSE, video, defense_notes)», which is 7 names, while everything else says 8. The «demo_video» requirement has no guidance on how to record it on a mid-range Windows laptop (free tool, length, voice). — where: `theory[8].paragraphs[2]`, `youDo.requirements[4]` — evidence: «evidence bundle de 8 artefactos (C4, README, ADR, cards, LICENSE, video, defense_notes)» — fix: `s52-career-strategy.ts`: list system_card and model_card separately, and add one line on recording (e.g. Windows Game Bar or OBS, ≤10 min). (Q2.)

**Verdicts**
- You Do doable from what was taught: partly [I]. Every individual piece was practised across S40–S51, and the weDo drilled every action code. But the You Do asks for an 80-hour integration with no scaffold beyond a checklist, and never names the CP-FINAL starter. Without that she would build six contexts from scratch while the grader expects 12 contracts.
- Self-check tests what was taught: partly [O]. Items 0–2 and 4 test the gate vocabulary, each with three absurd options («Publicar el DNI de stakeholders»). Item 3 tests the rollback-minutes version of RTO that theory[7] rejects.
- Exam tests the badge claim (`evidence_grounded_ai_systems_capstone`, `progress_journey_completed`): no [O]. The exam is answerable by always picking B. `evidence_grounded_ai_systems_capstone` (required_sections `['S52']`) claims eight non-compensatory competencies: `sql_competency`, `leakage_prevention`, `selector_resilience`, `type_safety_production_hardening`, `mlops_fluency`, `business_framing_judgment`, `communication_audience_tuned` and `reproducibility_determinism`. No S52 exam item touches SQL, leakage, selectors or type safety, and its own description admits the curriculum has «gaps (leakage prevention, type safety, SQL performance tuning)». Its name is CP-N4-C's («Evidence-Grounded AI Systems»), while `catalog.ts` gives CP-FINAL `badgeId 'capstone_integrated_mastery'`, which has 0 hits in `badge_catalog.json`. Fix in `badge_catalog.json` (rename and re-scope) + `catalog.ts`.
- Value: shortchanged. The framing is mature and useful: no-go as a decision, propose-not-decide HITL, RTO ≠ rollback in theory[7], claims with a baseline, and personal contribution. But the last section of a paid course ends on 24 inverted-boolean drills, a self-typed READY, an exam passable blind, and a playground that teaches fake CV numbers. It gives no help for the junior data-analyst job she is paying to reach.
  - GitHub-readiness of the You Do: as written, nothing publishable. It is a checklist script, and the 8-artifact bundle is a list of names.
- Browser-runtime risks (Pyodide 0.26.2): none for the lesson snippets; they are stdlib dict logic, labelled `local-python`. The playground is stdlib only and prints the ✓ character. The CP-FINAL package writes `evidence_backups/*.json` (local only), and `tests/test_integration_deep.py` imports `pytest`, which is absent from `.venv-content`.

## 4. Badges

Sources: `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` (31 badges, v1.0.0), `badge-specs.ts`,
`claim_evidence_contracts/*.json`, `src/lib/credential-gates.ts`, the learner pages `/badge-notice`
and `/credential-policy`, and the Dashboard's «Sobre los badges» card. Section titles below are the
**current** ones (from `src/lib/course/index.ts`), not the stale id slugs.

### 4.0 Facts that apply to every badge

- **T1 — The learner is never told what a specific badge asks of her [O].** No page lists the 31
  badges, their criteria or their floors (searched `src/app`, `src/components`; the only consumer of
  the catalog is the server route `src/app/api/credentials/issue`). The per-badge
  `newbie_friendly_description` exists only in the catalog and is **in English**. The
  `/badge-notice` page promises «puedes ver una vista previa de tu elegibilidad para insignias»; no
  component uses the eligibility engine (`src/lib/eligibility/engine.ts`), so that preview does not
  exist. Open Badges 3.0 expects `criteria` the earner can read; here she cannot.
- **T2 — The thresholds she is told are not the ones that count [O].** Dashboard: «Alcanzaste el
  umbral de la rúbrica (70% o más)». Self-check: «Si sacas 70% o más, desbloqueas la siguiente
  sección». Catalog floors: self-check 85, You Do 80, exam 85, integrator 85, critical 100, defense
  100. (W08)
- **T3 — Most of the evidence cannot be produced or recorded on this build [O].**
  - Exams: not reachable for a signed-in Pro learner (W02); where the bank is used, «always B» passes
    in 31 of 52 sections (W09).
  - Self-check: stored only in her browser/progress doc.
  - You Do: completion is the button «Proyecto enviado a mi GitHub», self-declared; nothing is submitted
    or checked.
  - Integrator projects (`BADGE:<id>:integrator`): described only in internal files
    (`industry_alignment/badge_requirements/*.md`), never shown to the learner.
  - Defense: no mechanism anywhere in the app.
  - «Pruebas ocultas» claimed by 15 competency badges: none exist (We Do has no runner, W05).
  - `src/lib/credential-gates.ts` says so itself: «Self-checks, You Do rubrics, the integrator project,
    the defense and prerequisite badges are not [recorded], so a credential is not issued until each of
    them has a verifier here.»
- **T4 — The section mapping predates the section renames [O].** Badges list sections by number,
  and the numbers' topics changed. The catalog was generated 2026-07-28, before the V3 re-sequencing
  that `src/lib/section-id-migrations.ts` records. 19 of the 21 competency badges (rows 6–26) require at least
  one section that does not teach the claimed skill (table below).
- **T5 — Placeholders in the evidence contracts [O] (MUST, §6).** All 31
  `claim_evidence_contracts/*.json` have `specification_hash` of the literal form
  `"sha256:<badge_id>:<version>"` (not a hash), and an empty `market_task_mappings: []`.
  `catalog.ts` points capstones at badge ids that do not exist in the catalog (`capstone_foundations`,
  `capstone_independent`, …).

**Badge verdict:** today no learner can earn a competency badge or a capstone credential, because
the evidence cannot be produced (T3). If it could, 19 of the 21 competency badges would certify skills their required sections do not teach (T4), against exams that a test-wise
learner passes without the skill (W09), with criteria she never sees (T1). The progress markers
(class A) are the only honest ones, and even they require exams their own claims do not mention.

- `src/lib/eligibility/badge_catalog.json` and `claim_evidence_contracts/*.json`: remap
  `required_sections` from current titles, e.g. SQL → S12+S29, selectors → S23, ML evaluation →
  S33–S35, RAG → S48, FastAPI → S41. Mark `applied_deep_learning_practice` and the
  fine-tuning/graph-RAG part of `llmops_production_delivery` as `retired` or `planned` until taught.
  This changes what a credential claims, which AGENTS.md puts under "Ask first", so it is the owner's
  decision.
- Translate `newbie_friendly_description` to Spanish and show it, with the floors, on a badges page
  wired to `EligibilityEngine` (the preview `/badge-notice` already promises).
- Make prerequisite chains respect level order (rows 27–29).
- Replace placeholder `specification_hash` values with real hashes of the spec.

## 5. Value to a paying client

Judged as Lucía: paying monthly from a modest salary, aiming at a first data job in Lima. Each
level verdict draws on its sections' "Value" lines (§3) and the capstone checks below. These are
judgements: **[I]** unless marked.

### 5.1 Per level

| Level | Sections | What she gets that is genuinely valuable | Where she is shortchanged | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **L1 Fundamentos Guiados** | S01–S13 | Rare, job-relevant discipline from day one: reproducible environment and Git hygiene (S01), raw/clean traceability and `Decimal` for soles (S02), None ≠ 0 and tri-state validation (S03), honest denominators (S04), quarantine plus a reconciling manifest (S08), PII-safe logging (S09), «score ≠ veredicto» and abstention (S13). Unicode for Peruvian names (S07) is better than most paid courses. Section-level verdicts: 6 fulfilled, 7 mixed, 0 shortchanged. | Practice outruns teaching (loops, `try/except`, classes and regex used before they are taught: S02–S05, S07, S09). Graded material contradicts itself (S03, S04, S07, S08). S10's You Do arrives already written. S12 is her first SQL but never explains `SELECT/WHERE/JOIN`. The level closes on a templated exam she cannot reach (W02, W09) and a capstone with placeholder tests. | **Mostly fulfilled on content, shortchanged on practice and closure.** For this persona it is the best-value level. |
| **L2 Práctica Aplicada Independiente** | S14–S26 | The analyst core: NumPy, pandas dtypes and coercion reports (S14–S15), cleaning judgement (S16), joins with fan-out/anti-join and as-of leakage (S17), EDA with p-hacking and peeking measured (S18, "better than most paid bootcamps"), honest charts with WCAG (S19), Excel/report/email automation with human approval (S20–S22). | S15 is written for pandas 3, never tells her to install it, and its graded project fails if she follows the lesson. S16 parses Peruvian money 1000× wrong. S19 gives little plotting practice and its playground is a SyntaxError (W12). S23 «Browser RPA con Playwright» never runs Playwright, S24 «OCR» never touches an image, S25 «Endpoints de IA» never calls a model. Answer keys are B throughout, with wrong keys in S18, S19 and S21. | **Mixed.** S14–S19 are the heart of a data-analyst course and mostly deliver; S23–S25 are titled for tools they don't use. |
| **L3 Integración y Evaluación Avanzada** | S27–S39 | Testing judgement (oracle problem, mutants, S27; metamorphic and golden tests, S28), real SQL interview material (windows, anti-joins, plans, S29), entity resolution with measured blocking (S30), metrics/calibration/thresholds under capacity (S34), fairness with n beside every metric (S35). | Mostly flag-and-dict simulations: no fitted model in the "supervised ML" section (S33), no `KMeans`/`PCA`/`IsolationForest` run in S36, no pytest run in the pytest section (S27). You Do checks that cannot fail (S37) or fail correct code (S32). Gates grade different products than their sections build (S30→CP-N3-A, S34→CP-N3-B, S39→CP-N3-C). | **Mixed, leaning shortchanged.** The ideas are interview-grade; the hands-on volume for 118 estimated hours is thin. |
| **L4 Sistemas de Producción Gobernados** | S40–S52 | Sound production thinking: ADRs and bounded contexts (S40), HTTP contracts (S41), SSRF/authz (S42), pinning and supply chain (S43–S44), idempotent queues (S45), evals and red teaming (S50), observability and incident response (S51). Runs without paid API keys (readings S48–S50). | Far from her goal of a first analyst job: SRE/platform material taught by simulation. S41 «APIs con FastAPI» never shows FastAPI. S43/S44 need Docker, a second reviewer and a deployed service that the course never sets up. You Dos are checklists of self-typed `True`s (S47, S51, S52). The S52 playground teaches fabricated CV metrics («salvando S/2M anuales»). | **Shortchanged for this persona** (it targets another job), and mixed on its own terms. |

**Overall [I].** A paying beginner gets a course with an unusually mature *conscience*:
privacy, abstention, «score ≠ culpa», honest denominators and fail-closed are taught everywhere,
better than in most paid alternatives. She is shortchanged on *doing*:
- **Practice is read-only.** She can type and run code in one off-topic playground per section; We Do
  is read-the-solution (W05).
- **Assessment does not measure skill.** Exams can't be reached, and where they exist «always B»
  passes (W02, W09).
- **Projects don't connect.** Capstones grade products the sections never build.
- **Credentials can't be earned.** Badges either can't be earned or would certify the wrong skills
  (§4).
- **Effort is not rewarded.** Finishing a section can silently un-finish it (W01).

### 5.2 Would she feel accomplished by the projects?

**[I] Partly, during L1–L2; less after.** The You Do projects with real files and a reconciling
manifest (S08, S17, S20–S22) produce something she can open and show. Too many others end in:
- a starter that is already the solution (S10, S36, S42),
- a check that passes before she writes a line (S13, S20, S26, S37),
- checks that fail correct code (S15, S32),
- a list of self-typed `True` values (S40, S47, S51, S52).

Each of those takes away the feeling of having *made* it. The capstones are where accomplishment
should peak, and §5.3 shows why it doesn't.

### 5.3 Portfolio check: would the projects read well on GitHub?

Judged as a recruiter or a client in Lima opening the repository. Detailed per-capstone evidence
is in the "Capstone checks from the readings" below; the source is `course-state/capstones/CP-*/`.

| Capstone | Gate | README | Tests | Data | Deploy / demo | Reads well? |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CP-N1-A Admisión de clientes (CLI) | S04 | 3-line STARTER README | 5 of 6 `assert True` placeholders | 3 generic records, same generator as B and C | stdout JSON | **No.** Strong idea (correct denominators). |
| CP-N1-B ETL con cuarentena y manifiesto | S08 | same | 5/6 placeholders | same toy file; no `clients.csv`/`transactions.json` as briefed | stdout JSON | **No.** Strong idea (idempotent manifest). |
| CP-N1-C Tablero de evidencia de familiaridad | S13 | same | 5/6 placeholders | same | reference `demo.py` contradicts S13's formulas and hard-codes `"status": "pass"` | **No.** |
| CP-N2-A Portafolio EDA | S17 | same | 5/6 placeholders | toy | no rendered report | **No.** The brief is exactly what an analyst portfolio needs. |
| CP-N2-B Fábrica de reportes accesible | S21 | same | 5/6 placeholders | toy | no rendered artifact | **No.** |
| CP-N2-C RPA + analista IA con aprobación | S26 | same | 6/7 placeholders | toy | reference sets `approved = True` without a human | **No.** |
| CP-N3-A Resolución de entidades | S30 | same | 7/8 placeholders | 5 hand-typed records | fails its own P0s | **No.** |
| CP-N3-B Grafo de relaciones | S34 | same | 6/7 placeholders | 5 nodes | fails its own P0s | **No.** |
| CP-N3-C Triaje responsable con ML | S39 | same | 8/9 placeholders | toy | "model" scores 1.0 on labels it generated (leakage, its own P0) | **No.** |
| CP-N4-A Servicio Python gobernado | S43 | same | 7/8 placeholders | toy | no Dockerfile, no rate limiting, no migrations | **No.** |
| CP-N4-B Plataforma de datos y ML | S47 | same | 7/8 placeholders | toy | fails 3 of its 4 critical criteria | **No.** |
| CP-N4-C Copiloto multi-agente auditable | S51 | none at top level | **47 real tests**, 21 adversarial pass | synthetic corpus | `RUN.md` names files that don't exist (`RAG.py`, `incidente.py`) | **Yes, after the run instructions are fixed.** |
| CP-FINAL Plataforma integrada | S52 | none at top level | 31 tests, but quality flags are constants (`entry["redacted"] = True`) | synthetic | «Licencia: uso interno de capacitación únicamente» | **No.** A technical reviewer stops trusting it at `triage.py`. |

**Portfolio verdict [I].** As shipped, **1 of 13** capstones would read well on a GitHub profile.
For Lucía's target (a junior data-analyst role in Lima) none shows what a recruiter screens for
first: SQL against a real-shaped dataset, a pandas notebook or script with a chart, and a
dashboard. The briefs, though, are portfolio-grade, especially CP-N1-B, CP-N2-A, CP-N2-B, CP-N3-A
and CP-N3-C. What each one is missing is concrete and the same every time:
1. A Spanish README: problem, how to run, sample output, limits, what she did.
2. Real fixtures that match the brief.
3. One real test per `tests_required`.
4. One rendered artifact: an HTML report, a chart, a case sheet or a confusion table.
5. A licence.
6. For L4, a free deploy path (Codespaces/Docker instructions for Windows, or a static demo).

**Proposed fix:** `course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py`. Generate real tests from
`RUBRIC.json.tests_required` and per-capstone fixtures from each BRIEF's «Datos». Add a README
template and an MIT/CC-BY `LICENSE`. Compute `tests_pass` from a real run. Regenerate BRIEF
prerequisites from current section titles. Then connect each gate section's You Do to its capstone
interface, so that finishing the section *is* the first commit of the capstone.

### Capstone checks from the readings

**L1 capstones**
Judged as Lucía publishing each one to her GitHub profile from what `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-{A,B,C}/` ships (README, tests, data, demo).
- [high] [O] All three test suites are tautological. In each `tests/test_demo.py`, 5 of 6 tests are `assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check`. The sixth only checks that `demo.py` exits 0 and prints `METRICS_JSON:`. Each `execution.json` nonetheless records «"tests_pass": true». CP-N1-C's `demo.py` also contains `assert … or len(review_queue) >= 0` (always true) and hard-codes `"status": "pass"` in its metrics, which is the «flag de aprobado» S13 forbids. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-{A,B,C}/tests/test_demo.py`, `execution.json`, `CP-N1-C/demo.py` — evidence: «assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check» — fix: `course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py`: generate one real assert per `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` entry, set `tests_pass` only from a real run, and remove the tautological assert and the hard-coded status. (A hiring reviewer who opens `tests/` sees placeholders, which is worse than no tests. AGENTS.md calls tautological tests worse than none.)
- [high] [O] CP-N1-C's reference `demo.py` contradicts S13 on every contract. Its ER is `0.7*name-Jaccard + 0.3*phone`, where S13 uses doc+block 1.0/0.5/0.0. Its relationship score is `0.6*geo + 0.4*phone`, where S13 uses 0.5/0.3/0.2. Its thresholds are 0.75/0.35 rather than 0.80/0.40, and it outputs the decision label «auto_same_entity_candidate». The phone feeds both scores, which is the mixing S13 warns against. `a.get("phone_last4") == b.get("phone_last4")` scores two missing phones as shared, which is the `''==''` bug weDo.steps[6] teaches her to fix. The demo has no privacy sheet and no correction mechanism, its own P0s. — where: `course-state/capstones/CP-N1-C/demo.py` — evidence: «rec["decision"] = "auto_same_entity_candidate"» — fix: `CP-N1-C/demo.py`: re-implement it from the S13 You Do solution. (A learner who compares her work with the reference will "fix" correct code toward the wrong one.)
- [medium] [O] The data are placeholders, and the same generator ships with all three capstones. `data/generate.py` is byte-identical across A, B and C except for one comment. It returns three generic records («Ana Sintetico», «Bruno Demo», an empty name), defines an unused `rand`, and is never read by `demo.py`. CP-N1-B's BRIEF promises «clients.csv … y transactions.json», and CP-N1-C's promises «entity_id, signal_type, signal_value, provenance, confidence, timestamp». — where: `CP-N1-{A,B,C}/data/generate.py` — evidence: «{"id":"SYN-003","name":"","email":"bad","amount":50,"status":"failure"}» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py`: emit capstone-specific fixtures that match each BRIEF's «Datos», and have `demo.py` load them. (A data-job portfolio is judged first on its data.)
- [medium] [O] The STARTER is empty and the README is three lines long. Every `STARTER/cp_n1_*.py` is `raise NotImplementedError`, and every `STARTER/README.md` says «Copia este directorio y completa `cp_n1_c.py` según `BRIEF.md`». For CP-N1-C there are now three competing entry points (`STARTER/cp_n1_c.py`, S13's `familiarity_dashboard.py`, and the required `python -m demo_n1_dashboard`), and none of them is `familiarity.review` from `FINAL_INTERFACE.md`. All the BRIEFs list wrong prerequisite sections (CP-N1-A «S03 (estructuras de datos)», CP-N1-B «S06 (NumPy) … S08 (pandas)»). `catalog.ts` assigns `badgeId 'capstone_foundations'`, which does not exist in `badge_catalog.json`. — where: `CP-N1-*/STARTER/*`, `CP-N1-*/BRIEF.md`, `src/lib/capstones/catalog.ts` l.59–70 — evidence: «S05 (OOP), S06 (NumPy), S07 (adquisición de datos), S08 (pandas)» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py` + `catalog.ts`: generate BRIEFs from the live section titles, name a single entry module per capstone matching its interface, use `badgeId: 'integrated_python_ai_capstone_foundations'`, and give each capstone a README template (problem, how to run, sample output, tests, limits, privacy). (Lucía cannot tell which file is "the" deliverable.)
- Verdict: as briefed, none of CP-N1-A/B/C would read well on a GitHub profile. A reviewer would see stdlib scripts that print JSON, identical toy data, placeholder tests, an empty starter and English/Spanish naming drift. The ideas underneath, though, are portfolio-grade and unusually mature for Level 1: A's validation with correct denominators, B's quarantine plus an idempotent manifest hash, and C's ER≠REL with abstention and a privacy sheet. Once they have real tests, real fixtures, a README with run instructions and sample output, and one visible artifact each (a summary table for A, a manifest for B, a Markdown case sheet for C), they would be a credible trio for a junior data role.

Judged as Lucía publishing each one to her GitHub profile from what `course-state/capstones/CP-N3-{A,B,C}/` ships (README, tests, data, demo).
- [high] [O] All three test suites are placeholders that pass. The only real test checks that `demo.py` exits 0 and prints `METRICS_JSON:`. Running C's suite prints «9 passed, 0 failed, 9 total». Each `execution.json` records «"tests_pass": true», and each `demo.py` hard-codes `"status": "pass"`. — where: `CP-N3-{A,B,C}/tests/test_demo.py`, `execution.json`, `demo.py` — evidence: «assert True  # placeholder — replace with real check» — fix: `course-state/capstones/_generate_formal_packages.py`: generate one real assertion per `RUBRIC.json.tests_required` entry, and derive `tests_pass`/`status` from an actual run. (These are tautological tests: a reviewer opening `tests/` sees a green suite that checks nothing.)
- [high] [O] CP-N3-C's reference demo commits its own P0 «Fuga de datos» and lacks «calibración». In `synthesize()` the label is `0.6*urgency + 0.3*volume + 0.1*tier/2 > 0.55`, and `score()` is `0.55*u + 0.35*v + 0.10*(t/2)`: the "model" is nearly the labelling rule. Running it prints `"model_acc_non_abstain": 1.0` against a `baseline_acc` of 0.645. There is no train/eval split, no calibration, no cost-based threshold (fixed 0.62/0.42), and «desempeño por subgrupo» is only the abstain rate per tier. `drift_proxy` is one batch's mean minus 0.5, with no reference window. — where: `CP-N3-C/demo.py` vs `BRIEF.md` «Fallos críticos (P0)» — evidence: «return 0.55 * u + 0.35 * v + 0.10 * (t / 2)» — fix: `CP-N3-C/demo.py`: generate labels with noise and hidden features, split by timestamp, fit a baseline + logistic model, report a reliability table and Brier score, pick the threshold from `cost_fp`/`cost_fn`, and report precision/recall per subgroup. (A 100%-accurate reference model is the leakage lesson in reverse.)
- [high] [O] CP-N3-A and CP-N3-B also fail their own P0s. A: five hard-coded records, no train/dev/test split («Sin separación train/dev/test» is a P0), no accents or shared-household case although the BRIEF lists both, no false-positive analysis (P0), and recall 0.50. B: `shortest_path` is a hop-count BFS with no depth limit. Edges have no direct/inferred flag («Sin distinción enlace directo/inferido» is a P0), there is no authorization filter (P0), and `disconnected_path` is computed and never used. — where: `CP-N3-A/demo.py`, `CP-N3-B/demo.py` vs each `BRIEF.md` — evidence: «disconnected_path, _, _ = shortest_path({"X": [], "Y": []}, "X", "Y")\n    # work on empty-ish: ensure no crash» — fix: `CP-N3-A/demo.py`, `CP-N3-B/demo.py`: re-implement from the S30 and S31–S34 You Do solutions with the BRIEF's fields. (The reference implementations would fail their own gates.)

The BRIEFs promise `record_id, name, email, phone, address, label` (A), an edge list with `source_ref, timestamp, inferred, uncertainty` (B), and `case_id, features, label, subgrupo, timestamp, cost_fp, cost_fn` (C). No `demo.py` reads the generator. — where: `CP-N3-{A,B,C}/data/generate.py` — evidence: «{"id":"SYN-003","name":"","email":"bad","amount":50,"status":"failure"}» — fix: `_generate_formal_packages.py`: emit capstone-specific seeded CSV/JSON fixtures matching each «Datos» section, and load them in `demo.py`. (A data-job portfolio is judged first on its data.)
